\begin{split}\frac{1}{n}\theta_{pr,-}(\varphi^{\prime};n)&=-\frac{1}{(2^{5}\gamma)^{1/2}}\int_{0}^{t}\mathrm{d}s\frac{1}{n}\sum_{y=1}^{n}\mathbb{E}_{n}\left(\mathcal{E}_{y}(s)\right)\sum_{|\ell|\leq n^{\kappa}}c_{\ell}(u_{y})(\pi|\ell|)^{1/2}\widehat{(\varphi^{\prime})_{n,o}}(\ell)+o_{n}(1)\\
&=-\frac{1}{(2^{3}\gamma)^{1/2}}\int_{0}^{t}\mathrm{d}s\frac{1}{n}\sum_{y=1}^{n}\mathbb{E}_{n}\left(\mathcal{E}_{y}(s)\right)\sum_{\ell=1}^{+\infty}c_{\ell}(u_{y})(\pi\ell)^{1/2}\widehat{(\varphi^{\prime})}_{s}(\ell)+o_{n}(1).\end{split}

To obtain the last equality we use (
6.17
) and choose
\kappa\in(0,2/3)
.
Since
\widehat{(\varphi^{\prime})}_{s}(\ell)=-\pi\ell\hat{\varphi}_{c}(\ell)
the formula (
6.19
)
follows, provided we prove that

(8.20)

\lim_{n\to\infty}\theta_{pr,+}(\varphi^{\prime};n)=0.

In order to show (
8.20
) we need the following bound that will be proven
in Section
12.4
. Define

(8.21)

\widehat{\mathfrak{E}}_{n}(t,\ell):=\frac{1}{2n}\sum_{y=1}^{n}\big\langle\!\big\langle\left(\nabla^{*}p_{y}\right)^{2}\big\rangle\!\big\rangle_{t}c_{\ell}(u_{y})

Lemma 8.3
.

For any
t>0
there exists
C>0
such that

(8.22)

\left|\widehat{\mathfrak{E}}_{n}(t,\ell)\right|\leq C\left(1\wedge\frac{n^{1/4}}{\ell}\right).

and

(8.23)

\sum_{\ell=1}^{n}\frac{1}{\ell}\left|\widehat{\mathfrak{E}}_{n}(t,\ell)\right|\leq C.

As for (
8.14
), arguing as in the case of

\theta_{pr,-}(\varphi^{\prime};n)
, cf. (
8.15
), we conclude that

(8.24)

\begin{split}\theta_{pr,+}(\varphi^{\prime};n)&=\frac{\gamma}{2^{2}n^{3/2}}\sum_{|\ell|\leq n^{\kappa}}|\pi\ell|\widehat{(\varphi^{\prime})_{n,o}}(\ell)\sum_{j^{\prime}=-\delta n}^{\delta n}\frac{\left(\pi k_{j^{\prime}}\right)^{2}}{\left(\frac{\pi\ell}{n}\right)^{2}+\gamma^{2}(\pi k_{j^{\prime}})^{4}}\widehat{\mathfrak{E}}_{n}(t,2j^{\prime}+\ell)+o_{n}(1).\end{split}

We split the summation over
j^{\prime}
in the above expression into the case
|j^{\prime}|>n^{1/3}
and

|j^{\prime}|\leq n^{1/3}
and choose
\kappa\in(0,1/3)
.

When
|j^{\prime}|\geq n^{1/3}
we have

\left|\widehat{\mathfrak{E}}_{n}(t,2j^{\prime}+\ell)\right|\leq Cn^{-1/12}
, by (
8.22
),
and using the same calculation as in (
8.15
) the corresponding term
will be also bounded by
Cn^{-1/12}
and it vanishes, as
n\to\infty
.

In the case when
|j^{\prime}|\leq n^{1/3}
, the respective term is estimated by

\displaystyle\frac{C}{n^{3/2}}\sum_{\ell=1}^{n^{\kappa}}(\pi\ell)|\widehat{(\varphi^{\prime})_{o}}(\ell)|\sum_{|j^{\prime}|\leq n^{1/3}}\frac{(\pi k_{j^{\prime}})^{2}}{\Big(\frac{\ell\pi}{n}\Big)^{2}+\gamma^{2}(\pi k_{j^{\prime}})^{4}}\leq\frac{C}{n^{1/2}}\int_{0}^{n^{-2/3}}\frac{\varrho^{2}\mathrm{d}\varrho}{\Big(\frac{\pi}{n}\Big)^{2}+\gamma^{2}\varrho^{4}},

that again tends to
0
, as
n\to+\infty
.
This ends the proof of (
6.19
).∎

9.
Asymptoptics of the boundary terms. Proof
of Proposition
6.2

Recall that
\pi^{(pr)}_{\iota}(\varphi^{\prime};n)
is given by formulas
(
6.8
) and (
5.24
).
Then,

(9.1)

\begin{split}&\sum_{\iota\in I}\frac{1}{n}\pi^{(pr)}_{\iota}=\bar{\pi}^{(pr)}_{p}(n)+\bar{\pi}^{(pr)}_{pr}(n),\quad\mbox{where}\\
&\bar{\pi}^{(pr)}(n):=\frac{\tilde{\gamma}}{n}\sum_{j=0}^{n}\sum_{j^{\prime}=1}^{n}{\cal W}_{j,j^{\prime}}\Theta_{pr}(\lambda_{j},\lambda_{j^{\prime}})B_{j,j^{\prime}}^{(p)}\quad\mbox{and}\\
&\bar{\pi}^{(pr)}_{pr}(n):=\frac{1}{n}(\pi^{(pr)}_{pr}+\pi^{(pr)}_{rp}).\end{split}

Here
I=\{p,pr,rp,r\}
and
B_{j,j^{\prime}}^{(\iota)}
,
\iota\in I

are given by (
5.18
). The term
\bar{\pi}^{(pr)}_{pr}(n)
is negligible, as we shall see in
Section
9.2
below. We deal first with
\bar{\pi}^{(pr)}_{p}(n)

9.1.
Asymptotics of
\bar{\pi}^{(pr)}_{p}(n)

By using equation (
8.2
) we have

(9.2)

\bar{\pi}^{(pr)}_{p}(n)=\bar{\pi}_{p,o}^{(pr)}(n)+\bar{\pi}_{p,e}^{(pr)}(n).

By the same symmetry argument as used in (
8.5
):

(9.3)

\begin{split}\bar{\pi}_{p,e}^{(pr)}(n):=-\tilde{\gamma}\Big(\frac{1}{2n}\Big)^{1/2}\sum_{j,j^{\prime}=1}^{n}\sin\Big(\frac{\pi k_{j}}{2}\Big)\Big[\widehat{(\varphi^{\prime})_{n,e}}(j+j^{\prime})-\widehat{(\varphi^{\prime})_{n,e}}(j-j^{\prime})\Big]\Theta_{pr}(\lambda_{j},\lambda_{j^{\prime}})B_{j,j^{\prime}}^{(p)}\\
=-\tilde{\gamma}\Big(\frac{n}{2}\Big)^{1/2}\sum_{j=1}^{n}\sum_{j^{\prime}=1}^{n}\Big[\widehat{(\varphi^{\prime})_{n,e}}(j+j^{\prime})-\widehat{(\varphi^{\prime})_{n,e}}(j-j^{\prime})\Big]\frac{\sqrt{\lambda_{j}\lambda_{j^{\prime}}}(\lambda_{j}-\lambda_{j^{\prime}})}{\theta(\lambda_{j},\lambda_{j^{\prime}})}B_{j,j^{\prime}}^{(p)}=0.\end{split}

Furthermore, we define
\bar{\pi}_{p,o}^{(pr)}=\bar{\pi}_{p,o}^{(pr,0)}+\bar{\pi}_{p,o}^{(pr,n)}
, where we separate the contributions
coming from the left and right endpoints of the chain, writing

(9.4)

\begin{split}\bar{\pi}_{p,o}^{(pr,z)}(n)&=-\tilde{\gamma}\Big(\frac{1}{2n}\Big)^{1/2}\sum_{j=0}^{n}\sum_{j^{\prime}=1}^{n}\left(1-\frac{\delta_{0,j}}{2}\right)^{1/2}\cos\Big(\frac{\pi k_{j}}{2}\Big)\\
&\times\Big[\widehat{(\varphi^{\prime})_{n,o}}(j-j^{\prime})-\widehat{(\varphi^{\prime})_{n,o}}(j+j^{\prime})\Big]\frac{\sqrt{\lambda_{j^{\prime}}}(\lambda_{j}-\lambda_{j^{\prime}})}{\theta(\lambda_{j},\lambda_{j^{\prime}})}B_{j,j^{\prime}}^{(p,z)}\end{split}

for
z=0,n
.
Here for
v=0,1
and
T_{0}=T_{L}
,
T_{1}=T_{R}
:

(9.5)

\begin{split}&B_{j,j^{\prime}}^{(p)}=B_{j,j^{\prime}}^{(p,0)}+B_{j,j^{\prime}}^{(p,n)},\quad\mbox{where}\\
&B_{j,j^{\prime}}^{(p,vn)}=\sum_{x=0}^{n}\left(\psi_{j}(vn)\psi_{j^{\prime}}(x)+\psi_{j}(x)\psi_{j^{\prime}}(vn)\right)\big\langle\!\big\langle b^{(p)}_{nv,x}\big\rangle\!\big\rangle_{t}\quad\mbox{and}\\
&b^{(p)}_{nv,nv}(s)=T_{v}-\mathbb{E}_{n}p_{nv}^{2}(s),\quad b^{(p)}_{nv,x}(s)=-\mathbb{E}_{n}\big[p_{nv}(s)p_{x}(s)\big],\quad x\not\in\{0,n\}.\end{split}

Consider
\bar{\pi}_{p,o}^{(pr,0)}
. Using the fact that

B_{j,-j^{\prime}}^{(p,0)}=B_{j,j^{\prime}}^{(p,0)}
and the definitions
of
\psi_{j}(x)
we have

(9.6)

\begin{split}&\bar{\pi}_{p,o}^{(pr,0)}(n)=-\frac{2\tilde{\gamma}}{n+1}\Big(\frac{2}{n}\Big)^{1/2}\sum_{j=0}^{n}\sum_{j^{\prime}=-n-1}^{n}\sum_{x=0}^{n}\left(1-\frac{\delta_{0,j}}{2}\right)\sin\Big(\frac{\pi k_{j^{\prime}}}{2}\Big)\cos\Big(\frac{\pi k_{j}}{2}\Big)\frac{\lambda_{j}-\lambda_{j^{\prime}}}{\theta(\lambda_{j},\lambda_{j^{\prime}})}\\
&\times\widehat{(\varphi^{\prime})_{n,o}}(j-j^{\prime})\left[\cos\Big(\frac{\pi k_{j}}{2}\Big)\cos\Big(\frac{\pi j^{\prime}(2x+1)}{2(n+1)}\Big)+\cos\Big(\frac{\pi j(2x+1)}{2(n+1)}\Big)\cos\Big(\frac{\pi k_{j^{\prime}}}{2}\Big)\right]\big\langle\!\big\langle b^{(p)}_{0,x}\big\rangle\!\big\rangle_{t}\\
&=-\tilde{\gamma}\Big(\frac{1}{2n}\Big)^{1/2}\sum_{j,j^{\prime}=-n-1}^{n}\Big(1-\frac{\delta_{0,j}}{2}\Big)^{-1/2}\Big(1-\frac{\delta_{0,j^{\prime}}}{2}\Big)^{-1/2}\psi_{j}(0)\widehat{(\varphi^{\prime})_{o}}(j-j^{\prime})\\
&\times\sin\Big(\frac{\pi(j^{\prime}+j)}{2(n+1)}\Big)\frac{\lambda_{j}-\lambda_{j^{\prime}}}{\theta(\lambda_{j},\lambda_{j^{\prime}})}\big\langle\!\big\langle\tilde{b}_{0,j^{\prime}}^{(p)}\big\rangle\!\big\rangle_{t},\quad\mbox{where}\quad\tilde{b}_{z,j}^{(p)}(t)=\sum_{x=0}^{n}\psi_{j}(x)b^{(p)}_{z,x}(t),\,z=0,n.\end{split}

Thanks to estimate (
4.3
) and the Plancherel identity we conclude that for any
t>0

there exists
C>0
such that

(9.7)

\displaystyle(n+1)^{1/2}\sum_{j=0}^{n}\int_{0}^{t}\Big(\tilde{b}_{0,j}^{(p)}(s)\Big)^{2}\mathrm{d}s=(n+1)^{1/2}\sum_{x=0}^{n}\int_{0}^{t}\Big({b}^{(p)}_{0,x}(s)\Big)^{2}\mathrm{d}s\leq C

for
n=1,2,\ldots
.
Let

\varrho_{j}:=\frac{j\pi}{(n+1)^{1/2}}
and

\tilde{b}_{z,j}^{(p)}(t)=\sum_{x=0}^{n}\psi_{j}(x)b^{(p)}_{z,x}(t),\,z=0,n.

Define sequences of functions

{\cal b}^{(p,v)}_{n}:[0,+\infty)^{2}\to\mathbb{R},\quad v=0,1,\,n=1,2,\ldots,

as follows: for
\varrho>\varrho_{(n+1)^{2/3}}

and
t\geq 0
we let
{\cal b}^{(p,v)}_{n}(t,\varrho)=0
.
For
0\leq j\leq(n+1)^{2/3}
,
\varrho\in\big[\varrho_{j},\varrho_{j+1}\big)
we let

(9.8)

\displaystyle{\cal b}^{(p,v)}_{n}(t,\varrho)=(n+1)^{1/2}\tilde{b}_{nv,j}^{(p)}(t),\quad v=0,1.

Thanks to (
9.7
) for any
t>0
there exists
C>0
such that

(9.9)

\displaystyle\int_{0}^{t}\mathrm{d}s\int_{0}^{+\infty}\big[{\cal b}^{(p,v)}_{n}(s,\varrho)\big]^{2}\mathrm{d}\varrho\leq C,\quad\,v=0,1,\,n=1,2,\ldots.

Invoking the definitions of
\theta(\lambda_{j},\lambda_{j^{\prime}})
, see (
5.22
), and
\Gamma(k_{j},k_{j^{\prime}})
, see (
8.9
), we can further write

\displaystyle\bar{\pi}^{(pr,0)}_{p,o}(n)=-\frac{\tilde{\gamma}}{2^{2}(n+1)n^{1/2}}\sum_{j,j^{\prime}=-n-1}^{n}\int_{0}^{t}{\cal b}^{(p,0)}_{n}(s,\varrho_{j^{\prime}})\mathrm{d}s\Big(1-\frac{\delta_{0,j^{\prime}}}{2}\Big)^{-1/2}\cos\Big(\frac{\pi\varrho_{j}}{2(n+1)^{1/2}}\Big)

\displaystyle\times\sin^{2}\Big(\frac{\pi(\varrho_{j}+\varrho_{j^{\prime}})}{2(n+1)^{1/2}}\Big)\widehat{(\varphi^{\prime})_{o}}(j-j^{\prime})\sin\Big(\frac{\pi(j-j^{\prime})}{2(n+1)}\Big)

\displaystyle\times\Big\{\sin^{2}\Big(\frac{\pi(j-j^{\prime})}{2(n+1)}\Big)\sin^{2}\Big(\frac{\pi(\varrho_{j}+\varrho_{j^{\prime}})}{2(n+1)^{1/2}}\Big)+2^{3}\gamma^{2}\Gamma\Big(\frac{\varrho_{j}}{(n+1)^{1/2}},\frac{\varrho_{j^{\prime}}}{(n+1)^{1/2}}\Big)\Big\}^{-1}

Let
\kappa\in(0,1/2)
. Denoting
\ell=j-j^{\prime}
and using the
approximations

\displaystyle\sin\Big(\frac{\pi(\varrho_{j}+\varrho_{j^{\prime}})}{2(n+1)^{1/2}}\Big)\approx\frac{\pi\varrho_{j^{\prime}}}{(n+1)^{1/2}},\quad\sin\Big(\frac{\pi(j-j^{\prime})}{2(n+1)}\Big)\approx\frac{\pi(j-j^{\prime})}{2(n+1)},

\displaystyle\cos\Big(\frac{\pi\varrho_{j}}{2(n+1)^{1/2}}\Big)\approx 1,

valid for
|j^{\prime}-j|\leq n^{\kappa}
,
we can write that

\displaystyle\bar{\pi}^{(pr,0)}_{p,o}(n)=-\frac{\tilde{\gamma}}{2(n+1)^{1/2}}\sum_{|\ell|\leq n^{\kappa}}\pi\ell\widehat{(\varphi^{\prime})_{o}}(\ell)\sum_{|j^{\prime}|\leq(n+1)^{2/3}}\int_{0}^{t}\frac{{\cal b}^{(p,0)}_{n}(s,\varrho_{j^{\prime}})\mathrm{d}s}{(\pi\ell)^{2}+\gamma^{2}(\pi\varrho_{j^{\prime}})^{4}}+o_{n}(1)

\displaystyle=\frac{\tilde{\gamma}}{2(n+1)^{1/2}}\sum_{|\ell|\leq n^{\kappa}}\hat{\varphi}_{c}(\ell)(\pi\ell)^{2}\sum_{|j^{\prime}|\leq(n+1)^{2/3}}\int_{0}^{t}\frac{{\cal b}^{(p,0)}_{n}(s,\varrho_{j^{\prime}})\mathrm{d}s}{(\pi\ell)^{2}+\gamma^{2}\varrho_{j^{\prime}}^{4}}+o_{n}(1)

(9.10)

\displaystyle=\frac{2^{1/2}\tilde{\gamma}}{\pi}\sum_{\ell=1}^{+\infty}c_{\ell}(0)(\pi\ell)^{2}\hat{\varphi}_{c}(\ell)\int_{0}^{t}\mathrm{d}s\int_{0}^{+\infty}\frac{{\cal b}^{(p,0)}_{n}(s,\varrho)\mathrm{d}\varrho}{(\pi\ell)^{2}+\gamma^{2}\varrho^{4}}+o_{n}(1).

We have the following result.

Theorem 9.1
.

For any test function

f\in L^{2}[0,+\infty)
,
t>0

and
v=0,1
we have

(9.11)

\displaystyle\int_{0}^{t}\mathrm{d}s\int_{0}^{+\infty}{\cal b}^{(p,nv)}_{n}(s,\varrho)f(\varrho)\mathrm{d}\varrho

\displaystyle=\sqrt{2}\int_{0}^{+\infty}\Bigg(tT_{v}-\sum_{\ell=0}^{+\infty}\frac{\gamma^{2}\varrho^{4}c_{\ell}(v)\widehat{\mathfrak{E}}_{n}(t,\ell)}{(\ell\pi)^{2}+\gamma^{2}\varrho^{4}}\Bigg)F(\tilde{\gamma},\gamma\varrho^{2})f(\rho)\mathrm{d}\varrho

\displaystyle-\sqrt{2}\int_{0}^{+\infty}\Bigg(tT_{1-v}-\sum_{\ell=0}^{+\infty}\frac{\gamma^{2}\varrho^{4}c_{\ell}(1-v)\widehat{\mathfrak{E}}_{n}(t,\ell)}{(\ell\pi)^{2}+\gamma^{2}\varrho^{4}}\Bigg)G(\tilde{\gamma},\gamma\varrho^{2})f(\rho)\mathrm{d}\varrho+o_{n}(1).

where
\widehat{\mathfrak{E}}_{n}(t,\ell)
is defined by (
8.21
) and

F(\cdot,\cdot)
,
G(\cdot,\cdot)
are defined in (
2.17
).

The proof Theorem
9.1
is presented in Section

10
. As an immediate conclusion of the theorem and estimate (
9.9
) we formulate the following.

Corollary 9.2
.

Suppose that
T(\cdot)
is a limiting point of
\Big({\rm E}_{n}(\cdot)\Big)_{n\geq 1}
and
{\cal b}^{(v)}(s,\varrho)

v=0,\,1
are defined in (
7.4
).
Then, (
7.5
) holds.

Here
we apply it to the function

f(\varrho)=\left[(\pi\ell)^{2}+\gamma^{2}\varrho^{4}\right]^{-1}
.
Then by using asymptotics (
9.10
) and Proposition

8.2
we obtain

(9.12)

\begin{split}\bar{\pi}^{(pr,0)}_{p,o}(n)&=\frac{2^{1/2}\tilde{\gamma}}{\pi}\sum_{\ell=1}^{+\infty}\int_{0}^{\infty}\frac{c_{\ell}(0)(\pi\ell)^{2}\widehat{\varphi}_{c}(\ell)F(\tilde{\gamma},\gamma\varrho^{2})}{(\pi\ell)^{2}+\gamma^{2}\varrho^{4}}\Bigg(tT_{0}-\sum_{\ell^{\prime}=0}^{+\infty}\frac{\gamma^{2}\varrho^{4}c_{\ell^{\prime}}(0)\widehat{\mathfrak{E}}_{n}(t,\ell^{\prime})}{(\ell^{\prime}\pi)^{2}+\gamma^{2}\varrho^{4}}\Bigg)\mathrm{d}\varrho\\
&-\frac{2^{1/2}\tilde{\gamma}}{\pi}\sum_{\ell=1}^{+\infty}\int_{0}^{+\infty}\frac{c_{\ell}(0)(\pi\ell)^{2}\widehat{\varphi}_{c}(\ell)G(\tilde{\gamma},\gamma\varrho^{2})}{(\pi\ell)^{2}+\gamma^{2}\varrho^{4}}\Bigg(tT_{1}-\sum_{\ell=0}^{+\infty}\frac{\gamma^{2}\varrho^{4}c_{\ell}(1)\widehat{\mathfrak{E}}_{n}(t,\ell)}{(\ell\pi)^{2}+\gamma^{2}\varrho^{4}}\Bigg)\mathrm{d}\varrho+o_{n}(1).\end{split}

Observe that, since
{\rm supp}\,\varphi\subset(0,1)
, we have

(9.13)

\begin{split}&0=\varphi(0)=\sum_{\ell=1}^{\infty}\frac{(\pi\ell)^{2}\widehat{\varphi}_{c}(\ell)c_{\ell}(0)}{(\pi\ell)^{2}+\gamma^{2}\varrho^{4}}+\sum_{\ell=0}^{\infty}\frac{\varrho^{4}\widehat{\varphi}_{c}(\ell)c_{\ell}(0)}{(\pi\ell)^{2}+\gamma^{2}\varrho^{4}}\\
&=\sum_{\ell=1}^{\infty}\frac{(\pi\ell)^{2}\widehat{\varphi}_{c}(\ell)c_{\ell}(0)}{(\pi\ell)^{2}+\gamma^{2}\varrho^{4}}+\int_{0}^{1}V_{\gamma\varrho^{4}}(u,0)\varphi(u)\mathrm{d}u.\end{split}

By virtue of (
2.15
) we also have

(9.14)

\begin{split}tT_{0}=tT_{0}\int_{0}^{1}V_{\gamma^{2}\varrho^{4}}(u,0)\mathrm{d}u=\Big(\frac{1}{n}\sum_{y=0}^{n}V_{\gamma^{2}\varrho^{4}}(0,u_{y})\Big)T_{0}t+o_{n}(1).\end{split}

Using (
9.13
) and (
9.14
) in the expression on the utmost
right hand side of (
9.12
) and changing variables

\varrho^{\prime}:=\gamma^{2}\varrho^{4}
we conclude (
6.20
).
The argument for the other boundary
x=n
(and
v=1
) is analogous.
The things that yet need to be done to conclude the proof of
Proposition
6.1
are the proofs of Theorem

9.1
and negligibility of
\bar{\pi}^{(pr)}_{pr}(n)
appearing in (
9.1
).

9.2.
Estimates of
\bar{\pi}^{(pr)}_{pr}(n)

Our goal in the present section is to show the following.

Lemma 9.3
.

There exists a constant
C>0
such that

(9.15)

|\bar{\pi}^{(pr)}_{pr}(n)|\leq\frac{C}{n^{1/2}},\quad n=1,2,\ldots.

Proof.

Let

(9.16)

\begin{split}b^{(pr)}_{z,x}(s)=\mathbb{E}_{n}[p_{z}(s)r_{x}(s)],\quad\quad x=1,\ldots,n,\,\,z=0,n.\end{split}

After a straightforward calculation using (
5.24
) and the parities
of
\widehat{(\varphi^{\prime})_{o}}(j)
and
\widehat{(\varphi^{\prime})_{e}}(j)
we conclude that

\displaystyle\bar{\pi}^{(pr)}_{pr}(n)=\sum_{z=0,n}\bar{\pi}^{(pr)}_{z}(n),\quad\mbox{where}

\displaystyle\bar{\pi}^{(pr)}_{z}(n):=\frac{\tilde{\gamma}\gamma}{2^{3/2}n^{1/2}}\sum_{x=1}^{n}\sum_{j,j^{\prime}=-n-1}^{n}\widehat{(\varphi^{\prime})_{o}}(j-j^{\prime})\sin\Big(\frac{\pi(k_{j}+k_{j^{\prime}})}{2}\Big)\sin\Big(\frac{\pi k_{j^{\prime}}}{2}\Big)

\displaystyle\times\Delta^{-1}(k_{j},k_{j^{\prime}})\Big[\sin^{2}\Big(\frac{\pi k_{j}}{2}\Big)+\sin^{2}\Big(\frac{\pi k_{j^{\prime}}}{2}\Big)\Big]\psi_{j}(z)\phi_{j^{\prime}}(x)\big\langle\!\big\langle b^{(pr)}_{z,x}\big\rangle\!\big\rangle_{t}.

Here

(9.17)

\begin{split}&\Delta(k,k^{\prime}):=\sin^{2}\Big(\frac{\pi(k-k^{\prime})}{2}\Big)\sin^{2}\Big(\frac{\pi(k+k^{\prime})}{2}\Big)\\
&+2^{3}\gamma^{2}\sin^{2}\Big(\frac{\pi k}{2}\Big)\sin^{2}\Big(\frac{\pi k^{\prime}}{2}\Big)\left(\sin^{2}\Big(\frac{\pi k}{2}\Big)+\sin^{2}\Big(\frac{\pi k^{\prime}}{2}\Big)\right).\end{split}

This expression can be further rewritten in the form

(9.18)

\displaystyle\bar{\pi}^{(pr)}_{z}(n)=\frac{\tilde{\gamma}\gamma}{n^{1/2}}\sum_{\ell=-n-1}^{n}\widehat{(\varphi^{\prime})_{o}}(\ell)\sum_{x=1}^{n}\big\langle\!\big\langle b^{(pr)}_{z,x}\big\rangle\!\big\rangle_{t}{\mathfrak{i}}_{x,z}^{(pr)}(\ell)

and

(9.19)

\displaystyle{\mathfrak{i}}_{x,z}^{(pr)}(\ell)=\frac{1}{2^{9/2}}\sum_{j^{\prime}=1}^{n}\phi_{j^{\prime}}(x)\psi_{j^{\prime}+\ell}(z)\sin\Big(\frac{\pi(2k_{j^{\prime}}+k_{\ell})}{2}\Big)\sin\Big(\frac{\pi k_{j^{\prime}}}{2}\Big)

\displaystyle\times\Big[\sin^{2}\Big(\frac{\pi(k_{j^{\prime}}+k_{\ell})}{2}\Big)+\sin^{2}\Big(\frac{\pi k_{j^{\prime}}}{2}\Big)\Big]\Delta^{-1}(k_{j^{\prime}+\ell},k_{j^{\prime}}).

By the Cauchy-Schwarz inequality

(9.20)

\displaystyle\Big|\sum_{x=1}^{n}\big\langle\!\big\langle b^{(pr)}_{z,x}\big\rangle\!\big\rangle_{t}{\mathfrak{i}}_{x,z}^{(pr)}(\ell)\Big|\leq\Big({\cal B}^{(pr)}_{z}\Big)^{1/2}\Big({\cal I}^{(pr)}_{z}(\ell)\Big)^{1/2},\quad\mbox{where}

\displaystyle{\cal B}^{(pr)}_{z}:=\sum_{x=0}^{n}\big\langle\!\big\langle b^{(pr)}_{z,x}\big\rangle\!\big\rangle_{t}^{2},\qquad{\cal I}^{(pr)}_{z}(\ell):=\sum_{x=0}^{n}\big({\mathfrak{i}}_{x,z}^{(p)}(\ell)\big)^{2}.

From estimate (
4.3
) we conclude that
for each
t_{*}>0
there exists
C>0
such that

(9.21)

\sum_{z=0,n}{\cal B}^{(pr)}_{z}\leq\frac{C}{n^{1/2}},\quad t\in[0,t_{*}],\,n=1,2,\ldots.

By the Plancherel identity we have

\displaystyle\sum_{x=1}^{n}({\mathfrak{i}}_{x,z}^{(pr)}(\ell))^{2}=\frac{2}{n+1}\sum_{j^{\prime}=1}^{n}\sin^{2}\Big(\frac{\pi(2k_{j^{\prime}}+k_{\ell})}{2}\Big)\sin^{2}\Big(\frac{\pi k_{j^{\prime}}}{2}\Big)\cos^{2}\Big(\frac{\pi(k_{j^{\prime}}+k_{\ell})}{2}\Big)

\displaystyle\times\Big[\sin^{2}\Big(\frac{\pi(k_{j^{\prime}}+k_{\ell})}{2}\Big)+\sin^{2}\Big(\frac{\pi k_{j^{\prime}}}{2}\Big)\Big]^{2}\Delta^{-2}(k_{j^{\prime}+\ell},k_{j^{\prime}}).

As in Section
9.1
we can restrict ourselves to the case

|\ell|\leq n^{\kappa}
for some
\kappa\in(0,1)
. This allows us to estimate

(9.22)

\displaystyle\sum_{x=1}^{n}({\mathfrak{i}}_{x,z}^{(p,r)}(\ell))^{2}\leq\frac{C}{n+1}\sum_{j^{\prime}=1}^{n}\frac{\sin^{6}\Big(\frac{\pi k_{j^{\prime}}}{2}\Big)}{\Big[(\frac{\ell}{n+1}\Big)^{2}+\sin^{4}\Big(\frac{\pi k_{j^{\prime}}}{2}\Big)\Big]^{2}}

\displaystyle\leq C\int_{0}^{1}\frac{u^{6}\mathrm{d}u}{\Big[(\frac{\ell}{n+1}\Big)^{2}+u^{4}\Big]^{2}}\leq\frac{Cn^{1/2}}{\ell^{1/2}},\quad 1\leq\ell\leq n^{\kappa}.

Combining this estimate with (
9.18
), (
9.20
)
we conclude (
9.15
).∎

10.
Proof of Theorem
9.1

We denote
{\cal b}^{(p,v)}_{n,+}(t,\varrho):={\cal b}^{(p,v)}_{n}(t,\varrho)
, where the functions on the right are defined in (
9.8
).
Define also sequences of functions

(10.1)

\displaystyle{\cal b}^{(p,v)}_{n,-}(t,\varrho):=\tilde{b}_{v,j}^{(p,-)}(t),\quad\mbox{where}\quad\tilde{b}_{v,j}^{(p,-)}(t):=\sum_{x=0}^{n}\psi_{j}(n-x)b_{v,x}^{(p)}(t),

and

(10.2)

\displaystyle{\cal b}^{(pr,v)}_{n}(t,\varrho)=(n+1)^{1/2}\tilde{b}_{nv,j}^{(pr)}(t),\quad\mbox{where}\quad\tilde{b}_{z,j}^{(pr)}(t):=\sum_{x=1}^{n}\phi_{j}(x)b_{z,x}^{(pr)}(t),\quad z=0,n.

for
0\leq j\leq(n+1)^{2/3}
,
t\geq 0,\,\varrho\in\big[\varrho_{j},\varrho_{j+1}\big)
, see (
9.5
) and
(
9.16
).
For
\varrho>\varrho_{(n+1)^{2/3}}
and all
t\geq 0
we let
{\cal b}^{(p,v)}_{n,\epsilon}(t,\varrho)\equiv 0
,
v\in\{0,1\}
,
\varepsilon=\pm
.

By the Plancherel identity and (
4.3
)
for
\iota=p,pr
we have

\displaystyle\int_{0}^{t}\mathrm{d}s\int_{0}^{+\infty}\big[{\cal b}^{(\iota,v)}_{n,\varepsilon}(s,\varrho)\big]^{2}\mathrm{d}\varrho\leq(n+1)^{1/2}\sum_{x=0}^{n}\int_{0}^{t}\big(b_{nv,x}^{(\iota)}(s)\big)^{2}\mathrm{d}s\leq C.

The proof of Theorem
9.1
is a consequence of the following two propositions.

Proposition 10.1
.

For any test function

f\in L^{2}[0,+\infty)
,
t>0

we have

(10.3)

\displaystyle\int_{0}^{t}\mathrm{d}s\int_{0}^{+\infty}{\cal b}^{(p,v)}_{n,+}(s,\varrho)f(\varrho)\mathrm{d}\varrho=

\displaystyle\int_{0}^{+\infty}\Bigg(tT_{v}-\sum_{\ell=0}^{+\infty}\frac{\gamma^{2}\varrho^{4}c_{\ell}(v)\widehat{\mathfrak{E}}_{n}(t,\ell)}{(\ell\pi)^{2}+\gamma^{2}\varrho^{4}}\Bigg)\frac{\sqrt{2}f(\varrho)}{1+2\tilde{\gamma}\coth(\gamma\varrho^{2})}\mathrm{d}\varrho

\displaystyle-\int_{0}^{+\infty}{\cal b}^{(p,1-v)}_{n,-}(\varrho)\frac{2\tilde{\gamma}f(\varrho)}{\sinh(\gamma\varrho^{2})+2\tilde{\gamma}\cosh(\gamma\varrho^{2})}\mathrm{d}\varrho+o_{n}(1).

Proposition 10.2
.

For any test function

f\in L^{2}[0,+\infty)
,
t>0
and
v=0,1

we have

(10.4)

\begin{split}&\int_{0}^{t}\mathrm{d}s\int_{0}^{\infty}{\cal b}^{(p,1-v)}_{n,-}(s,\varrho)f(\varrho)\mathrm{d}\varrho=\int_{0}^{+\infty}\Bigg(tT_{1-v}-\sum_{\ell=0}^{+\infty}\frac{\gamma^{2}\varrho^{4}c_{\ell}(1-v)\widehat{\mathfrak{E}}_{n}(t,\ell)}{(\ell\pi)^{2}+\gamma^{2}\varrho^{4}}\Bigg)\frac{\sqrt{2}f(\varrho)}{1+2\tilde{\gamma}\coth(\gamma\varrho^{2})}\mathrm{d}\varrho\\
&-2\tilde{\gamma}\int_{0}^{t}\mathrm{d}s\int_{0}^{\infty}{\cal b}^{(p,v)}_{n,+}(s,\varrho)\frac{f(\varrho)}{\sinh(\gamma\varrho^{2})+2\tilde{\gamma}\cosh(\gamma\varrho^{2})}\mathrm{d}\varrho+o_{n}(1).\end{split}

10.1.
Asymptotics of
\int_{0}^{t}\cal{b}^{(p,0)}_{n}(s,\varrho)\mathrm{d}s
:
proof of Proposition
10.1

We prove the result for
v=0
. The argument for
v=1
is analogous.

10.1.1.
Preliminaries

Suppose that
f\in C_{c}^{\infty}(0,+\infty)
is a test function
such that
{\rm supp}f\subset[\delta,M]
for some

0<\delta<M<+\infty
.
Summing the expression in (
S3.13
) corresponding to
\iota=p
,
over
j^{\prime}
, we can write

(10.5)

\begin{split}\int_{0}^{t}{\cal b}^{(p,0)}_{n}(s,\varrho)\mathrm{d}s=tT_{L}(\varrho)+{\cal T}^{(p)}_{n}(\varrho)+{\cal P}^{(p)}_{n,p}(\varrho)+{\cal P}^{(p)}_{n,pr}(\varrho)+\sum_{\iota^{\prime}\in I}{\cal X}_{n,\iota^{\prime}}^{(p)}(\varrho).\end{split}

Here
I=\{p,pr,r\}
and for
\varrho\in\big[\varrho_{j},\varrho_{j+1}\big)
(cf (
5.22
), (
5.23
)
and (
5.24
)) we let

\displaystyle T_{L}(\varrho):=\Big(2-\delta_{j,0}\Big)^{1/2}T_{L}\cos\Big(\frac{\varrho_{j}}{2(n+1)^{1/2}}\Big),

\displaystyle{\cal T}_{n}^{(p)}(\varrho)=-(n+1)^{1/2}\sum_{j^{\prime}=0}^{n}\Theta_{p}(\lambda_{j},\lambda_{j^{\prime}})\lambda_{j}^{1/2}\lambda_{j^{\prime}}^{1/2}F_{j,j^{\prime}}\psi_{j^{\prime}}(0),

\displaystyle{\cal X}_{n,\iota^{\prime}}^{(p)}(\varrho)=-(n+1)^{1/2}\sum_{j^{\prime}=0}^{n}\Xi^{(p)}_{\iota^{\prime}}(\lambda_{j},\lambda_{j^{\prime}})R_{j,j^{\prime}}^{(\iota^{\prime})}\psi_{j^{\prime}}(0),\quad\iota^{\prime}\in\{p,pr,rp,r\},

\displaystyle{\cal P}^{(p)}_{n,\iota}(\varrho)=

\displaystyle-(n+1)^{1/2}\sum_{z=0,n}\sum_{j^{\prime}=0}^{n}\Pi^{(p)}_{\iota}(\lambda_{j},\lambda_{j^{\prime}})\psi_{j}(z)\psi_{j^{\prime}}(0)\big\langle\!\big\langle\tilde{b}_{z,j^{\prime}}^{(\iota)}\big\rangle\!\big\rangle_{t}

\displaystyle-(n+1)^{1/2}\sum_{z=0,n}\sum_{j^{\prime}=0}^{n}\Pi^{(p)}_{\iota}(\lambda_{j^{\prime}},\lambda_{j})\psi_{j^{\prime}}(0)\psi_{j^{\prime}}(z)\big\langle\!\big\langle\tilde{b}_{z,j}^{(\iota)}\big\rangle\!\big\rangle_{t},\quad\iota\in\{p,pr\}.

Clearly

(10.6)

\displaystyle t\int_{0}^{+\infty}T_{L}(\varrho)f(\varrho)\mathrm{d}\varrho=\sqrt{2}tT_{0}\int_{0}^{+\infty}f(\varrho)\mathrm{d}\varrho+o_{n}(1).

In the following we prove that

(10.7)

\displaystyle\int_{0}^{\infty}{\cal T}_{n}^{(p)}(\varrho)f(\varrho)\;\mathrm{d}\varrho=-\sum_{\ell=-\infty}^{+\infty}(1+\delta_{0,\ell})^{1/2}\widehat{\mathfrak{E}}_{n}(t,\ell)\int_{0}^{+\infty}\frac{\gamma^{2}\varrho^{4}f(\varrho)\mathrm{d}\varrho}{(\ell\pi)^{2}+\gamma^{2}\varrho^{4}}+o_{n}(1),

(10.8)

\displaystyle\int_{0}^{\infty}{\cal P}^{(p)}_{n,p}(\varrho)f(\varrho)\;\mathrm{d}\varrho=-2\tilde{\gamma}\int_{0}^{t}\mathrm{d}s\int_{0}^{\infty}{\cal b}^{(p,0)}_{n}(s,\varrho)f(\varrho)\mathrm{d}\varrho+o_{n}(1),

(10.9)

\displaystyle\int_{0}^{\infty}{\cal P}^{(p)}_{n,pr}(\varrho)f(\varrho)\;\mathrm{d}\varrho=\frac{\tilde{\gamma}}{\pi}\int_{0}^{t}\mathrm{d}s\int_{0}^{+\infty}{\mathfrak{T}}f(v){\cal b}^{(pr,0)}_{n}(s,v)\mathrm{d}v+o_{n}(1),

(10.10)

\displaystyle\int_{0}^{\infty}\sum_{\iota^{\prime}\in I}{\cal X}_{n,\iota^{\prime}}^{(p)}(\varrho)f(\varrho)\;\mathrm{d}\varrho=o_{n}(1).

Adding up (
10.6
), (
10.7
), (
10.8
), (
10.9
), and (
10.10
)
we conclude Proposition
10.1
.

10.1.2.
Calculation of
{\cal T}_{n}^{(p)}(\varrho)

We have (cf (
8.1
) and (
8.21
))

(10.11)

\displaystyle\int_{0}^{+\infty}{\cal T}_{n}^{(p)}(\varrho)f(\varrho)\mathrm{d}\varrho={\cal T}_{n,+}^{(p)}-{\cal T}_{n,-}^{(p)},\quad\mbox{where}

\displaystyle{\cal T}_{n,\pm}^{(p)}:=2^{4}\gamma^{2}\sum_{1\leq j\leq M(n+1)^{1/2}}\int_{\varrho_{j}}^{\varrho_{j+1}}f(\varrho)\mathrm{d}\varrho\sum_{j^{\prime}=1}^{n}(1+\delta_{0,j\pm j^{\prime}})^{1/2}\cos\Big(\frac{\pi k_{j^{\prime}}}{2}\Big)

\displaystyle\qquad\times\sin^{3}\Big(\frac{\pi k_{j}}{2}\Big)\sin^{3}\Big(\frac{\pi k_{j^{\prime}}}{2}\Big)\Delta^{-1}(k_{j},k_{j^{\prime}})\widehat{\mathfrak{E}}_{n}(t,j\pm j^{\prime}),

with
\Delta(k_{j},k_{j^{\prime}})
defined in (
9.17
). Since
\pi k_{j}=\frac{\varrho_{j}}{(n+1)^{1/2}}
and
{\rm supp}f\subset[\delta,M]
for some

0<\delta<M<+\infty

the summation in (
10.11
) has been restricted to
1\leq j^{\prime}\leq 100M(n+1)^{1/2}
.

Using Lemma
8.3
and repeating calculations leading to estimate of

\bar{\theta}^{(o)}_{pr,+}(\varphi^{\prime};n)
in Section
8
we
conclude that
{\cal T}_{n,+}^{(p)}=o_{n}(1)
.
Consider now
{\cal T}_{n,-}^{(p)}
. We have
\sin^{3}\Big(\frac{\pi k_{j}}{2}\Big)\approx\frac{\varrho^{3}}{2^{3}(n+1)^{3/2}}
for
\varrho\in[\varrho_{j},\varrho_{j+1})
. Denoting
\ell:=j-j^{\prime}
we can write

\displaystyle{\cal T}_{n,-}^{(p)}(t)=-4\gamma^{2}\sum_{1\leq j\leq M(n+1)^{1/2}}\sum_{1-j\leq\ell\leq 100M(n+1)^{1/2}-j}(1+\delta_{0,\ell})^{1/2}

(10.12)

\displaystyle\times\frac{\rho_{j+\ell}^{3}}{\Delta^{\prime\prime}_{\ell}(\varrho_{j},\varrho_{j+\ell})}\int_{\varrho_{j}}^{\varrho_{j+1}}\varrho^{3}f(\varrho)\mathrm{d}\varrho\;\hat{\mathfrak{E}}_{n}(t,\ell)+o_{n}(1),\qquad\mbox{with}

\displaystyle\Delta^{\prime\prime}_{\ell}(\varrho,\varrho^{\prime}):=(\varrho+\varrho^{\prime})^{2}(\ell\pi)^{2}+2\gamma^{2}\varrho^{2}(\varrho^{\prime})^{2}(\varrho+\varrho^{\prime})^{2}.

The last expression can rewritten as

{\cal T}_{n,\leq}^{(p)}+{\cal T}_{n,>}^{(p)}
,
where
{\cal T}_{n,\leq}^{(p)}
,
{\cal T}_{n,>}^{(p)}
correspond to the summation over

|\ell|\leq n^{1/4}
and
|\ell|>n^{1/4}
, respectively.
We have

\frac{\varrho_{j+\ell}^{3}}{(\varrho_{j}+\varrho_{j+\ell})^{2}}\leq C,\quad\mbox{for }1\leq j,\,j+\ell\leq 100M(n+1)^{1/2},

therefore

\displaystyle|{\cal T}_{n,>}^{(p)}|\leq C\sum_{1\leq j\leq M(n+1)^{1/2}}\int_{\rho_{j}}^{\rho_{j+1}}\varrho^{3}|f(\varrho)|\mathrm{d}\varrho\sum_{|\ell|>n^{1/4}}\frac{1}{\ell^{2}}\to 0,

as
n\to+\infty
.
As a result

(10.13)

\displaystyle{\cal T}_{n,-}^{(p)}=-\sum_{\ell=-\infty}^{+\infty}(1+\delta_{0,\ell})^{1/2}\widehat{\mathfrak{E}}_{n}(\ell)\int_{0}^{+\infty}\frac{\gamma^{2}\varrho^{4}f(\varrho)\mathrm{d}\varrho}{(\ell\pi)^{2}+\gamma^{2}\varrho^{4}}+o_{n}(1).

10.1.3.
Calculation of
{\cal P}^{(p)}_{n,p}(\varrho)

We have

\displaystyle\int_{0}^{+\infty}{\cal P}^{(p)}_{n,p}(\varrho)f(\varrho)\mathrm{d}\varrho=\sum_{z=0,n}\big({\rm I}_{n,z}+{\rm II}_{n,z}\big),

\displaystyle{\rm I}_{n,z}=-2\gamma\tilde{\gamma}\sum_{0\leq j\leq M(n+1)^{1/2}}\sum_{j^{\prime}=1}^{n}\int_{0}^{t}{\cal b}^{(p,z)}_{n,+}(s,\varrho_{j^{\prime}})\mathrm{d}s\int_{\varrho_{j}}^{\varrho_{j+1}}f(\varrho)\theta_{j,j^{\prime}}\psi_{j}(z)\psi_{j^{\prime}}(0)\mathrm{d}\varrho

\displaystyle{\rm II}_{n,z}=-2\gamma\tilde{\gamma}\sum_{0\leq j\leq M(n+1)^{1/2}}\sum_{j^{\prime}=0}^{n}\int_{0}^{t}\mathrm{d}s\psi_{j^{\prime}}(z)\psi_{j^{\prime}}(0)\theta_{j,j^{\prime}}\int_{\varrho_{j}}^{\varrho_{j+1}}f(\varrho){\cal b}^{(p,z)}_{n,+}(s,\varrho)\mathrm{d}\varrho,

\displaystyle\mbox{where}\qquad\theta_{j,j^{\prime}}=\frac{\lambda_{j}\lambda_{j^{\prime}}}{(\lambda_{j}-\lambda_{j^{\prime}})^{2}+2\gamma^{2}\lambda_{j}\lambda_{j^{\prime}}(\lambda_{j}+\lambda_{j^{\prime}})}.

Calculation of
{\rm II}_{n,0}

For
\ell=j^{\prime}-j
fixed we can write

\displaystyle\theta_{j,j+\ell}\psi_{j+\ell}^{2}(0)=\frac{2}{n+1}\cdot\frac{\varrho_{j}^{2}\varrho_{j^{\prime}}^{2}}{(\varrho_{j}-\varrho_{j^{\prime}})^{2}(\varrho_{j}+\varrho_{j^{\prime}})^{2}+\frac{2\gamma^{2}}{(n+1)}\varrho_{j}^{2}\varrho_{j^{\prime}}^{2}(\varrho_{j}^{2}+\varrho_{j^{\prime}}^{2})}

\displaystyle=\frac{\varrho^{2}_{j}}{2[(\pi\ell)^{2}+\gamma^{2}\varrho^{4}_{j}]}+o_{n}(1)

and, as can be easily seen for fixed
0<\delta<M
we have

0\leq\theta_{j,j+\ell}\psi_{j+\ell}^{2}(0)\leq\frac{C}{1+\ell^{2}},\quad\ell\in\mathbb{Z},

with the constant
C>0
independent of
n
.
Therefore, we can
write

\displaystyle{\rm II}_{n,0}=-\gamma\tilde{\gamma}\sum_{0\leq j\leq M(n+1)^{1/2}}\int_{0}^{t}\mathrm{d}s\int_{\varrho_{j}}^{\varrho_{j+1}}f(\varrho){\cal b}^{(p,0)}_{n,+}(s,\varrho)\mathrm{d}\varrho\Big(\sum_{\ell=-j}^{M(n+1)^{1/2}-\ell}\frac{\varrho^{2}}{(\pi\ell)^{2}+\gamma^{2}\varrho^{4}}\Big)+o_{n}(1)

\displaystyle=-\gamma\tilde{\gamma}\sum_{0\leq j\leq M(n+1)^{1/2}}\int_{0}^{t}\mathrm{d}s\int_{\varrho_{j}}^{\varrho_{j+1}}f(\varrho){\cal b}^{(p,0)}_{n,+}(s,\varrho)\mathrm{d}\varrho\Big(\sum_{\ell\in\mathbb{Z}}\frac{\varrho^{2}}{(\pi\ell)^{2}+\gamma^{2}\varrho^{4}}\Big)+o_{n}(1).

Using the well known formula

\sum_{\ell\in\mathbb{Z}}\frac{A}{(\pi\ell)^{2}+A^{2}}=\coth(A).

We conclude that

(10.14)

\displaystyle{\rm II}_{n,0}=-\tilde{\gamma}\int_{0}^{t}\mathrm{d}s\int_{0}^{+\infty}f(\varrho)\coth(\gamma\rho^{2}){\cal b}^{(p,z)}_{n,+}(s,\varrho)\mathrm{d}\varrho.

Calculation of
{\rm II}_{n,n}

For
j^{\prime}=j+\ell
and
\ell
fixed
we can write

\displaystyle\theta_{j,j+\ell}\psi_{j+\ell}(0)\psi_{j+\ell}(n)=\theta_{j,j+\ell}\psi_{j+\ell}^{2}(0)(-1)^{j+\ell}

\displaystyle=\frac{2}{n+1}\cdot\frac{(-1)^{j+\ell}\varrho_{j}^{2}\varrho_{j^{\prime}}^{2}}{(\varrho_{j}-\varrho_{j^{\prime}})^{2}(\varrho_{j}+\varrho_{j^{\prime}})^{2}+\frac{2\gamma^{2}}{(n+1)}\varrho_{j}^{2}\varrho_{j^{\prime}}^{2}(\varrho_{j}^{2}+\varrho_{j^{\prime}}^{2})}+o_{n}(1)

\displaystyle=\frac{(-1)^{j+\ell}\varrho_{j}^{2}}{2[(\pi\ell)^{2}+\gamma^{2}\varrho_{j}^{4}]}+o_{n}(1)

As a result, repeating the calculations made for
{\rm II}_{n,0}
and
using the formula

\sum_{\ell\in\mathbb{Z}}\frac{A(-1)^{\ell}}{(\pi\ell)^{2}+A^{2}}={\rm csch}\,(A),

we get

\displaystyle{\rm II}_{n,n}=-\tilde{\gamma}\int_{0}^{t}\mathrm{d}s\int_{0}^{+\infty}{\rm csch}\,(\gamma\rho^{2})f(\varrho){\cal b}^{(p,n)}_{n,-}(s,\varrho)\mathrm{d}\varrho+o_{n}(1),

where
{\rm csch}\,(u)=\frac{1}{\sinh u}
is the hyperbolic cosecant.

Calculation of
{\rm I}_{n,0}

For
j^{\prime}=j+\ell
and a fixed

\ell
we can write

\displaystyle\psi_{j^{\prime}}^{2}(0)\theta_{j^{\prime}-\ell,j^{\prime}}=\frac{\varrho^{2}_{j^{\prime}}}{2[(\pi\ell)^{2}+\gamma^{2}\varrho^{4}_{j^{\prime}}]}+o_{n}(1).

Hence

\displaystyle{\rm I}_{n,0}=-\gamma\tilde{\gamma}\sum_{j^{\prime}=0}^{2M(n+1)^{1/2}}\sum_{\ell=j^{\prime}-2M(n+1)^{1/2}}^{j^{\prime}}\frac{\varrho_{j^{\prime}}^{2}}{(\pi\ell)^{2}+\gamma^{2}\varrho_{j^{\prime}}^{4}}\int_{0}^{t}\int_{\varrho_{j}^{\prime}}^{\varrho_{j+1}^{\prime}}{\cal b}^{(p,0)}_{n,+}(s,\varrho_{j^{\prime}})\mathrm{d}sf(\varrho^{\prime})\mathrm{d}\varrho^{\prime}+o_{n}(1)

As a result we have

\displaystyle{\rm I}_{n,0}=-\gamma\tilde{\gamma}\sum_{j^{\prime}=0}^{2M(n+1)^{1/2}}\sum_{\ell\in\mathbb{Z}}\int_{0}^{t}\int_{\varrho_{j}^{\prime}}^{\varrho_{j+1}^{\prime}}\frac{(\varrho^{\prime})^{2}}{(\pi\ell)^{2}+\gamma^{2}(\varrho^{\prime})^{4}}{\cal b}^{(p,0)}_{n,+}(s,\varrho^{\prime})\mathrm{d}sf(\varrho^{\prime})\mathrm{d}\varrho^{\prime}+o_{n}(1)

\displaystyle=-\tilde{\gamma}\int_{0}^{t}\mathrm{d}s\int_{0}^{+\infty}f(\varrho)\coth(\gamma\rho^{2}){\cal b}^{(p,0)}_{n,+}(s,\varrho)\mathrm{d}\varrho.

Conducting a similar calculation for
{\rm I}_{n,n}
we obtain that,
due to the cancelation, appearing in the same way as in the case of

{\rm II}_{n,n}
that

\displaystyle{\rm I}_{n,n}=-\tilde{\gamma}\int_{0}^{t}\mathrm{d}s\int_{0}^{+\infty}f(\varrho){\rm csch}\,(\gamma\rho^{2}){\cal b}^{(p,n)}_{n,-}(s,\varrho)\mathrm{d}\varrho.

Summarizing, we have shown that

\displaystyle\int_{0}^{+\infty}{\cal P}^{(p)}_{n,p}(\varrho)f(\varrho)\mathrm{d}\varrho=-2\tilde{\gamma}\int_{0}^{t}\mathrm{d}s\int_{0}^{\infty}{\cal b}^{(p,0)}_{n,+}(s,\varrho)\coth(\gamma\rho^{2})f(\varrho)\mathrm{d}\varrho

(10.15)

\displaystyle-2\tilde{\gamma}\int_{0}^{t}\mathrm{d}s\int_{0}^{\infty}{\cal b}^{(p,n)}_{n,-}(s,\varrho){\rm csch}\,(\gamma\rho^{2})f(\varrho)\mathrm{d}\varrho+o_{n}(1).

10.1.4.
Calculation of
{\cal P}^{(p)}_{n,pr}(\varrho)

We have (cf (
5.23
), (
5.24
))

\displaystyle\int_{0}^{+\infty}{\cal P}^{(p)}_{n,pr}(\varrho)f(\varrho)\mathrm{d}\varrho=\sum_{z=0,n}\big({\rm I}_{n,z}+{\rm II}_{n,z}\big),\quad\mbox{where}

\displaystyle{\rm I}_{n,z}=\tilde{\gamma}\sum_{j=0}^{M(n+1)^{1/2}}\sum_{j^{\prime}=1}^{n}\psi_{j}(z)\psi_{j^{\prime}}(0)\theta^{(p,pr)}_{j,j^{\prime}}\int_{0}^{t}{\cal b}^{(pr,z)}_{n}(s,\varrho_{j^{\prime}})\mathrm{d}s\int_{\varrho_{j}}^{\varrho_{j+1}}f(\varrho)\mathrm{d}\varrho,

\displaystyle{\rm II}_{n,z}=\tilde{\gamma}\sum_{j=0}^{M(n+1)^{1/2}}\sum_{j^{\prime}=0}^{n}\psi_{j^{\prime}}(0)\psi_{j^{\prime}}(z)\theta^{(p,pr)}_{j^{\prime},j}\int_{0}^{t}\mathrm{d}s\int_{\varrho_{j}}^{\varrho_{j+1}}{\cal b}^{(pr,z)}_{n}(s,\varrho)f(\varrho)\mathrm{d}\varrho

(10.16)

\displaystyle\mbox{and}\qquad\theta^{(p,pr)}_{j,j^{\prime}}:=\frac{\lambda_{j^{\prime}}^{1/2}(\lambda_{j^{\prime}}-\lambda_{j})}{(\lambda_{j}-\lambda_{j^{\prime}})^{2}+2\gamma^{2}\lambda_{j}\lambda_{j^{\prime}}(\lambda_{j}+\lambda_{j^{\prime}})}.

Calculation of
{\rm I}_{n,0}+{\rm II}_{n,0}

For
j^{\prime}=j+\ell
,
\ell
fixed
and
\kappa\in(0,1/100)
we can write

\displaystyle\psi_{j+\ell}^{2}(0)\theta^{(p,pr)}_{j,j+\ell}=\frac{2}{n+1}\cdot\frac{\varrho_{j^{\prime}}(\varrho_{j}-\varrho_{j+\ell})(\varrho_{j}+\varrho_{j+\ell})}{(\varrho_{j}-\varrho_{j+\ell})^{2}(\varrho_{j}+\varrho_{j+\ell})^{2}+\frac{2\gamma^{2}}{(n+1)}\varrho_{j}^{2}\varrho_{j+\ell}^{2}(\varrho_{j}^{2}+\varrho_{j+\ell}^{2})}

\displaystyle=\frac{-\pi\ell}{(n+1)^{1/2}[(\pi\ell)^{2}+\gamma^{2}\varrho_{j}^{4}]}+o_{n}(1)

and

\psi_{j+\ell}^{2}(0)|\theta^{(p,pr)}_{j,j+\ell}|\leq\frac{C|\ell|}{(n+1)^{1/2}(1+\ell^{2})}

Taking this into account we can write

\displaystyle{\rm II}_{n,0}=-\frac{\tilde{\gamma}}{(n+1)^{1/2}}\sum_{j=1}^{M(n+1)^{1/2+\kappa}}\sum_{\ell=-j}^{M(n+1)^{1/2+\kappa}-j}\frac{\pi\ell}{(\pi\ell)^{2}+\gamma^{2}\varrho_{j}^{4}}

\displaystyle\times\varrho_{j}\int_{0}^{t}\mathrm{d}s\int_{\varrho_{j}}^{\varrho_{j+1}}{\cal b}^{(pr,z)}_{n}(s,\varrho)f(\varrho)\mathrm{d}\varrho+o_{n}(1).

Since
{\rm supp}\,f\subset[\delta,M]
for some
0<\delta<M
, the summation over
j
extends
only from
0
to
M(n+1)^{1/2+\kappa}/2
. Performing the summation
over
\ell
yields

\displaystyle{\rm II}_{n,0}=-\frac{\tilde{\gamma}}{(n+1)^{1/2}}\sum_{j=1}^{M(n+1)^{1/2+\kappa}}\sum_{\ell=j+1}^{M(n+1)^{1/2+\kappa}-j}\frac{\pi\ell}{(\pi\ell)^{2}+\gamma^{2}\varrho_{j}^{4}}

\displaystyle\times\varrho_{j}\int_{0}^{t}\mathrm{d}s\int_{\varrho_{j}}^{\varrho_{j+1}}{\cal b}^{(pr,z)}_{n}(s,\varrho)f(\varrho)\mathrm{d}\varrho+o_{n}(1).

Hence

\displaystyle|{\rm II}_{n,0}|\leq\frac{C}{(n+1)^{1/2}}\sum_{j=1}^{M(n+1)^{1/2+\kappa}}\sum_{\ell=j+1}^{M(n+1)^{1/2+\kappa}-j}\frac{1}{\pi\ell}

\displaystyle\times|\varrho_{j}|\int_{0}^{t}\mathrm{d}s\int_{\varrho_{j}}^{\varrho_{j+1}}|{\cal b}^{(pr,z)}_{n}(s,\varrho)||f(\varrho)|\mathrm{d}\varrho+o_{n}(1).

\displaystyle\leq\sum_{j=1}^{M(n+1)^{1/2+\kappa}}\frac{\log j}{(n+1)^{1/2}}s\int_{0}^{t}\mathrm{d}s\int_{\varrho_{j}}^{\varrho_{j+1}}|{\cal b}^{(pr,z)}_{n}(s,\varrho)||f(\varrho)|\mathrm{d}\varrho+o_{n}(1)

\displaystyle\leq\frac{C\log n}{n}\int_{0}^{t}\mathrm{d}s\int_{0}^{+\infty}|{\cal b}^{(pr,z)}_{n}(s,\varrho)||f(\varrho)|\mathrm{d}\varrho+o_{n}(1),

which tends to
0
, as
n\to+\infty
.

On the other hand

\displaystyle{\rm I}_{n,0}=\frac{2\tilde{\gamma}}{\pi}\sum_{j,j^{\prime}=1}^{M(n+1)^{1/2+\kappa}}\varrho_{j^{\prime}}\int_{0}^{t}\mathrm{d}s\int_{\varrho_{j^{\prime}}}^{\varrho_{j^{\prime}+1}}{\cal b}^{(pr,0)}_{n}(s,\varrho^{\prime})\mathrm{d}\varrho^{\prime}\int_{\varrho_{j}}^{\varrho_{j+1}}f(\varrho^{\prime})\mathrm{d}\varrho^{\prime}

\displaystyle\times\frac{(\varrho_{j^{\prime}}-\varrho_{j})(\varrho_{j}+\varrho_{j^{\prime}})}{(\varrho_{j}-\varrho_{j^{\prime}})^{2}(\varrho_{j}+\varrho_{j^{\prime}})^{2}+\frac{2\gamma^{2}}{(n+1)}\varrho_{j}^{2}\varrho_{j^{\prime}}^{2}(\varrho_{j}^{2}+\varrho_{j^{\prime}}^{2})}+o_{n}(1).

Fixing
\ell=j^{\prime}-j
we get

\displaystyle\Big(\int_{\varrho_{j}}^{\varrho_{j+1}}f(\varrho^{\prime})\mathrm{d}\varrho^{\prime}\Big)\frac{\varrho_{j^{\prime}}(\varrho_{j^{\prime}}-\varrho_{j})(\varrho_{j}+\varrho_{j^{\prime}})}{(\varrho_{j}-\varrho_{j^{\prime}})^{2}(\varrho_{j}+\varrho_{j^{\prime}})^{2}+\frac{2\gamma^{2}}{(n+1)}\varrho_{j}^{2}\varrho_{j^{\prime}}^{2}(\varrho_{j}^{2}+\varrho_{j^{\prime}}^{2})}

\displaystyle=\frac{\pi\ell f(\varrho_{j^{\prime}})}{(n+1)[(\pi\ell)^{2}+\gamma^{2}\varrho_{j^{\prime}}^{4}]}+o_{n}(1)

and, as an be easily seen for
\rho_{j}\in[\delta,M]
we have

\displaystyle\Big|\int_{\varrho_{j}}^{\varrho_{j+1}}f(\varrho^{\prime})\mathrm{d}\varrho^{\prime}\Big|\frac{\varrho_{j^{\prime}}|\varrho_{j^{\prime}}-\varrho_{j}|(\varrho_{j}+\varrho_{j^{\prime}})}{(\varrho_{j}-\varrho_{j^{\prime}})^{2}(\varrho_{j}+\varrho_{j^{\prime}})^{2}+\frac{2\gamma^{2}}{(n+1)}\varrho_{j}^{2}\varrho_{j^{\prime}}^{2}(\varrho_{j}^{2}+\varrho_{j^{\prime}}^{2})}

\displaystyle\leq\frac{C|\ell|}{(n+1)[(\ell)^{2}+1]}.

The same argument as in the case of
{\rm II}_{n,0}
allows us to
conclude that
{\rm I}_{n,0}\to 0
, as
n\to+\infty
.

In the case of
{\rm I}_{n,n}+{\rm II}_{n,n}
, the presence
of factors
\psi_{j}(n)
and
\psi_{j^{\prime}}(n)
does not influence the
situation (check!). As a result we get
{\rm I}_{n,n}+{\rm II}_{n,n}\to 0
.

10.1.5.
Calculation of
{\cal X}_{n,\iota}^{(p)}(\varrho)
,

\iota=p,pr,rp,r

Recall that
{\rm supp}f\subset[\delta,M]
for some

0<\delta<M<+\infty
. We have

\displaystyle\int_{0}^{+\infty}{\cal X}_{n,p}^{(p)}(\varrho)f(\varrho)\mathrm{d}\varrho

\displaystyle=-\frac{(n+1)^{1/2}}{n^{3/2}}\sum_{0\leq j\leq M(n+1)^{1/2}}\sum_{j^{\prime}=0}^{n}\Xi^{(p)}_{p}(\lambda_{j},\lambda_{j^{\prime}})\psi_{j^{\prime}}(0)\delta_{0,t}{\tilde{S}}_{j,j^{\prime}}^{(p)}\int_{\frac{j\pi}{(n+1)^{1/2}}}^{\frac{(j+1)\pi}{(n+1)^{1/2}}}f(\varrho)\mathrm{d}\varrho.

Substituting for
\Xi^{(p)}_{p}(\lambda_{j},\lambda_{j^{\prime}})
from (
5.23
), we can
write that the right hand side equals
{\rm I}_{n}+o_{n}(1)
, where

\displaystyle{\rm I}_{n}=-\frac{2\gamma}{n(n+1)^{1/2}}\sum_{0\leq j,j^{\prime}\leq 100M(n+1)^{1/2}}\sin^{2}\Big(\frac{k_{j}\pi}{2}\Big)\sin^{2}\Big(\frac{k_{j^{\prime}}\pi}{2}\Big)\cos\Big(\frac{k_{j^{\prime}}\pi}{2}\Big)\delta_{0,t}{\tilde{S}}_{j,j^{\prime}}^{(p)}

\displaystyle\times\Delta^{-1}(k_{j},k_{j^{\prime}})\int_{\frac{j\pi}{(n+1)^{1/2}}}^{\frac{(j+1)\pi}{(n+1)^{1/2}}}f(\varrho)\mathrm{d}\varrho.

Hence,

\displaystyle|{\rm I}_{n}|\leq\frac{C}{n^{3/2}}\sum_{0\leq j\leq M(n+1)^{1/2}}\sum_{0\leq j^{\prime}\leq 100M(n+1)^{1/2}}\big|\delta_{0,t}{\tilde{S}}_{j,j^{\prime}}^{(p)}\big|\int_{\frac{j\pi}{(n+1)^{1/2}}}^{\frac{(j+1)\pi}{(n+1)^{1/2}}}|f(\varrho)|\mathrm{d}\varrho

(10.17)

\displaystyle\times\frac{\varrho_{j}^{2}\varrho_{j^{\prime}}^{2}}{(\varrho_{j}-\varrho_{j^{\prime}})^{2}(\varrho_{j}+\varrho_{j^{\prime}})^{2}+\frac{1}{n+1}(\varrho_{j}\varrho_{j^{\prime}})^{2}(\varrho_{j}^{2}+\varrho_{j^{\prime}}^{2})}

\displaystyle\leq\frac{C}{n^{2}}\sum_{0\leq j,j^{\prime}\leq 100M(n+1)^{1/2}}\big|\delta_{0,t}{\tilde{S}}_{j,j^{\prime}}^{(p)}\big|={\rm I}_{n,\leq}^{(1)}+{\rm I}_{n,\leq}^{(2)}+{\rm I}_{n,\leq}^{(3)},

where the terms of the summation on the utmost right hand side correspond to the cases
n^{1/4}<|j-j^{\prime}|
,

1\leq|j-j^{\prime}|\leq n^{1/4}
and
j=j^{\prime}
.

The term
{\rm I}_{n,\leq}^{(1)}
can be estimated using the
Cauchy-Schwarz inequality and (
4.2
) as follows

\displaystyle{\rm I}_{n,\leq}^{(1)}\leq\frac{C}{n^{3/2}}(n+1)^{1/2}\sum_{0\leq j,j^{\prime}\leq 100M(n+1)^{1/2}}\big|\delta_{0,t}{\tilde{S}}_{j,j^{\prime}}^{(p)}\big|\int_{\frac{j\pi}{(n+1)^{1/2}}}^{\frac{(j+1)\pi}{(n+1)^{1/2}}}|f(\varrho)|\mathrm{d}\varrho

\displaystyle\leq\frac{C}{n}\Big(\sum_{0\leq j,j^{\prime}\leq 100M(n+1)^{1/2}}\big[\delta_{0,t}{\tilde{S}}_{j,j^{\prime}}^{(p)}\big]^{2}\Big)^{1/2}\leq\frac{C}{n^{1/2}}.

Concerning
{\rm I}_{n,\leq}^{(3)}
we have

\displaystyle{\rm I}_{n,\leq}^{(3)}\leq\frac{C}{n^{2}}\sum_{\delta(n+1)^{1/2}\leq j\leq 100M(n+1)^{1/2}}\varrho_{j}^{4}\mathbb{E}_{n}\big[\tilde{p}_{j}^{2}(t)+\tilde{p}_{j}^{2}(0)\big]\Big(\frac{\varrho_{j}^{6}}{n+1}\Big)^{-1}

(10.18)

\displaystyle\leq C\sum_{\delta(n+1)^{1/2}\leq j\leq 100M(n+1)^{1/2}}\frac{\mathbb{E}_{n}\big[\tilde{p}_{j}^{2}(t)+\tilde{p}_{j}^{2}(0)\big]}{j^{2}}.

From Corollary
5.1
it follows that
for any
t_{*}>0
there exists
C>0
such that

(10.19)

\frac{1}{n}\sum_{j=0}^{n}\Big[\Big(\mathbb{E}_{n}[\tilde{p}_{j}^{2}(t)]\Big)^{2}+\Big(\mathbb{E}_{n}[\tilde{r}_{j}^{2}(t)]\Big)^{2}\Big]\leq C,\quad n=1,2,\ldots,\,t\in[0,t_{*}].

Using (
10.19
) and the Cauchy-Schwarz
inequality we can write

\displaystyle{\rm I}_{n,\leq}^{(3)}\leq C\left\{\sum_{0\leq j\leq 100M(n+1)^{1/2}}\frac{1}{j^{4}}\right\}^{1/2}\left\{\sum_{\delta(n+1)^{1/2}\leq j\leq 100M(n+1)^{1/2}}\big(\mathbb{E}_{n}\big[\tilde{p}_{j}^{2}(t)\big]\big)^{2}\right\}^{1/2}

\displaystyle\leq\frac{C}{n^{3/4}}\left\{\sum_{0\leq j\leq n}\big(\mathbb{E}_{n}\big[\tilde{p}_{j}^{2}(t)\big]\big)^{2}\right\}^{1/2}\leq\frac{C}{n^{3/4}}(n+1)^{1/2}\to 0,\quad n\to+\infty.

Finally,

\displaystyle{\rm I}_{n,\leq}^{(2)}\leq\frac{C}{n^{2}}\sum_{\delta(n+1)^{1/2}\leq j\leq 100M(n+1)^{1/2}}\mathop{\sum_{|\ell|\leq(n+1)^{1/4}}}_{0\leq j+\ell\leq M(n+1)^{1/2}}\frac{|j|^{2}|j+\ell|^{2}}{|\ell|^{2}|2j+\ell|^{2}}\big|\mathbb{E}_{n}\big[\tilde{p}_{j}(t)\tilde{p}_{j+\ell}(t)\big]\big|

\displaystyle\leq C\sum_{\delta(n+1)^{1/2}\leq j\leq 100M(n+1)^{1/2}}\mathop{\sum_{1\leq|\ell|\leq(n+1)^{1/4}}}_{0\leq j+\ell\leq M(n+1)^{1/2}}\frac{\big|\mathbb{E}_{n}\big[\tilde{p}_{j}^{2}(t)\big]\big|+\big|\mathbb{E}_{n}\big[\tilde{p}_{j+\ell}^{2}(t)\big]\big|}{|\ell|^{2}|2j+\ell|^{2}}

\displaystyle\leq C\Big(\sum_{1\leq|\ell|\leq(n+1)^{1/4}}\frac{1}{|\ell|^{2}}\Big)\sum_{\delta(n+1)^{1/2}\leq j\leq 100M(n+1)^{1/2}}\frac{\big|\mathbb{E}_{n}\big[\tilde{p}_{j}^{2}(t)\big]\big|}{j^{2}}\leq\frac{C}{n^{1/4}}.

In a similar fashion one can also show that

\int_{0}^{+\infty}{\cal X}_{r,n}^{(p)}(\varrho)f(\varrho)\mathrm{d}\varrho\to 0
,
as
n\to+\infty
.

On the other hand,

\displaystyle\int_{0}^{+\infty}\big({\cal X}_{pr,n}^{(p)}(\varrho)+{\cal X}_{rp,n}^{(p)}(u)\big)f(\varrho)\mathrm{d}\varrho=-\frac{(n+1)^{1/2}}{n^{3/2}}\sum_{0\leq j\leq M(n+1)^{1/2}}\int_{\frac{j\pi}{(n+1)^{1/2}}}^{\frac{(j+1)\pi}{(n+1)^{1/2}}}f(\varrho)\mathrm{d}\varrho

\displaystyle\times\sum_{j^{\prime}=0}^{n}\Big(\Xi^{(p)}_{pr}(\lambda_{j},\lambda_{j^{\prime}})\delta_{0,t}{\tilde{S}}_{j,j^{\prime}}^{(pr)}+\Xi^{(p)}_{rp}(\lambda_{j},\lambda_{j^{\prime}})\delta_{0,t}{\tilde{S}}_{j,j^{\prime}}^{(rp)}\Big)\psi_{j^{\prime}}(0).

Choosing
\kappa>0
, to be determined later on, and using the fact
that
\Xi^{(p)}_{pr}(\lambda_{j},\lambda_{j^{\prime}})=\Xi^{(p)}_{rp}(\lambda_{j^{\prime}},\lambda_{j})

we conclude that the left hand side equals
{\rm J}_{n}^{(1)}+{\rm J}_{n}^{(2)}+o_{n}(1)
, where

\displaystyle{\rm J}_{n}^{(1)}=-\frac{2^{1/2}}{n(n+1)^{1/2}}\sum_{0\leq j,j^{\prime}\leq M(n+1)^{1/2+\kappa}}\Xi^{(p)}_{pr}(\lambda_{j},\lambda_{j^{\prime}})\delta_{0,t}{\tilde{S}}_{j,j^{\prime}}^{(pr)}\int_{\frac{j\pi}{(n+1)^{1/2}}}^{\frac{(j+1)\pi}{(n+1)^{1/2}}}f(\varrho)\mathrm{d}\varrho,

\displaystyle{\rm J}_{n}^{(2)}:=-\frac{2^{1/2}}{n(n+1)^{1/2}}\sum_{0\leq j,j^{\prime}\leq M(n+1)^{1/2+\kappa}}\Xi^{(p)}_{rp}(\lambda_{j},\lambda_{j^{\prime}})\delta_{0,t}{\tilde{S}}_{j,j^{\prime}}^{(rp)}\int_{\frac{j\pi}{(n+1)^{1/2}}}^{\frac{(j+1)\pi}{(n+1)^{1/2}}}f(\varrho)\mathrm{d}\varrho.

Using the definitions of
\Xi^{(p)}_{pr}(\lambda_{j},\lambda_{j^{\prime}})
and

\Xi^{(p)}_{rp}(\lambda_{j},\lambda_{j^{\prime}})
(see (
5.23
)) we conclude that

\displaystyle|{\rm J}_{n}^{(1)}|\leq\frac{C}{n^{2}}\sup_{t\in[0,t_{*}]}\sum_{0\leq j,j^{\prime}\leq M(n+1)^{1/2+\kappa}}\Big|\sin\Big(\frac{k_{j^{\prime}}\pi}{2}\Big)\Big|

\displaystyle\times\Big|\sin\Big(\frac{(k_{j}-k_{j^{\prime}})\pi}{2}\Big)\sin\Big(\frac{(k_{j}+k_{j^{\prime}})\pi}{2}\Big)\Big|\frac{\big|\mathbb{E}_{n}\big[\tilde{r}_{j^{\prime}}(t)\tilde{p}_{j}(t)\Big]\big|}{\Delta(k_{j},k_{j^{\prime}})}

\displaystyle\leq\frac{C}{n}\sup_{t\in[0,t_{*}]}\sum_{0\leq j,j^{\prime}\leq M(n+1)^{1/2+\kappa}}\frac{j^{\prime}|j-j^{\prime}|(j+j^{\prime})}{(j-j^{\prime})^{2}(j+j^{\prime})^{2}}\big|\mathbb{E}_{n}\big[\tilde{r}_{j^{\prime}}(t)\tilde{p}_{j}(t)\Big]\big|

\displaystyle\leq\frac{C}{n}\sup_{t\in[0,t_{*}]}\sum_{0\leq j,j^{\prime}\leq M(n+1)^{1/2+\kappa}}\frac{\big|\mathbb{E}_{n}\big[\tilde{r}_{j^{\prime}}(t)\tilde{p}_{j}(t)\Big]\big|}{|j-j^{\prime}|+1}.

We can estimate the last expression using the Cauchy-Schwarz
inequality and estimate (
5.25
). Hence,

\displaystyle|{\rm J}_{n}^{(1)}|\leq\frac{C}{n}\sup_{t\in[0,t_{*}]}\left\{\sum_{0\leq j,j^{\prime}\leq M(n+1)^{1/2+\kappa}}\big|\mathbb{E}_{n}\big[\tilde{r}_{j^{\prime}}(n^{3/2}t)\tilde{p}_{j}(n^{3/2}t)\Big]\big|^{2}\right\}^{1/2}

\displaystyle\times\left\{\sum_{0\leq j,j^{\prime}\leq M(n+1)^{1/2+\kappa}}\frac{1}{(|j-j^{\prime}|+1)^{2}}\right\}^{1/2}\leq\frac{C}{n^{1/4-\kappa/2}}\to 0,

provided
\kappa<1/2
. Similarly, we have

{\rm J}_{n}^{(2)}\to 0
, as
n\to+\infty
.
This ends the proof of Proposition
10.1
.∎

10.1.6.
Summary

We have shown the following.

(10.20)

\displaystyle\int_{0}^{t}\mathrm{d}s\int_{0}^{\infty}{\cal b}^{(p,0)}_{n,+}(s,\varrho)[1+2\tilde{\gamma}\coth(\gamma\rho^{2})]f(\varrho)\mathrm{d}\varrho

\displaystyle=\sqrt{2}\int_{0}^{+\infty}\Bigg(tT_{0}-\sum_{\ell=0}^{+\infty}\frac{\gamma^{2}\varrho^{4}c_{\ell}(v)\widehat{\mathfrak{E}}_{n}(t,\ell)}{(\ell\pi)^{2}+\gamma^{2}\varrho^{4}}\Bigg)f(\varrho)\mathrm{d}\varrho

\displaystyle-2\tilde{\gamma}\int_{0}^{t}\mathrm{d}s\int_{0}^{\infty}{\cal b}^{(p,n)}_{n,-}(s,\varrho){\rm csch}\,(\gamma\rho^{2})f(\varrho)\mathrm{d}\varrho+o_{n}(1).

Substituing
[1+2\tilde{\gamma}\coth(\gamma\rho^{2})]f(\varrho)

in place
f(\varrho)
we conclude Proposition
10.1
.∎

10.2.
Asymptotics of
\int_{0}^{t}\mathrm{d}s\int_{0}^{\infty}{\cal b}^{(p,1-v)}_{n,-}(s,\varrho)

: proof of Proposition
10.2

We could repeat the argument made in Section
10.1
, but
instead note that using the transformation
x\mapsto n-x
we can
invert the roles of the left and right endpoints of the chain. With
this observation we can conclude (
10.4
) directly from (
10.3
).∎

10.3.
Proof of Theorem
9.1

We consider only the case
v=0
, as the case
v=1
can be dealt with
analogously. Combining the results of Propositions
10.1
and

10.2
we obtain

f\in C_{c}(0,+\infty)
,
t>0

we get

\displaystyle\int_{0}^{t}\mathrm{d}s\int_{0}^{+\infty}{\cal b}^{(p,0)}_{n,+}(s,\varrho)f(\varrho)\Bigg[1-\Big(\frac{2\tilde{\gamma}}{\sinh(\gamma\varrho^{2})+2\tilde{\gamma}\cosh(\gamma\varrho^{2})}\Big)^{2}\Bigg]\mathrm{d}\varrho

\displaystyle=\int_{0}^{+\infty}\Bigg(tT_{L}-\sum_{\ell=0}^{+\infty}\frac{\gamma^{2}\varrho^{4}c_{\ell}(0)\widehat{\mathfrak{E}}_{n}(t,\ell)}{(\ell\pi)^{2}+\gamma^{2}\varrho^{4}}\Bigg)\frac{\sqrt{2}f(\varrho)}{1+2\tilde{\gamma}\coth(\gamma\varrho^{2})}\mathrm{d}\varrho

\displaystyle-\int_{0}^{+\infty}\Bigg(tT_{R}-\sum_{\ell=0}^{+\infty}\frac{\gamma^{2}\varrho^{4}c_{\ell}(1)\widehat{\mathfrak{E}}_{n}(t,\ell)}{(\ell\pi)^{2}+\gamma^{2}\varrho^{4}}\Bigg)\frac{\sqrt{2}}{1+2\tilde{\gamma}\coth(\gamma\varrho^{2})}\cdot\frac{2\tilde{\gamma}f(\varrho)}{\sinh(\gamma\varrho^{2})+2\tilde{\gamma}\cosh(\gamma\varrho^{2})}\mathrm{d}\varrho+o_{n}(1).

Using the identity

\displaystyle 1-\left(\frac{2\widetilde{\gamma}}{\sinh(\gamma\varrho^{2})+2\widetilde{\gamma}\cosh(\gamma\varrho^{2})}\right)^{2}

\displaystyle=\frac{\sinh(\gamma\varrho^{2})\left[(1+4\widetilde{\gamma}^{2})\sinh(\gamma\varrho^{2})+4\widetilde{\gamma}\cosh(\gamma\varrho^{2})\right]}{\left[\sinh(\gamma\varrho^{2})+2\widetilde{\gamma}\cosh(\gamma\varrho^{2})\right]^{2}}.

we get

\displaystyle\int_{0}^{t}\mathrm{d}s\int_{0}^{+\infty}{\cal b}^{(p,0)}_{n,+}(s,\varrho)f(\varrho)\frac{\sinh(\gamma\varrho^{2})\left[(1+4\widetilde{\gamma}^{2})\sinh(\gamma\varrho^{2})+4\widetilde{\gamma}\cosh(\gamma\varrho^{2})\right]}{\left[\sinh(\gamma\varrho^{2})+2\widetilde{\gamma}\cosh(\gamma\varrho^{2})\right]^{2}}\mathrm{d}\varrho

(10.21)

\displaystyle=\int_{0}^{+\infty}\Bigg(tT_{L}-\sum_{\ell=0}^{+\infty}\frac{\gamma^{2}\varrho^{4}c_{\ell}(0)\widehat{\mathfrak{E}}_{n}(t,\ell)}{(\ell\pi)^{2}+\gamma^{2}\varrho^{4}}\Bigg)\frac{\sqrt{2}f(\varrho)}{1+2\tilde{\gamma}\coth(\gamma\varrho^{2})}\mathrm{d}\varrho

\displaystyle-\int_{0}^{+\infty}\Bigg(tT_{R}-\sum_{\ell=0}^{+\infty}\frac{\gamma^{2}\varrho^{4}c_{\ell}(1)\widehat{\mathfrak{E}}_{n}(t,\ell)}{(\ell\pi)^{2}+\gamma^{2}\varrho^{4}}\Bigg)\frac{\sqrt{2}}{1+2\tilde{\gamma}\coth(\gamma\varrho^{2})}\cdot\frac{2\tilde{\gamma}f(\varrho)}{\sinh(\gamma\varrho^{2})+2\tilde{\gamma}\cosh(\gamma\varrho^{2})}\mathrm{d}\varrho+o_{n}(1).

Thus (
9.11
) follows.∎

11.
The time-coboundary terms

We prove here Proposition
6.3
.
Suppose that
\kappa\in(0,1)
.
Using the rapid decay of
|\widehat{(\varphi^{\prime})_{o}}(\ell)|
and (
8.3
), we can write

(11.1)

\displaystyle\frac{1}{n+1}\xi^{(pr)}_{\iota}(\varphi^{\prime};n)=\bar{\xi}^{(pr)}_{\iota}(n)+o_{n}(1),\quad\iota=p,r,\quad\mbox{and}

\displaystyle\frac{1}{n}\big(\xi^{(pr)}_{rp}(\varphi^{\prime};n)+\xi^{(pr)}_{pr}(\varphi^{\prime};n)\big)=\bar{\xi}^{(pr)}_{pr}(n)+o_{n}(1),

where

(11.2)

\bar{\xi}^{(pr)}_{\iota}(n)=\sum_{|\ell|\leq n^{\kappa}}\widehat{(\varphi^{\prime})_{o}}(\ell)\pi\ell\bar{\xi}^{(pr)}_{\iota}(\ell;n)

and

(11.3)

\begin{split}&\bar{\xi}^{(pr)}_{p}(\ell;n)=\frac{1}{2n(n+1)^{2}}\sum_{j^{\prime}=-n-1}^{n}\frac{\cos^{2}\Big(\frac{\pi k_{j^{\prime}}}{2}\Big)\delta_{0,t}\tilde{S}_{j^{\prime}+\ell,j^{\prime}}^{(p)}}{\Delta^{\prime}(\ell,k_{j^{\prime}})}\\
&\bar{\xi}^{(pr)}_{pr}(\ell;n)=\frac{\gamma}{2^{1/2}n(n+1)}\sum_{j^{\prime}=-n-1}^{n}\frac{\sin\Big(\frac{\pi k_{j^{\prime}}}{2}\Big)\sin\Big(\frac{\pi k_{j^{\prime}}}{2}\Big)\delta_{0,t}\tilde{S}_{j^{\prime}+\ell,j^{\prime}}^{(p,r)}}{\Delta^{\prime}(\ell,k_{j^{\prime}})},\\
&\bar{\xi}^{(pr)}_{r}(\ell;n)=\frac{1}{2^{5/2}(n+1)^{3}}\mathop{\sum_{j^{\prime}=-n-1}^{n}}_{j^{\prime}\not=0,-\ell}\frac{\cos\Big(\frac{\pi(k_{j^{\prime}}+k_{\ell})}{2}\Big)\sin\Big(\frac{\pi(2k_{j^{\prime}}+k_{\ell})}{2}\Big)\delta_{0,t}\tilde{S}_{j^{\prime}+\ell,j^{\prime}}^{(r)}}{\sin\Big(\frac{\pi(k_{j^{\prime}}+k_{\ell})}{2}\Big)\Delta^{\prime}(\ell,k_{j^{\prime}})},\\
&\mbox{and}\quad\Delta^{\prime}(\ell,k):=\Big(\frac{\ell\pi}{n+1}\Big)^{2}\cos^{2}\Big(\frac{\pi k}{2}\Big)+2^{4}\gamma^{2}\sin^{4}\Big(\frac{\pi k}{2}\Big).\end{split}

We claim that for each
\iota=p,pr,r
we have

(11.4)

\bar{\xi}^{(pr)}_{\iota}(n)=o_{n}(1),\quad\mbox{as}\quad n\to+\infty.

We show (
11.4
) for
\iota=p
. The
arguments in the remaining cases are similar.
It suffices only to prove that

(11.5)

\bar{\xi}^{(pr)}_{p}(\ell;n)=o_{n}(1),\quad\mbox{as}\quad n\to+\infty.

for each
\ell\not=0
. We can write

\bar{\xi}^{(pr)}_{p}(\ell;n)=\bar{\xi}^{(pr)}_{p,\leq}(\ell;n)+\bar{\xi}^{(pr)}_{p,>}(\ell;n)
, where
the terms on the right correspond to the summation over
|j^{\prime}|\leq(n+1)^{3/5}
and
(n+1)^{3/5}<|j^{\prime}|\leq(n+1)
, respectively. The term
\bar{\xi}^{(pr)}_{p,>}(\ell;n)
can
be estimated by

\displaystyle|\bar{\xi}^{(pr)}_{p,>}(\ell;n)|\leq Cn\sum_{(n+1)^{3/5}<|j^{\prime}|\leq n+1}\frac{|\delta_{0,t}\tilde{S}_{j^{\prime}+\ell,j^{\prime}}^{(p)}|}{(j^{\prime})^{4}}.

Using estimate (
5.26
) we get

(11.6)

\displaystyle|\bar{\xi}^{(pr)}_{p,>}(\ell,n)|\leq Cn^{3/2}\sum_{(n+1)^{3/5}<|j^{\prime}|\leq n+1}\frac{1}{(j^{\prime})^{4}}\leq\frac{C}{n^{3/10}}\to 0,

as
n\to+\infty
.

Concerning
\bar{\xi}^{(pr)}_{p,\leq}(\ell,n)
, we write
|\bar{\xi}^{(pr)}_{p,\leq}(\ell,n)|\leq I_{n}(t)+I_{n}(0)
, where

\displaystyle I_{n}(t):=\frac{C}{n}\sum_{|j^{\prime}|\leq(n+1)^{3/5}}\frac{\mathbb{E}_{n}\tilde{p}_{j^{\prime}}^{2}(t)}{(\ell\pi)^{2}+\varrho_{j^{\prime}}^{4}}.

We can write

\displaystyle I_{n}(t)=\frac{C}{n}\sum_{|j^{\prime}|\leq(n+1)^{3/5}}\sum_{y,y^{\prime}=0}^{n}\mathbb{E}_{n}\big[p_{y}(t)p_{y^{\prime}}(t)\big]\frac{\psi_{j^{\prime}}(y)\psi_{j^{\prime}}(y^{\prime})}{(\ell\pi)^{2}+\varrho_{j^{\prime}}^{4}}\leq I_{n,+}(t)+I_{n,-}(t)

where

\displaystyle I_{n,\pm}(t):=\frac{C}{(n+1)^{2}}\Big|\sum_{y,y^{\prime}=0}^{n}\mathbb{E}_{n}\big[p_{y}(t)p_{y^{\prime}}(t)\big]\sum_{|j^{\prime}|\leq(n+1)^{3/5}}\frac{\exp\{i\pi j^{\prime}(u_{y}\pm u_{y^{\prime}})\}}{(\ell\pi)^{2}+\varrho_{j^{\prime}}^{4}}\Big|,

with
\varrho_{j^{\prime}}=j^{\prime}/(n+1)^{1/2}
.
Furthermore,
I_{n,-}(t)=I_{n,-,\leq}(t)+I_{n,-,>}(t)
, where the terms correspond to the
summation over
|y-y^{\prime}|\leq(n+1)^{1/4}
and

|y-y^{\prime}|>(n+1)^{1/4}
, respectively.
Then,

\displaystyle|I_{n,-,\leq}(t)|\leq\frac{C}{(n+1)^{2}}\mathop{\sum_{y,y^{\prime}=0}^{n}}_{|y-y^{\prime}|\leq(n+1)^{1/4}}\Big(\mathbb{E}_{n}\big[p_{y}^{2}(t)\big]+\mathbb{E}_{n}\big[p_{y^{\prime}}^{2}(t)\big]\Big)

\displaystyle\times\sum_{|j^{\prime}|\leq(n+1)^{3/5}}\frac{1}{(\ell\pi)^{2}+\varrho_{j^{\prime}}^{4}}\leq\frac{C}{(n+1)^{3/2}}\int_{\mathbb{R}}\frac{\mathrm{d}\varrho}{(\ell\pi)^{2}+\varrho^{4}}

\displaystyle\times\mathop{\sum_{y,y^{\prime}=0}^{n}}_{|y-y^{\prime}|\leq(n+1)^{1/4}}\Big(\mathbb{E}_{n}\big[p_{y}^{2}(t)\big]+\mathbb{E}_{n}\big[p_{y^{\prime}}^{2}(t)\big]\Big)\leq\frac{C}{n^{1/4}}\to 0,\quad\mbox{as $n\to+\infty$.}

In the penultimate estimate we have used bound (
4.2
).

On the other hand

\displaystyle I_{n,-,>}(t)=\frac{C}{(n+1)^{2}}\Big|\mathop{\sum_{y,y^{\prime}=0}^{n}}_{|y-y^{\prime}|>(n+1)^{1/4}}\frac{\mathbb{E}_{n}\big[p_{y}(t)p_{y^{\prime}}(t)\big]}{e^{i\pi(u_{y}-u_{y^{\prime}})}-1}\sum_{|j^{\prime}|\leq(n+1)^{3/5}}\frac{\nabla_{j^{\prime}}\exp\{i\pi{j^{\prime}}(u_{y}-u_{y^{\prime}})\}}{(\ell\pi)^{2}+\varrho_{j^{\prime}}^{4}}\Big|.

Denote by
m_{n}
(
M_{n}
) the smallest (resp. largest) integer larger
(resp. smaller) than
-(n+1)^{3/5}
(resp.
(n+1)^{3/5}
). Summing by
parts in
j^{\prime}
we can write

\displaystyle I_{n,-,>}(t)=|I_{n,-,>}^{(b)}(t)+I_{n,-,>}^{(a)}(t;M_{n})-I_{n,-,>}^{(a)}(t;m_{n})|,\quad\mbox{where}

\displaystyle I_{n,-,>}^{(a)}(t;m):=\frac{C}{(n+1)^{2}}\mathop{\sum_{y,y^{\prime}=0}^{n}}_{|y-y^{\prime}|>(n+1)^{1/4}}\frac{\mathbb{E}_{n}\big[p_{y}(t)p_{y^{\prime}}(t)\big]e^{i\pi m(u_{y}-u_{y^{\prime}})}}{\Big[(\ell\pi)^{2}+\varrho_{m}^{4}\Big]\Big[e^{i\pi(u_{y}-u_{y^{\prime}})}-1\Big]}

\displaystyle I_{n,-,>}^{(b)}(t):=-\frac{C}{(n+1)^{2}}\mathop{\sum_{y,y^{\prime}=0}^{n}}_{|y-y^{\prime}|>(n+1)^{1/4}}\sum_{j^{\prime}=m_{n}+1}^{M_{n}}\frac{\mathbb{E}_{n}\big[p_{y}(t)p_{y^{\prime}}(t)\big]e^{i\pi j^{\prime}(u_{y}-u_{y^{\prime}})}}{e^{i\pi(u_{y}-u_{y^{\prime}})}-1}

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\times\nabla_{j^{\prime}}^{\star}\Bigg(\frac{1}{(\ell\pi)^{2}+\varrho_{j^{\prime}}^{4}}\Bigg).

By the Cauchy-Schwarz inequality and the estimates in Corollary
5.1

we get

\displaystyle\mathop{\sum_{y,y^{\prime}=0}^{n}}_{|y-y^{\prime}|>(n+1)^{1/4}}\frac{|\mathbb{E}_{n}\big[p_{y}(t)p_{y^{\prime}}(t)\big]|}{|y-y^{\prime}|}

\displaystyle\leq\left\{\mathop{\sum_{y,y^{\prime}=0}^{n}}_{|y-y^{\prime}|>(n+1)^{1/4}}\Big[\mathbb{E}_{n}\big[p_{y}(t)p_{y^{\prime}}(t)\big]\Big]^{2}\right\}^{1/2}

(11.7)

\displaystyle\times\left\{\mathop{\sum_{y,y^{\prime}=0}^{n}}_{|y-y^{\prime}|>(n+1)^{1/4}}\frac{1}{(|y-y^{\prime}|+1)^{2}}\right\}^{1/2}\leq Cn^{7/8}.

Hence,

|I_{n,-,>}^{(a)}(t;m)|\leq C\frac{n^{7/8}}{n}=\frac{C}{n^{1/8}}\to 0,\quad\mbox{as $n\to+\infty$. }

Using an estimate

\Bigg|\nabla_{j^{\prime}}^{\star}\Bigg(\frac{1}{(\ell\pi)^{2}+\varrho_{j^{\prime}}^{4}}\Bigg)\Bigg|\leq\frac{C}{(n+1)^{1/2}}\cdot\frac{|\varrho_{j^{\prime}}|^{3}}{[(\ell\pi)^{2}+\varrho_{j^{\prime}}^{4}]^{2}}

we can write

\displaystyle|I_{n,-,\geq}^{(b)}(t)|\leq\frac{C}{n+1}\int_{\mathbb{R}}\frac{|\varrho|^{3}\mathrm{d}\varrho}{[(\ell\pi)^{2}+\varrho^{4}]^{2}}\mathop{\sum_{y,y^{\prime}=0}^{n}}_{|y-y^{\prime}|>(n+1)^{1/4}}\frac{|\mathbb{E}_{n}\big[p_{y}(t)p_{y^{\prime}}(t)\big]|}{|y-y^{\prime}|}.

Invoking (
11.7
) we conclude that

\displaystyle|I_{n,-,\geq}^{(b)}(t)|\leq\frac{Cn^{7/8}}{n}=\frac{C}{n^{1/8}}\to 0.

as
n\to+\infty
. We have shown therefore that
I_{n,-}(t)=o_{n}(1)
.
Likewise we can show that

I_{n,+}(t)=o_{n}(1).

These facts together imply that

I_{n}(t;\ell)\to 0.

Hence,

\bar{\xi}^{(pr)}_{p,\leq}(n)=o_{n}(1)
and in consequence (
11.5
) follows.∎

12.
Proofs of some
technical results

12.1.
Equivalence of some kinetic energy functionals

Proposition 12.1
.

For any
t>0
there exists a constant
C>0
such that

(12.1)

\frac{1}{n}\sum_{x=0}^{n-1}\int_{0}^{t}\big(\mathbb{E}_{n}\left[p_{x+1}(s)p_{x}(s)\right]\big)^{2}\mathrm{d}s\leq\frac{C}{n^{1/2}}.

Proof.

From the Cauchy-Schwarz inequality we have

\displaystyle\left(\mathbb{E}_{n}\left[p_{x+1}(s)\big(p_{x}(s)-p_{0}(s)\big)\right]\right)^{2}\leq n\sum_{x^{\prime}=1}^{x}\left(\mathbb{E}_{n}\left[p_{x+1}(s)\nabla^{\star}p_{x^{\prime}}(s)\right]\right)^{2}.

We also have

\displaystyle\sum_{x=0}^{n-1}\int_{0}^{t}\left(\mathbb{E}_{n}\left[p_{x+1}(s)p_{x}(s)\right]\right)^{2}\mathrm{d}s

\displaystyle\leq 2\sum_{x=0}^{n-1}\int_{0}^{t}\left(\mathbb{E}_{n}\left[p_{x+1}(s)\big(p_{x}(s)-p_{0}(s)\big)\right]\right)^{2}\mathrm{d}s+2\sum_{x=0}^{n-1}\int_{0}^{t}\left(\mathbb{E}_{n}\left[p_{x+1}(s)p_{0}(s)\right]\right)^{2}\mathrm{d}s

\displaystyle\leq 2n\sum_{x=0}^{n-1}\sum_{x^{\prime}=1}^{x}\int_{0}^{t}\left(\mathbb{E}_{n}\left[p_{x+1}(s)\nabla^{\star}p_{x^{\prime}}(s)\right]\right)^{2}\mathrm{d}s+2\sum_{x=0}^{n-1}\int_{0}^{t}\left(\mathbb{E}_{n}\left[p_{x+1}(s)p_{0}(s)\right]\right)^{2}\mathrm{d}s.

From (
4.3
) we conclude that
the right hand side can be estimated by

Cn^{1/2}
.∎

As a direct application of Proposition
12.1

and estimate (
4.1
) we conclude the following.

Corollary 12.2
.

For any
t>0
there exists a constant
C>0
such that

(12.2)

\frac{1}{n+1}\sum_{x=0}^{n}\int_{0}^{t}\big\{\mathbb{E}_{n}\left[(\nabla p_{x}(s))^{2}\right]-2\mathbb{E}_{n}\left[p^{2}_{x}(s)\right]\big\}^{2}\mathrm{d}s\leq\frac{C}{n^{1/2}}.

12.2.
Equipartition property

Theorem 12.3

(Equipartition property)
.

For any compactly supported,
continuous function
\Phi:[0,+\infty)\times[0,1]\to\mathbb{R}

(12.3)

\lim_{n\to+\infty}\frac{1}{n}\sum_{x=0}^{n}\int_{0}^{+\infty}\Phi\left(t,\frac{x}{n}\right)\Big\{\mathbb{E}_{n}\big[p_{x}^{2}(t)\big]-\mathbb{E}_{n}\big[r_{x}^{2}(t)\big]\Big\}\mathrm{d}t=0.

Proof.

By an approximation it suffices to show that for any

\varphi\in C^{1}_{c}(0,1)
and
t>0
:

(12.4)

\lim_{n\to+\infty}\frac{1}{n}\sum_{x=0}^{n}\int_{0}^{t}\varphi_{x}\Big\{\mathbb{E}_{n}\big[p_{x}^{2}(s)\big]-\mathbb{E}_{n}\big[r_{x}^{2}(s)\big]\Big\}\mathrm{d}s=0,

where
\varphi_{x}=\varphi\left(\frac{x}{n}\right)
.
Define the position functional by letting

(12.5)

q_{x}=\sum_{y=1}^{x}r_{y},\quad x=1,\ldots n,\qquad\text{and}\qquad q_{0}=0.

Then, cf (
2.5
), remembering that
\varphi_{0}=\varphi_{n}=0
, we get

\begin{split}&\mathcal{G}\bigg(\frac{1}{n}\sum_{x=0}^{n}\varphi_{x}p_{x}q_{x}\bigg)=\frac{1}{n}\sum_{x=0}^{n}\varphi_{x}\Big(p_{x}^{2}-p_{x}p_{0}+q_{x}\nabla r_{x}+\gamma q_{x}\Delta p_{x}\Big).\end{split}

We now use the identity
\nabla(q_{x}r_{x})=r_{x+1}^{2}+q_{x}\nabla r_{x}
,
valid for
x=1,\dots,n-1
.
Summing by parts we obtain

\begin{split}&\frac{1}{n}\sum_{x=0}^{n}\varphi_{x}\int_{0}^{t}\left[\mathbb{E}_{n}p_{x}^{2}(s)-\mathbb{E}_{n}r_{x+1}^{2}(s)\right]\mathrm{d}s={\rm I}_{n}+{\rm II}_{n}+{\rm III}_{n},\quad\mbox{where}\\
&{\rm I}_{n}=\frac{1}{n}\sum_{x=0}^{n}(\nabla^{*}\varphi_{x})\int_{0}^{t}\mathbb{E}_{n}\big[q_{x}(s)r_{x}(s)\big]\mathrm{d}s\\
&{\rm II}_{n}=\frac{1}{n}\sum_{x=0}^{n}\varphi_{x}\int_{0}^{t}\mathrm{d}s\Big\{\mathbb{E}_{n}\big[p_{x}(s)p_{0}(s)\big]-\gamma\mathbb{E}_{n}\big[q_{x}(s)\Delta p_{x}(s)\big]\Big\}\\
&{\rm III}_{n}=\frac{1}{n^{5/2}}\sum_{x=0}^{n}\varphi_{x}\Big\{\mathbb{E}_{n}\big[p_{x}(t)q_{x}(t)\big]-\mathbb{E}_{n}\big[p_{x}(0)q_{x}(0)\big]\Big\}.\end{split}

By the Cauchy-Schwarz inequality and (
2.27
) we have that

{\rm III}_{n}\leq\frac{C}{n^{3/2}}\left(\mathcal{H}_{n}(t)+\mathcal{H}_{n}(0)\right)\leq\frac{C}{n^{1/2}}.

We have by (
4.3
)

\begin{split}|{\rm I}_{n}|&\leq\frac{\|\varphi^{\prime}\|_{\infty}}{n^{2}}\sum_{x,y=0}^{n}\Big|\int_{0}^{t}\mathbb{E}_{n}\big[r_{x}(s)r_{y}(s)\big]\mathrm{d}s\Big|\\
&\leq\frac{C}{n}\Big\{\sum_{x,y=0}^{n}\int_{0}^{t}\Big[\mathbb{E}_{n}\big[r_{x}(s)r_{y}(s)\big]\Big]^{2}\mathrm{d}s\Big\}^{1/2}\leq\frac{C}{n^{1/2}}.\end{split}

Finally, again from (
4.3
), we conclude that

\displaystyle\frac{1}{n}\sum_{x=0}^{n}\Big|\int_{0}^{t}\mathbb{E}_{n}\big[p_{x}(s)p_{0}(s)\big]\mathrm{d}s\Big|\leq\frac{1}{n^{1/2}}\left\{\sum_{x=0}^{n}\Big[\int_{0}^{t}\mathbb{E}_{n}\big[p_{x}(s)p_{0}(s)\big]\mathrm{d}s\Big]^{2}\right\}^{1/2}\leq\frac{C}{n^{3/4}}

and

\displaystyle\frac{1}{n}\sum_{x=0}^{n}\Big|\int_{0}^{t}\mathbb{E}_{n}\big[q_{x}(s)\Delta p_{x}(s)\big]\mathrm{d}s\Big|\leq\frac{C}{n}\sum_{x,x^{\prime}=0}^{n}\Big|\int_{0}^{t}\mathbb{E}_{n}\big[r_{x^{\prime}}(s)\nabla p_{x}(s)\big]\mathrm{d}s\Big|

\displaystyle\leq C\left\{\sum_{x,x^{\prime}=0}^{n}\Big[\int_{0}^{t}\mathbb{E}_{n}\big[r_{x^{\prime}}(s)\nabla p_{x}(s)\big]\mathrm{d}s\Big]^{2}\right\}^{1/2}\leq\frac{C}{n^{1/4}}.

In conclusion
|{\rm II}_{n}|\leq C/n^{1/4}
and the theorem has been
proved.∎

Define

(12.6)

\hat{\cal E}_{n}(t,\ell):=\frac{1}{n+1}\sum_{x=0}^{n}\mathbb{E}_{n}\left[{\cal E}_{x}(t)\right]c_{\ell}(u_{x}),\quad\ell=0,1,\ldots.

Combining the results of Corollary
12.2
and Theorem

12.3
we conclude the following.

Corollary 12.4
.

For any
t>0
and
\varphi\in C^{\infty}_{c}(0,1)
we have

(12.7)

\lim_{n\to+\infty}\frac{1}{n+1}\sum_{x=0}^{n}\varphi\Big(\frac{x}{n}\Big)\int_{0}^{t}\Big\{\mathbb{E}_{n}\left[(\nabla p_{x}(s))^{2}\right]-2\mathbb{E}_{n}{\cal E}_{x}(s)\Big\}\mathrm{d}s=0

and (cf (
8.21
)) in consequence

(12.8)

\lim_{n\to\infty}\sum_{\ell=0}^{+\infty}\hat{\varphi}_{c}(\ell)\left[\int_{0}^{t}\hat{\cal E}_{n}(s,\ell)\mathrm{d}s-\hat{\mathfrak{E}}_{n}(t,\ell)\right]=0.

12.3.
Estimates of the
gradient of the kinetic energy

Proposition 12.5
.

For any
t_{*}>0
there exists
C>0
s such that

(12.9)

\frac{1}{n}\sum_{x=1}^{n}\int_{0}^{t}\Big\{\mathbb{E}_{n}\big[\nabla^{\star}\big(p_{x}p_{x+1}\big)(s)\big]\Big\}^{2}\mathrm{d}s\leq\frac{C}{n^{3/2}},\quad n=1,2,\ldots,\quad t\in[0,t_{*}].

Proof.

Since

\nabla^{\star}\big(p_{x}p_{x+1}\big)=p_{x+1}\nabla^{\star}p_{x}+p_{x-1}\nabla p_{x+1}

the left hand side of (
12.9
) can be estimated by

\displaystyle\frac{2}{n}\sum_{x=1}^{n}\int_{0}^{t}\left\{\mathbb{E}_{n}\left[\big(\nabla^{\star}p_{x}(s)\big)p_{x+1}(s)\right]\right\}^{2}\mathrm{d}s+\frac{2}{n}\sum_{x=1}^{n}\int_{0}^{t}\left\{\mathbb{E}_{n}\left[\big(\nabla p_{x}(s)\big)p_{x-1}(s)\right]\right\}^{2}\mathrm{d}s\leq\frac{C}{n^{3/2}},

by virtue of (
4.3
).
∎

12.4.
Proof of Lemma
8.3

12.4.1.
Proof of (
8.22
)

We write

\displaystyle\hat{\mathfrak{E}}_{n}(t,\ell)=2{\mathfrak{E}}_{\rm kin}(t,\ell)+{\mathfrak{E}}_{\rm cor}(t,\ell)+\widehat{\cal R}_{n}(t,\ell)\quad\mbox{where}

\displaystyle{\mathfrak{E}}_{\rm kin}(t,\ell):=\frac{1}{2(n+1)}\sum_{y=0}^{n}c_{\ell}(u_{y})\big\langle\!\big\langle p_{y}^{2}\big\rangle\!\big\rangle_{t},

(12.10)

\displaystyle{\mathfrak{E}}_{\rm cor}(t,\ell):=-\frac{1}{n+1}\sum_{y=1}^{n}c_{\ell}(u_{y})\big\langle\!\big\langle p_{y}p_{y-1}\big\rangle\!\big\rangle_{t}

\displaystyle\widehat{\cal R}_{n}(t,\ell):=-\frac{1}{2(n+1)}\Big(\big\langle\!\big\langle p_{0}^{2}\big\rangle\!\big\rangle_{t}+c_{\ell}(u_{y})\big\langle\!\big\langle p_{n}^{2}\big\rangle\!\big\rangle_{t}\Big).

Thanks to Proposition
4.1
we have
|\widehat{\cal R}_{n}(t,\ell)|\leq C/n
.
By a direct calculation we conclude the following.

Lemma 12.6
.

For any sequence
(a_{y})
of real numbers and

\ell\in\mathbb{Z}
we have

(12.11)

\begin{split}&2\sum_{y=0}^{n}a_{y}\cos(\pi\ell u_{y})=a_{0}+(-1)^{\ell+1}a_{n}-\sum_{y=1}^{n}\frac{\sin\big(\pi k_{\ell}(y-1/2)\big)}{\sin\big(\frac{\pi k_{\ell}}{2}\big)}\nabla^{\star}a_{y}.\end{split}

Using formula (
12.11
) we can write

\displaystyle 2{\cal E}_{\rm kin}(t,\ell)=\bar{\rm E}_{n}^{(0)}(t,\ell)+{\rm r}_{n}^{(0)}(t,\ell),\quad{\cal E}_{\rm cor}(t,\ell)=\bar{\rm E}_{n}^{(1)}(t,\ell)+{\rm r}_{n}^{(1)}(t,\ell),

where

\displaystyle\bar{\rm E}_{n}^{(0)}(t,\ell):=-\frac{1}{2(n+1)}\sum_{y=1}^{n}\frac{\sin\big(\pi k_{\ell}(y-1/2)\big)}{\sin\big(\frac{\pi k_{\ell}}{2}\big)}\nabla^{\star}\big\langle\!\big\langle p_{y}^{2}\big\rangle\!\big\rangle_{t},

\displaystyle{\rm r}_{n}^{(0)}(t,\ell):=\frac{1}{2(n+1)}\Big(\big\langle\!\big\langle p_{0}^{2}\big\rangle\!\big\rangle_{t}+(-1)^{\ell+1}\big\langle\!\big\langle p_{n}^{2}\big\rangle\!\big\rangle_{t}\Big),

\displaystyle\bar{\rm E}_{n}^{(1)}(t,\ell):=-\frac{1}{2(n+1)}\sum_{y=2}^{n}\frac{\sin\big(\pi k_{\ell}(y-1/2)\big)}{\sin\big(\frac{\pi k_{\ell}}{2}\big)}\nabla^{\star}\big\langle\!\big\langle p_{y}p_{y-1}\big\rangle\!\big\rangle_{t},

\displaystyle{\rm r}_{n}^{(1)}(t,\ell):=-\frac{1}{2(n+1)}\Big(\big\langle\!\big\langle p_{1}p_{0}\big\rangle\!\big\rangle_{t}+{(-1)^{\ell}}\big\langle\!\big\langle p_{n}p_{n-1}\big\rangle\!\big\rangle_{t}\Big).

From Proposition
4.1
we conclude

\displaystyle\Big|{\rm r}_{n}^{(m)}(t,\ell)\Big|\leq\frac{C}{n},\quad|\ell|\leq n+1,\,m=0,1.

Using trigonometric identities we can verify that

\displaystyle 2\sum_{y=0}^{n}\sin\big(\pi k_{\ell}(y+1/2)\big)\sin\big(\pi k_{\ell^{\prime}}(y+1/2)\big)=(n+1)\delta_{\ell,\ell^{\prime}}-(n+1)\delta_{\ell,0}\delta_{\ell^{\prime},0}.

In consequence

\displaystyle\sum_{y=0}^{n}\chi_{\ell}(y)\chi_{\ell^{\prime}}(y)=\delta_{\ell,\ell^{\prime}},\quad\ell,\ell^{\prime}=1,\ldots,n,

where

\chi_{\ell}(y):=\left(\frac{2}{n+1}\right)^{1/2}\sin\big(\pi k_{\ell}(y+1/2)\big).

We can write therefore

\displaystyle\bar{\rm E}_{n}^{(0)}(t,\ell)=-\frac{1}{2^{3/2}(n+1)^{1/2}\sin\Big(\frac{\pi k_{\ell}}{2}\Big)}\sum_{y=0}^{n}\chi_{\ell}(y)\nabla^{\star}\big\langle\!\big\langle p_{y+1}^{2}\big\rangle\!\big\rangle_{t},

where, by a convention,
\nabla^{\star}\mathbb{E}_{n}[p_{n+1}^{2}(s)]=0
.
By virtue of (
4.3
) we have

\displaystyle|\bar{\rm E}_{n}^{(0)}(t,\ell)|\leq\frac{C(n+1)^{1/2}}{|\ell|}\left\{\sum_{y=0}^{n}\Big[\nabla^{\star}\big\langle\!\big\langle p_{y+1}^{2}\big\rangle\!\big\rangle_{t}\Big]^{2}\right\}^{1/2}\leq C\frac{(n+1)^{1/4}}{|\ell|}.

Using the same argument and estimate
(
12.9
) we conclude also that

\displaystyle|\bar{\rm E}_{n}^{(1)}(t,\ell)|\leq\frac{C(n+1)^{1/2}}{|\ell|}\left\{\sum_{y=2}^{n}\Big[\nabla^{\star}\big\langle\!\big\langle p_{y}p_{y-1}\big\rangle\!\big\rangle_{t}\Big]^{2}\right\}^{1/2}\leq C\frac{(n+1)^{1/4}}{|\ell|}.

This concludes the proof of
(
8.22
).

12.4.2.
Proof of (
8.23
)

We prove that there exists
C>0

(12.12)

\sum_{\ell=-n}^{n}\frac{1}{|\ell|+1}\Big|\hat{\mathfrak{E}}_{n}(t,\ell)\Big|\leq C\,

for
n=1,2,\ldots.
Using the Cauchy-Schwarz
inequality
we can estimate the left hand side of
(
12.12
) by

(12.13)

\begin{split}&\frac{C}{n+1}\left(\sum_{\ell=-n}^{n}\frac{1}{(|\ell|+1)^{2}}\right)^{1/2}\left\{\sum_{y,y^{\prime}=0}^{n}\big\langle\!\big\langle p_{y}^{2}\big\rangle\!\big\rangle_{t}\big\langle\!\big\langle p_{y^{\prime}}^{2}\big\rangle\!\big\rangle_{t}\sum_{\ell=-n}^{n}\cos\left(\pi\ell u_{y}\right)\cos\left(\pi\ell u_{y^{\prime}}\right)\right\}^{1/2}.\end{split}

Recalling an elementary trigonometric identity

\displaystyle\sum_{\ell=-n}^{n}\cos\left(\pi\ell u_{y}\right)\cos\left(\pi\ell u_{y^{\prime}}\right)=(2n+1)\Big[\cos\left(n\pi(u_{y}+u_{y^{\prime}})\right)1_{\mathbb{Z}}\Big(\frac{u_{y}+u_{y}^{\prime}}{2}\Big)

(12.14)

\displaystyle+\cos\left(n\pi(u_{y}-u_{y^{\prime}})\right)1_{\mathbb{Z}}\Big(\frac{u_{y}-u_{y}^{\prime}}{2}\Big)\Big]

we conclude that the expression in
(
12.13
) can be estimated by

(12.15)

\begin{split}&\frac{C}{(n+1)^{1/2}}\Big\{\sum_{y=0}^{n}\Big(\big\langle\!\big\langle p_{y}^{2}\big\rangle\!\big\rangle_{t}\Big)^{2}\Big\}^{1/2}\leq C,\end{split}

by virtue of (
4.3
). This ends the proof of (
12.12
).
∎

13.
Proof of energy bounds for arbitrary
T_{L},T_{R}

The main purpose of the present section is to provide the
proof of energy bounds given in Section
2.4

without the assumption that the temperatures
T_{L}
,
T_{R}
of both heat baths at the
end of the chain are equal, used in Section
4
. We shall also show the estimate of the
current given in Theorem
2.9
.

13.1.
Proof of Theorem
2.7

To indicate the dependence of the solution (
5.3
) of
(
5.4
) on the temperature of the heat baths we denote it in the present
section by

{\bf X}(t;T_{L},T_{R})=\left(\begin{array}[]{c}{\bf r}(t;T_{L},T_{R})\\
\\
{\bf p}(t;T_{L},T_{R})\end{array}\right).

Likewise we denote
by
S(t;T_{L},T_{R})
the
the respective covariance matrix, see (
5.8
),

(13.1)

S(t;T_{L},T_{R})=\mathbb{E}_{\mu_{n}}\Big[{\bf X}_{n}(t;T_{L},T_{R})\otimes{\bf X}_{n}(t;T_{L},T_{R})\Big]=\left[\begin{array}[]{cc}{S^{(r)}(t;T_{L},T_{R})}&S^{(r,p)}(t;T_{L},T_{R})\\
S^{(p,r)}(t;T_{L},T_{R})&S^{(p)}(t;T_{L},T_{R})\end{array}\right],

From (
5.12
) we conclude that

(13.2)

\begin{split}&A\big\langle\!\big\langle S(\cdot;T_{L},T_{R})\big\rangle\!\big\rangle_{t}+\big\langle\!\big\langle S(\cdot;T_{L},T_{R})\big\rangle\!\big\rangle_{t}A^{T}+\gamma\Sigma_{2}^{\prime}\Big(\big\langle\!\big\langle\overline{\mathfrak{\bf(\nabla p)}^{2}}(\cdot;T_{L},T_{R})\big\rangle\!\big\rangle_{t}\Big)\\
&+2\tilde{\gamma}\Sigma_{1}^{\prime}(T_{L},T_{R})=\frac{1}{n^{3/2}}\delta_{0,t}S(\cdot;T_{L},T_{R}),\end{split}

where
A
is given by (
5.5
),

(13.3)

\begin{split}\Sigma_{1}^{\prime}(T_{L};T_{R})=\left[\begin{array}[]{cc}0_{n,n}&0_{n,n+1}\\
0_{n+1,n}&D_{1}(T_{L};T_{R})\end{array}\right],\quad\Sigma_{2}({\bf x})=\left[\begin{array}[]{cc}0_{n,n}&0_{n,n+1}\\
0_{n+1,n}&D_{2}({\bf x})\end{array}\right],\end{split}

D_{1}(T_{L};T_{R})=[T_{L}\delta_{x,0}\delta_{0,y}+T_{R}\delta_{x,n}\delta_{0,n}]_{x,y=0,\ldots,n}
,

D_{2}({\bf x})
is defined in (
5.11
)
and

\overline{\mathfrak{\bf(\nabla p)}^{2}}(s;T_{L},T_{R})=\Big[\mathbb{E}_{n}\big(\nabla^{\star}p_{1}(s;T_{L},T_{R})\big)^{2},\ldots,\mathbb{E}_{n}\big(\nabla^{\star}p_{n}(s;T_{L},T_{R})\big)^{2}\Big].

Note that

\mathbb{E}_{n}{\bf(\nabla p)}^{2}(t;T_{L},T_{R})=\Gamma S(t;T_{L},T_{R})

for some matrix
\Gamma
.
Differentiating (
13.2
) in
t
we conclude that
S(t;T_{L},T_{R})
satisfies the
following inhomogeneous linear system of differential equations

(13.4)

\begin{split}&\frac{\mathrm{d}S(t;T_{L},T_{R})}{\mathrm{d}t}=-n^{3/2}\big(AS(t;T_{L},T_{R})+S(t;T_{L},T_{R})A^{T}\\
&+\gamma\Sigma_{2}^{\prime}\Gamma S(t;T_{L},T_{R})+2\tilde{\gamma}\Sigma_{1}^{\prime}(T_{L},T_{R}),\\
&S(0;T_{L},T_{R})=\mathbb{E}_{n}\Big[{\bf X}(0;T_{L},T_{R})\otimes{\bf X}(0;T_{L},T_{R})\Big].\end{split}

The following result holds.

Proposition 13.1
.

Suppose that
C
is a covariance matrix of a certain zero mean
random vector
{\bf X}
and
{\bf X}(t)
is the solution of
(
5.4
). Then its covariance matrix
C(t)
, given by
(
13.1
), satisfies (
13.4
), with
C(0)=C
.

Conversely, suppose that
C(t)
satisfies (
13.4
)
with
C(0)
that is a second moment of a certain zero mean random vector

{\bf X}
. Then,
C(t)
is the covariance matrix of
{\bf X}(t)
that is the solution of
(
5.4
) with the law of
{\bf X}(0)
coinciding with

{\rm X}
.

Consider two systems with the dynamics described by
(
5.1
) and (
5.2
): one corresponding to equal
temperatures
T
at both endpoint thermostats and the second
corresponding to
T_{L}
,
T_{R}
and such that

\max\{T_{L},T_{R}\}<T
. Suppose furthermore that they have the
same initial data.
Denote by
{\cal H}_{n}(t;T,T)
and
{\cal H}_{n}(t;T_{L},T_{R})
their
respective energy functionals defined in (
2.26
).

Let
\delta S(t):=S(t;T,T)-S(t;T_{L},T_{R})
. Then

\delta S(t)
satisfies equation (
13.4
) with
\Sigma_{1}^{\prime}(T_{L},T_{R})
replaced by
\Sigma_{1}^{\prime}(T-T_{L},T-T_{R})
. According to
Proposition
13.1
the solution
\delta S(t)
is the matrix of
covariances of the solution of the system
(
5.1
) and (
5.2
), where the temperatures at the left
and right endpoints equal
T-T_{L}
and
T-T_{R}
, respectively. Since the
diagonal elements of
\delta S(t)
are all nonnegative, we conclude that

{\cal H}_{n}(t;T,T)\geq{\cal H}_{n}(t;T_{L},T_{R})
. According to the
already proven energy estimate
in the case when the heat baths are at the same
temperature, see Section
Supplement
, for any
t_{*}>0
there exists
C>0
such that

(13.5)

\sup_{t\in[0,t_{*}]}{\cal H}_{n}(t;T,T)\leq Cn,\quad n=1,2,\ldots

thus the conclusion of Theorem
2.7
holds.∎

13.2.
Estimates of the energy current

Given a function
\beta:[0,1]\to(0,+\infty)
define

(13.6)

J_{n}(t;\beta):=\sum_{x=1}^{n}\beta_{x}\int_{0}^{t}\mathbb{E}_{n}j_{x-1,x}^{(a)}(s)\mathrm{d}s,

where
\beta_{x}=\beta\big(\frac{x}{n+1}\big)
.
We have the following.

Proposition 13.2
.

Suppose that

\beta:[0,1]\to[0,+\infty)
is a
C^{1}
class function. Then, for

z=0
and
z=n+1
we have

(13.7)

\begin{split}&|\int_{0}^{t}\mathbb{E}_{n}j_{z-1,z}(s)\mathrm{d}s|\leq\Big(\sum_{x=1}^{n}\beta_{x}\Big)^{-1}\Bigg\{|J_{n}(t;\beta)|+\|\beta\|_{\infty}(T_{L}+T_{R})t\\
&+\frac{(n+1)\|\beta\|_{\infty}}{n^{3/2}}\big(\mathbb{E}_{n}{\cal H}_{n}(t)+\mathbb{E}_{n}{\cal H}_{n}(0)\big)+\frac{\|\beta\|_{\infty}}{n^{3/2}}\mathbb{E}_{n}{\cal H}_{n}(0)+\frac{\gamma\|\beta\|_{\infty}}{2n}\int_{0}^{t}\mathbb{E}_{n}{\cal H}_{n}(s)\mathrm{d}s\Bigg\}\end{split}

for all
n=1,2,\ldots
and
t\geq 0
. In addition,

(13.8)

\begin{split}&\sup_{x=0,\ldots,n+1}|\int_{0}^{t}\mathbb{E}_{n}j_{x-1,x}(s)\mathrm{d}s|\leq\Big(\sum_{x=1}^{n}\beta_{x}\Big)^{-1}\Bigg\{|J_{n}(t;\beta)|+\|\beta\|_{\infty}(T_{L}+T_{R})t\\
&+\frac{1}{n^{3/2}}\Big[(n+1)\|\beta\|_{\infty}+\sum_{x=1}^{n}\beta_{x}\Big]\big(\mathbb{E}_{n}{\cal H}_{n}(t)+\mathbb{E}_{n}{\cal H}_{n}(0)\big)\\
&+\frac{\|\beta\|_{\infty}}{n^{3/2}}\mathbb{E}{\cal H}_{n}(0)+\frac{\gamma\|\beta^{\prime}\|_{\infty}}{2n}\left|\int_{0}^{t}{\cal H}_{n}(s)\mathrm{d}s\right|\Bigg\}.\end{split}

Proof.

We can obviously write

\displaystyle\Big(\sum_{x=1}^{n}\beta_{x}\Big)\left|\int_{0}^{t}\mathbb{E}_{n}j_{-1,0}(s)\mathrm{d}s\right|\leq I_{1}+I_{2},\quad\mbox{where}

(13.9)

\displaystyle I_{1}:=\left|\int_{0}^{t}\sum_{x=1}^{n}\beta_{x}\mathbb{E}_{n}j_{x-1,x}(s)\mathrm{d}s\right|

\displaystyle I_{2}:=\left|\int_{0}^{t}\sum_{x=1}^{n}\beta_{x}\mathbb{E}_{n}\big[j_{x-1,x}(s)-j_{-1,0}(s)\big]\mathrm{d}s\right|.

We have

\displaystyle I_{1}\leq|J_{n}(t;\beta)|+R_{1},\quad\mbox{where}

\displaystyle R_{1}:=\frac{\gamma}{2}\left|\int_{0}^{t}\sum_{x=0}^{n-1}\beta_{x+1}\mathbb{E}_{n}\nabla p_{x}^{2}(s)\mathrm{d}s\right|.

Summing by parts and using the fact that
\beta
is of
C^{1}

class we obtain

\displaystyle R_{1}\leq\frac{\gamma}{2}\left|\int_{0}^{t}\sum_{x=1}^{n-1}\nabla^{\star}\beta_{x+1}\mathbb{E}_{n}p_{x}^{2}(s)\mathrm{d}s\right|+\beta_{1}\int_{0}^{t}\mathbb{E}_{n}p_{0}^{2}(s)\mathrm{d}s+\beta_{n}\int_{0}^{t}\mathbb{E}_{n}p_{n}^{2}(s)\mathrm{d}s

\displaystyle\leq\frac{\gamma\|\beta^{\prime}\|_{\infty}}{n}\int_{0}^{t}\mathbb{E}_{n}{\cal H}_{n}(s)\mathrm{d}s+\|\beta\|_{\infty}\left(\int_{0}^{t}\mathbb{E}_{n}p_{0}^{2}(s)\mathrm{d}s+\int_{0}^{t}\mathbb{E}_{n}p_{n}^{2}(s)\mathrm{d}s\right).

Applying estimate (
2.35
)
we end up with

(13.10)

\displaystyle R_{1}\leq\frac{\gamma\|\beta^{\prime}\|_{\infty}}{n}\int_{0}^{t}\mathbb{E}_{n}{\cal H}_{n}(s)\mathrm{d}s+\|\beta\|_{\infty}\Big[(T_{L}+T_{R})t+\frac{1}{n^{3/2}}\mathbb{E}_{n}{\cal H}_{n}(0)\Big].

In consequence

(13.11)

\displaystyle I_{1}\leq|J_{n}(t;\beta)|+\frac{\gamma\|\beta^{\prime}\|_{\infty}}{n}\int_{0}^{t}\mathbb{E}_{n}{\cal H}_{n}(s)\mathrm{d}s

\displaystyle+\|\beta\|_{\infty}\Big[(T_{L}+T_{R})t+\frac{1}{n^{3/2}}\mathbb{E}_{n}{\cal H}_{n}(0)\Big].

Concerning
I_{2}
, using (
2.31
), we write

(13.12)

\displaystyle I_{2}=\left|\int_{0}^{t}\sum_{x=1}^{n}\beta_{x}\sum_{y=0}^{x-1}\mathbb{E}_{n}\nabla^{\star}j_{y,y+1}(s)\mathrm{d}s\right|=\frac{1}{n^{3/2}}\left|\int_{0}^{t}\sum_{x=1}^{n-1}\beta_{x}\sum_{y=0}^{x-1}\mathbb{E}_{n}{\cal G}{\cal E}_{y}(s)\mathrm{d}s\right|

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{n^{3/2}}\left|\sum_{x=1}^{n-1}\beta_{x}\sum_{y=0}^{x-1}\big[\mathbb{E}_{n}{\cal E}_{y}(t)-\mathbb{E}_{n}{\cal E}_{y}(0)\big]\mathrm{d}s\right|\leq(n+1)\|\beta\|_{\infty}\frac{\mathbb{E}_{n}{\cal H}_{n}(t)+\mathbb{E}_{n}{\cal H}_{n}(0)}{n^{3/2}}.

Combining (
13.11
) with (
13.12
) we get
(
13.7
) for
z=0
. The proof for
z=n+1
is analogous.

Using (
2.31
) we get

(13.13)

\begin{split}&\int_{0}^{t}\mathbb{E}_{n}j_{x-1,x}(s)\mathrm{d}s=\sum_{y=0}^{x-1}\int_{0}^{t}\mathbb{E}_{n}[\nabla^{\star}j_{y,y+1}(s)]\mathrm{d}s+\int_{0}^{t}\mathbb{E}_{n}j_{-1,0}(s)\mathrm{d}s\\
&=\frac{1}{n^{3/2}}\sum_{y=0}^{x-1}\mathbb{E}_{n}\mathcal{E}_{y}(0)-\frac{1}{n^{3/2}}\sum_{y=0}^{x-1}\mathbb{E}_{n}\mathcal{E}_{y}(t)+\int_{0}^{t}\mathbb{E}_{n}j_{-1,0}(s)\mathrm{d}s,\quad x=0,\ldots,n.\end{split}

Combining with (
13.7
) we obtain
(
13.8
) as well.
∎

13.3.
Proof of Theorem
2.9

We shall prove in Section
14
the following estimate: there exists

C>0
such that

(13.14)

\begin{split}&n^{1/2}|J_{n}(t;\beta^{\prime})|\leq C\Big[n+n^{3/4}|J_{n}(t,\beta^{\prime})|^{1/2}+\mathbb{E}_{n}{\cal H}_{n}(0)+\mathbb{E}_{n}{\cal H}_{n}(t)\\
&+\int_{0}^{t}\mathbb{E}_{n}{\cal H}_{n}(s)\mathrm{d}s+\Big(n\mathbb{E}_{n}{\cal H}_{n}(0)\Big)^{1/2}+\Big(n\mathbb{E}_{n}{\cal H}_{n}(t)\Big)^{1/2}+\Big(n\int_{0}^{t}\mathbb{E}_{n}{\cal H}_{n}(s)\mathrm{d}s\Big)^{1/2}\Big].\end{split}

Using Young’s inequality

(13.15)

ab\leq\frac{a^{2}}{2\gamma}+\frac{\gamma b^{2}}{2},\quad a,b,\gamma>0,

this leads to the estimate

(13.16)

n^{1/2}|J_{n}(t;\beta^{\prime})|\leq C\Big(n+\int_{0}^{t}\mathbb{E}_{n}{\cal H}_{n}(s)\mathrm{d}s+\mathbb{E}_{n}{\cal H}_{n}(t)+\mathbb{E}_{n}{\cal H}_{n}(0)\Big).

From estimate (
13.16
) and Corollary
2.7
,
shown modulo estimate
(
13.14
), we conclude that for any
t_{*}>0
there exists
C>0
such that

(13.17)

|J_{n}(t;\beta^{\prime})|\leq C\sqrt{n},\quad t\in[0,t_{*}],\,n=1,2,\ldots.

Then, estimate (
2.37
) is a conclusion of (
13.17
)
and (
13.8
).∎

14.
Proof of estimate (
13.14
)

14.1.
Preliminaries

We consider
\beta:[0,1]\to(0,+\infty)
that is
C^{\infty}
smooth and such
that
{\rm supp}\,\beta^{\prime}\subset(0,1)
. Recall the defintion of

J_{n}(t;\beta^{\prime})
given in (
6.4
)
As in (
6.6
) we can write

(14.1)

\displaystyle n^{1/2}J_{n}(t;\beta^{\prime})=-\theta_{pr}(\beta^{\prime};n)-\sum_{\iota\in I}\xi^{(pr)}_{\iota}(\beta^{\prime};n)-\sum_{\iota\in I}\pi^{(pr)}_{\iota}(\beta^{\prime};n).

Here
I=\{p,pr,rp,r\}
and the terms on the right hand side has been
defined in (
6.7
)–(
6.11
).
Denote also

(14.2)

{\mathfrak{G}}_{n}(t):=\frac{|J_{n}(t,\beta)|+1}{n^{1/2}}+\frac{1}{n}\big(\mathbb{E}_{n}{\cal H}_{n}(0)+\mathbb{E}_{n}{\cal H}_{n}(t)\big)+\frac{1}{n^{3/2}}\int_{0}^{t}\mathbb{E}_{n}{\cal H}_{n}(s)\mathrm{d}s.

Combining (
13.7
) and (
4.1
) we conclude in
particular the following:

Corollary 14.1
.

Suppose that
\beta:[0,1]\to[0,+\infty)
is a function satisfying the
assumptions of Propostion
13.2
such that

\sum_{x=0}^{n}\beta_{x}\sim n
.
Then, there exists a constant

C>0
such that

(14.3)

{\cal H}_{n}^{(2)}(t)\leq{\cal H}_{n}^{(2)}(0)+C{\mathfrak{G}}_{n}(t)

and

(14.4)

\begin{split}&\sum_{x=1}^{n}\mathop{\sum_{x^{\prime}=0}^{n}}_{x^{\prime}\not\in\{x-1,x\}}\int_{0}^{t}\left\{\mathbb{E}_{n}\left[\nabla^{\star}p_{x}(s)p_{x^{\prime}}(s)\right]\right\}^{2}\mathrm{d}s+\sum_{x=1}^{n}\int_{0}^{t}\Big[\nabla^{\star}\mathbb{E}_{n}p_{x}^{2}(s)\Big]^{2}\mathrm{d}s\\
&+\sum_{x=1}^{n}\sum_{x^{\prime}=1}^{n}\int_{0}^{t}\left\{\mathbb{E}_{n}\left[\nabla^{\star}p_{x}(s)r_{x^{\prime}}(s)\right]\right\}^{2}\mathrm{d}s+\sum_{z=0,n}\sum_{x=0}^{n}\int_{0}^{t}[b_{z,x}^{(p)}(s)]^{2}\mathrm{d}s\\
&+\sum_{z=0,n}\sum_{x^{\prime}=1}^{n}\int_{0}^{t}[b_{z,x}^{(pr)}(s)]^{2}\mathrm{d}s\leq\frac{1}{n^{1/2}}\Big({\cal H}_{n}^{(2)}(0)+C{\mathfrak{G}}_{n}(t)\Big)\end{split}

for all
n=1,2,\ldots
.

14.2.
Estimates of
\theta_{pr}(\beta^{\prime};n)

Using formula (
8.6
) we can write that
\theta_{pr}(\beta^{\prime};n)=\theta_{pr,-}(\beta^{\prime};n)-\theta_{pr,+}(\beta^{\prime};n)

where
\theta_{pr,\pm}(\beta^{\prime};n)
are defined in (
8.7
), with

\varphi
replaced by
\beta
. Thanks to (
8.16
) we conclude
that for any
t>0
there exists a constant
C>0
such that
the estimate

(14.5)

|\theta_{pr,-}(\beta^{\prime};n)|\leq C\int_{0}^{t}\mathbb{E}_{n}{\cal H}_{n}(s)\mathrm{d}s,\quad n=1,2,\ldots.

Concerning
\theta_{pr,+}^{(o)}(\beta^{\prime};n)
, after similar
calculations to those performed in the case of
\theta_{pr,-}^{(o)}(\beta^{\prime};n)
in Section
8
, we conclude that

(14.6)

\displaystyle\theta_{pr,+}(\beta^{\prime};n)=(n+1)\bar{\theta}_{pr,+}(\beta^{\prime};n)(1+o_{n}(1)),\quad\mbox{where}
