Compliance with Ethical Standards

Ethics declaration: not applicable.

Appendix A
Discrete lattice gradient and Laplacian

A.1.
Finite lattice gradient and divergence operators

Let
\mathbb{Z}_{n}:=\{0,\ldots,n\}
and suppose that
f:\mathbb{Z}_{n}\to\mathbb{R}
. It can be represented as a vector in finite
dimensional space

f=(f_{0},\ldots,f_{n})
.
Its divergence
\nabla^{\star}:\mathbb{R}^{n+1}\to\mathbb{R}^{n}
is given by

\nabla^{\star}f_{x}=f_{x}-f_{x-1}
,
x=1,\ldots,n
.
The gradient operator
\nabla:\mathbb{R}^{n}\to\mathbb{R}^{n+1}
assigns to each

f=\left(\begin{array}[]{c}f_{1}\\
\vdots\\
f_{n}\end{array}\right)

a vector
(\nabla f)_{x}=f_{x+1}-f_{x}
,
x=0,\ldots,n
, with the
convention
f_{0}=f_{n+1}=0
.
We have

\nabla^{T}=-\nabla^{\star}

and

\sum_{x=0}^{n}\nabla f_{x}\;g_{x}=-\sum_{x=1}^{n}f_{x}\nabla^{\star}g_{x},\quad f\in\mathbb{R}^{n},\,g\in\mathbb{R}^{n+1}.

A.2.
Discrete Neumann Laplacian
-\Delta_{\rm N}

The discrete Neumann Laplacian is defined as an operator on
\mathbb{R}^{n+1}
given by
the formula

\Delta_{\rm N}f_{x}:=f_{x+1}+f_{x-1}-2f_{x},\quad x=0,\ldots,n,

with the boundary condition
f_{-1}:=f_{0}
and
f_{n+1}:=f_{n}
.
Let
\lambda_{j,n}
and
\psi_{j}
,
j=0,\ldots,n
be the eigenvalues
and the respective eigenfunctions of
-\Delta_{\rm N}
.
They are given by

\displaystyle\lambda_{j}=\gamma_{j}^{2},\quad\psi_{j}(x)=\left(\frac{2-\delta_{0,j}}{n+1}\right)^{1/2}\cos\left(\frac{\pi j(2x+1)}{2(n+1)}\right),\quad\mbox{with}

(A.1)

\displaystyle\gamma_{j}=2\sin\left(\frac{j\pi}{2(n+1)}\right)

for
x,j=0,\ldots,n.

We have

\sum_{j={0}}^{n}\psi_{j}(x)\psi_{j}(x^{\prime})=\delta_{x,x^{\prime}},\quad\mbox{
and}\quad\sum_{x=0}^{n}\psi_{j}(x)\psi_{j^{\prime}}(x)=\delta_{j,j^{\prime}},\quad x,x^{\prime},j,j^{\prime}=0,\ldots,n.

A.3.
Dirichlet Laplacian

It is defined as an operator on
\mathbb{R}^{n}
that is given by
the formula

\Delta_{\rm D}f_{x}:=f_{x+1}+f_{x-1}-2f_{x},\quad x=1,\ldots,n

with the boundary condition
f_{0}=f_{n+1}:=0
. Its eigenvalues equal
\lambda_{j}
and the
respective eigenvectors are given by

(A.2)

\displaystyle\phi_{j}(x)=\left(\frac{2}{n+1}\right)^{1/2}\sin\left(\frac{jx\pi}{n+1}\right),\quad\mbox{with
}x,j=1,\ldots,n.

We have the orthogonality relations

\sum_{j=1}^{n}\phi_{j}(x)\phi_{j}(x^{\prime})=\delta_{x,x^{\prime}},\quad\mbox{
and}\quad\sum_{x=1}^{n}\phi_{j}(x)\phi_{j^{\prime}}(x^{\prime})=\delta_{j,j^{\prime}},\quad x,x^{\prime},j,j^{\prime}=1,\ldots,n.

Note that

(A.3)

\displaystyle\nabla^{\star}\psi_{j}=-\gamma_{j}\phi_{j}\quad\mbox{and}\quad\nabla\phi_{j}=\gamma_{j}\psi_{j},\quad j=0,\ldots,n.

In addition,

\displaystyle\nabla\nabla^{\star}f=\Delta_{\rm N}f,\quad f\in\mathbb{R}^{n+1}\quad\mbox{and}\quad\nabla^{\star}\nabla f=\Delta_{\rm D}f,\quad f\in\mathbb{R}^{n}.

Appendix B
Proofs of the results from Section
3

B.1.
Green’s function of the Neumann Laplacian

The Neumann Laplacian
\Delta_{\rm N}
is the generator of the
reflected Brownian motion
\Big(\sqrt{2}w_{t}^{({\rm N})}\Big)_{t\geq 0}
, where

w_{t}^{({\rm N})}=\chi\big(w_{t}\big)
,
t\geq 0
,
and
\chi:\mathbb{R}\to\mathbb{R}
is the
2
-periodic extension of the function

\chi(u)=|u|
,
u\in[-1,1]
and
(w_{t})_{t\geq 0}
is the standard Brownian
motion.
Its transition probabilities are given by

(B.1)

\begin{split}&p_{t}(u,v)=\sum_{n=-\infty}^{+\infty}\Big[p_{t}(u-v+2n)+p_{t}(2n+u+v)\Big],\quad\mbox{where}\\
&p_{t}(u):=\frac{1}{\sqrt{4\pi t}}e^{-u^{2}/(4t)},\quad u,v\in[0,1].\end{split}

The Green’s function kernel corresponding to the operator

(\lambda-\Delta_{\rm N})^{-1}
, see (
2.13
), is then given by

(B.2)

\displaystyle G_{\lambda}(u,v)=\sum_{n=0}^{+\infty}\frac{c_{n}(u)c_{n}(v)}{\lambda+(n\pi)^{2}}

\displaystyle=\sum_{n=-\infty}^{+\infty}\Big[g_{\lambda}(u-v+2n)+g_{\lambda}(2n+u+v)\Big],\quad\mbox{where}

\displaystyle g_{\lambda}(u)=\int_{0}^{+\infty}e^{-\lambda t}p_{t}(u)\mathrm{d}t.

Using the classical identity

\int_{0}^{\infty}t^{-1/2}e^{-at-b/t}\,dt=\sqrt{\frac{\pi}{a}}e^{-2\sqrt{ab}},\qquad a>0,\quad b\geq 0,

with
a=\lambda
and
b=u^{2}/4
, we obtain

(B.3)

g_{\lambda}(u)=\frac{1}{2\sqrt{\lambda}}e^{-\sqrt{\lambda}|u|}.

In particular, for
u\in[0,1]
,

g_{\lambda}(u)=\frac{1}{2\sqrt{\lambda}}e^{-\sqrt{\lambda}u}.

B.2.
Representation of
g_{m}(u,u^{\prime};v)
,
v=0,1

Recall that

V_{\varrho}(u,u^{\prime})=\varrho G_{\varrho}(u,u^{\prime}),

where, see Section
B.1
,

(B.4)

G_{\varrho}(u,v)=\frac{1}{\sqrt{\varrho}}\sum_{n\in\mathbb{Z}}e^{-\sqrt{\varrho}|u+v+2n|},\qquad v\in\{0,1\}.

is the Green function of the
Neumann Laplacian on
[0,1]
. In particular we have

(B.5)

G_{\varrho}(u,0)=\frac{\cosh(\sqrt{\varrho}(1-u))}{\sqrt{\varrho}\sinh\sqrt{\varrho}},\qquad G_{\varrho}(u,1)=\frac{\cosh(\sqrt{\varrho}u)}{\sqrt{\varrho}\sinh\sqrt{\varrho}}.

and, as a result,

(B.6)

V_{\varrho}(u,0)=\sqrt{\varrho}\frac{\cosh(\sqrt{\varrho}(1-u))}{\sinh\sqrt{\varrho}},\qquad V_{\varrho}(u,1)=\sqrt{\varrho}\frac{\cosh(\sqrt{\varrho}u)}{\sinh\sqrt{\varrho}}.

Using the above formulas, (
3.7
) and substitution

k:=\sqrt{\varrho}
, we get, for
m=0,1,2,\ldots

and
v\in\{0,1\}
,

\displaystyle g_{m}(u,u^{\prime};v)=2\sum_{n,n^{\prime}\in\mathbb{Z}}\int_{0}^{\infty}k^{3/2}e^{-k(2m+|u+v+2n|+|u^{\prime}+v+2n^{\prime}|)}\,\mathrm{d}k

\displaystyle=2\Gamma\left(\frac{5}{2}\right)\sum_{n,n^{\prime}\in\mathbb{Z}}\frac{1}{\bigl(2m+|u+v+2n|+|u^{\prime}+v+2n^{\prime}|\bigr)^{5/2}}.

Since

2\Gamma\left(\frac{5}{2}\right)=\frac{3\sqrt{\pi}}{2},
formula (
3.8
) follows. Performing analogous
calculations, using the definition of
g_{m}^{\rm cr}(u,u^{\prime};v)
, see
(
3.7
), we get (
3.9
).

B.3.
Green’s function for the Robin problem

Define

\displaystyle F_{\varrho}(u)

\displaystyle=\cosh(\sqrt{\varrho}u)+2\widetilde{\gamma}\sinh(\sqrt{\varrho}u),

\displaystyle H_{\varrho}(u)

\displaystyle=\cosh(\sqrt{\varrho}(1-u))+2\widetilde{\gamma}\sinh(\sqrt{\varrho}(1-u)).

These functions solve

-f^{\prime\prime}+\rho f=0

and satisfy the left and right Robin
boundary conditions, respectively:

F_{\varrho}^{\prime}(0)=2\widetilde{\gamma}\sqrt{\varrho}F_{\varrho}(0),\qquad H_{\varrho}^{\prime}(1)=-2\widetilde{\gamma}\sqrt{\varrho}H_{\varrho}(1).

Their Wronskian is constant and equals

\displaystyle F_{\varrho}^{\prime}(u)H_{\varrho}(u)-F_{\varrho}(u)H_{\varrho}^{\prime}(u)=\sqrt{\varrho}\bigl[

\displaystyle(1+4\widetilde{\gamma}^{2})\sinh\sqrt{\varrho}+4\widetilde{\gamma}\cosh\sqrt{\varrho}\bigr].

Consequently,

(B.7)

G_{\rho}^{(\widetilde{\gamma})}(u,u^{\prime})=\frac{F_{\varrho}(u\wedge u^{\prime})H_{\varrho}(u\vee u^{\prime})}{\sqrt{\varrho}\bigl[(1+4\widetilde{\gamma}^{2})\sinh\sqrt{\varrho}+4\widetilde{\gamma}\cosh\sqrt{\varrho}\bigr]}.

In particular we can see from the above formula that

(B.8)

G_{\rho}^{(\widetilde{\gamma})}(u,u^{\prime})\geq 0.

B.4.
Representation of the Robin Green’s function using
perturbative formula for the Neumann Green’s function

Recall that
G_{\rho}(u,u^{\prime})
denotes the Green’s function of the
Neumann resolvent

(\rho-\Delta_{N})^{-1},

and that

V_{\rho}(u,v)=\rho G_{\rho}(u,v).

Introduce the vector

{\bf G}_{\rho}(u):=\begin{pmatrix}G_{\rho}(u,0)\\
G_{\rho}(u,1)\end{pmatrix}.

The restriction of the Neumann Green’s function to the two
endpoints is (see (
B.5
))

(B.9)

B_{\rho}:=\begin{pmatrix}G_{\rho}(0,0)&G_{\rho}(0,1)\\
G_{\rho}(1,0)&G_{\rho}(1,1)\end{pmatrix}=\frac{1}{\sqrt{\rho}}\begin{pmatrix}\coth\sqrt{\rho}&(\sinh\sqrt{\rho})^{-1}\\
(\sinh\sqrt{\rho})^{-1}&\coth\sqrt{\rho}\end{pmatrix}.

Consider also the matrix

(B.10)

M_{\widetilde{\gamma}}(\rho):=\begin{pmatrix}F(\widetilde{\gamma},\sqrt{\rho})&-G(\widetilde{\gamma},\sqrt{\rho})\\
&\\
-G(\widetilde{\gamma},\sqrt{\rho})&F(\widetilde{\gamma},\sqrt{\rho})\end{pmatrix},

where
F
and
G
are given by (
2.17
).
A direct calculation gives

(B.11)

F(\widetilde{\gamma},\sqrt{\rho})^{2}-G(\widetilde{\gamma},\sqrt{\rho})^{2}=\frac{\sinh\sqrt{\rho}}{(1+4\widetilde{\gamma}^{2})\sinh\sqrt{\rho}+4\widetilde{\gamma}\cosh\sqrt{\rho}}.

Consequently, the inverse matrix to
M_{\widetilde{\gamma}}(\rho)
is
given by

(B.12)

\displaystyle M_{\widetilde{\gamma}}(\rho)^{-1}

\displaystyle=\begin{pmatrix}1+2\widetilde{\gamma}\coth\sqrt{\rho}&2\widetilde{\gamma}(\sinh\sqrt{\rho})^{-1}\\
&\\
2\widetilde{\gamma}(\sinh\sqrt{\rho})^{-1}&1+2\widetilde{\gamma}\coth\sqrt{\rho}\end{pmatrix}=I+2\widetilde{\gamma}\sqrt{\rho}\,B_{\rho}.

To verify this, fix
u^{\prime}\in(0,1)
. Since
G_{\rho}
is the
Neumann Green’s function,

(B.13)

\partial_{u}G_{\rho}(0,u^{\prime})=\partial_{u}G_{\rho}(1,u^{\prime})=0.

Using (
B.5
) we conclude that

(B.14)

\partial_{u}G_{\rho}(0,0)=-1,\qquad\partial_{u}G_{\rho}(1,0)=0,\qquad\partial_{u}G_{\rho}(0,1)=0,\qquad\partial_{u}G_{\rho}(1,1)=1.

Furthermore, both
G_{\rho}(\cdot,0)
and

G_{\rho}(\cdot,1)
satisfy

\left(\rho-\frac{d^{2}}{du^{2}}\right)f=0\qquad\text{in }(0,1).

For this fixed
u^{\prime}
, let

{\bf c}_{\rho}(u^{\prime})=\begin{pmatrix}c_{\rho,0}(u^{\prime})\\
c_{\rho,1}(u^{\prime})\end{pmatrix},

with the components, yet to be determined, such that

(B.15)

G_{\rho}^{(\widetilde{\gamma})}(u,u^{\prime})=G_{\rho}(u,u^{\prime})-c_{\rho,0}(u^{\prime})G_{\rho}(u,0)-c_{\rho,1}(u^{\prime})G_{\rho}(u,1)

would satisfy

\left(\rho-\frac{d^{2}}{du^{2}}\right)G_{\rho}^{(\widetilde{\gamma})}(u,u^{\prime})=\delta_{u^{\prime}}(u),\quad u,u^{\prime}\in(0,1),

with the Robin boundary conditions (
3.16
).

Using (
B.13
) and
(
B.14
), we have

\partial_{u}G_{\rho}^{(\widetilde{\gamma})}(u,u^{\prime})=c_{\rho,0}(u^{\prime}),\qquad\partial_{u}G_{\rho}^{(\widetilde{\gamma})}(u,u^{\prime})=-c_{\rho,1}(u^{\prime}).

At the left endpoint,

G_{\rho}^{(\widetilde{\gamma})}(u,u^{\prime})=G_{\rho}(0,u^{\prime})-c_{\rho,0}(u^{\prime})G_{\rho}(0,0)-c_{\rho,1}(u^{\prime})G_{\rho}(0,1),

whereas at the right endpoint,

G_{\rho}^{(\widetilde{\gamma})}(u,u^{\prime})=G_{\rho}(1,u^{\prime})-c_{\rho,0}(u^{\prime})G_{\rho}(1,0)-c_{\rho,1}(u^{\prime})G_{\rho}(1,1).

Therefore the two Robin conditions in
(
3.16
) are equivalent to

(B.16)

\displaystyle c_{\rho,0}(u^{\prime})

\displaystyle=2\widetilde{\gamma}\sqrt{\rho}\Big[G_{\rho}(0,u^{\prime})-c_{\rho,0}(u^{\prime})G_{\rho}(0,0)-c_{\rho,1}(u^{\prime})G_{\rho}(0,1)\Big],

(B.17)

\displaystyle c_{\rho,1}(u^{\prime})

\displaystyle=2\widetilde{\gamma}\sqrt{\rho}\Big[G_{\rho}(1,u^{\prime})-c_{\rho,0}(u^{\prime})G_{\rho}(1,0)-c_{\rho,1}(u^{\prime})G_{\rho}(1,1)\Big].

In matrix notation, (
B.16
)–(
B.17
)
read

(B.18)

\left(I+2\widetilde{\gamma}\sqrt{\rho}\,B_{\rho}\right){\bf c}_{\rho}(u^{\prime})=2\widetilde{\gamma}\sqrt{\rho}\begin{pmatrix}G_{\rho}(0,u^{\prime})\\
G_{\rho}(1,u^{\prime})\end{pmatrix}.

By symmetry of the Neumann Green’s function,

\begin{pmatrix}G_{\rho}(0,u^{\prime})\\
G_{\rho}(1,u^{\prime})\end{pmatrix}={\bf G}_{\rho}(u^{\prime}).

Using (
B.12
) in
(
B.18
), we therefore obtain

(B.19)

{\bf c}_{\rho}(u^{\prime})=2\widetilde{\gamma}\sqrt{\rho}\,M_{\widetilde{\gamma}}(\rho){\bf G}_{\rho}(u^{\prime}).

Substituting (
B.19
) into
(
B.15
) proves that

(B.20)

G_{\rho}^{(\widetilde{\gamma})}(u,u^{\prime})=G_{\rho}(u,u^{\prime})-2\widetilde{\gamma}\sqrt{\rho}\,{\bf G}_{\rho}(u)^{\mathsf{T}}M_{\widetilde{\gamma}}(\rho){\bf G}_{\rho}(u^{\prime}).

B.5.
Proof of Proposition
3.1

We express

r_{\widetilde{\gamma}}
in terms of the kernels that appear in formula (
3.10
).
Recall that
q(u,u^{\prime})
is given in (
3.2
).
Using (
B.4
) we obtain

2\sqrt{\rho}\,G_{\rho}(u,u^{\prime})=\sum_{n\in\mathbb{Z}}\left\{e^{-\sqrt{\rho}|u-u^{\prime}+2n|}+e^{-\sqrt{\rho}|u+u^{\prime}+2n|}\right\},

therefore

(B.21)

q(u,u^{\prime})=\frac{1}{\sqrt{2}\,\pi}\int_{0}^{\infty}\rho^{3/4}G_{\rho}(u,u^{\prime})\,d\rho.

On the other hand, from the definition of

h_{\widetilde{\gamma}}
and the identity

V_{\rho}(u,v)=\rho G_{\rho}(u,v)
, we obtain

\displaystyle\sum_{v=0,1}h_{\widetilde{\gamma}}(u,u^{\prime};v)

\displaystyle\quad=\int_{0}^{\infty}\rho^{5/4}\Big\{F(\widetilde{\gamma},\sqrt{\rho})\big[G_{\rho}(u,0)G_{\rho}(u^{\prime},0)+G_{\rho}(u,1)G_{\rho}(u^{\prime},1)\big]

\displaystyle\hskip 119.50157pt-G(\widetilde{\gamma},\sqrt{\rho})\big[G_{\rho}(u,1)G_{\rho}(u^{\prime},0)+G_{\rho}(u,0)G_{\rho}(u^{\prime},1)\big]\Big\}\,d\rho.

By (
B.10
), we can write

(B.22)

\sum_{v=0,1}h_{\widetilde{\gamma}}(u,u^{\prime};v)=\int_{0}^{\infty}\rho^{5/4}{\bf G}_{\rho}(u)^{\mathsf{T}}M_{\widetilde{\gamma}}(\rho){\bf G}_{\rho}(u^{\prime})\,d\rho.

We also have, see (
2.29
),

(B.23)

\frac{c_{\rm bd}}{c_{\rm bulk}}=\frac{\sqrt{2}\,\widetilde{\gamma}}{\pi}.

Combining
(
B.21
),
(
B.22
), and
(
B.23
), we get

\displaystyle q(u,u^{\prime})-\frac{\sqrt{2}\,\widetilde{\gamma}}{\pi}\sum_{v=0,1}h_{\widetilde{\gamma}}(u,u^{\prime};v)

\displaystyle\quad=\frac{1}{\sqrt{2}\,\pi}\int_{0}^{\infty}\rho^{3/4}\Big[G_{\rho}(u,u^{\prime})-2\widetilde{\gamma}\sqrt{\rho}\,{\bf G}_{\rho}(u)^{\mathsf{T}}M_{\widetilde{\gamma}}(\rho){\bf G}_{\rho}(u^{\prime})\Big]\,d\rho

(B.24)

\displaystyle\quad=\frac{1}{\sqrt{2}\,\pi}\int_{0}^{\infty}\rho^{3/4}G_{\rho}^{(\widetilde{\gamma})}(u,u^{\prime})\,d\rho.

The last expression follows from (
B.20
).
By (
B.8
), the last expression is
nonnegative, which ends the proof of positivity of

r_{\widetilde{\gamma}}(u,u^{\prime})
together with formula (
3.15
). ∎

Remark B.1
.

Substituting (
B.7
)
into (
3.15
)
we obtain

(B.25)

\displaystyle r_{\widetilde{\gamma}}(u,u^{\prime})={}

\displaystyle\frac{1}{\sqrt{2}\pi}\int_{0}^{\infty}\varrho^{1/4}\frac{F_{\varrho}(u\wedge u^{\prime})H_{\varrho}(u\vee u^{\prime})}{(1+4\widetilde{\gamma}^{2})\sinh\sqrt{\varrho}+4\widetilde{\gamma}\cosh\sqrt{\varrho}}\,\mathrm{d}\varrho.

The representation
(
B.25
)
immediately implies that

r_{\widetilde{\gamma}}(u,u^{\prime})=r_{\widetilde{\gamma}}(u^{\prime},u)>0,\qquad u\neq u^{\prime}.

B.6.
Proof of Proposition
3.3

From formulas (
3.5
) and (
3.11
) we obtain

\displaystyle b_{\widetilde{\gamma}}(u;v)=c_{\rm bd}\int_{0}^{\infty}\big[

\displaystyle F(\widetilde{\gamma},\sqrt{\varrho})V_{\varrho}(u,v)-G(\widetilde{\gamma},\sqrt{\varrho})V_{\varrho}(u,1-v)\big]\frac{d\varrho}{\varrho^{3/4}}.

Since, see (
B.6
),

V_{\varrho}(u,1-v)\leq\cosh(\sqrt{\varrho})V_{\varrho}(u,v),\qquad v\in\{0,1\}

and

F(\widetilde{\gamma},\sqrt{\varrho})-G(\widetilde{\gamma},\sqrt{\varrho})\cosh\sqrt{\varrho}=\frac{\sinh\sqrt{\varrho}}{(1+4\widetilde{\gamma}^{2})\sinh\sqrt{\varrho}+4\widetilde{\gamma}\cosh\sqrt{\varrho}}>0,

we conclude that

\displaystyle F(\widetilde{\gamma},\sqrt{\varrho})V_{\varrho}(u,v)-G(\widetilde{\gamma},\sqrt{\varrho})V_{\varrho}(u,1-v)

\displaystyle\qquad\geq\big[F(\widetilde{\gamma},\sqrt{\varrho})-G(\widetilde{\gamma},\sqrt{\varrho})\cosh(\sqrt{\varrho})\big]V_{\varrho}(u,v)>0.

Thus (
3.17
) follows.

Concerning (
3.18
), by the reflection symmetry, it suffices to consider

v=0
and
u\downarrow 0
.

Using (
B.6
) and changing variables
k=\sqrt{\rho}
in the
formula (
3.11
)
we obtain

(B.26)

\displaystyle b_{\widetilde{\gamma}}(u;0)=2c_{\rm bd}\int_{0}^{\infty}k^{1/2}\Bigg[\frac{\cosh(k(1-u))}{\sinh k}F(\widetilde{\gamma},k)-\frac{\cosh(ku)}{\sinh k}G(\widetilde{\gamma},k)\Bigg]\,dk.

From (
2.17
) we conclude that
for
k\to+\infty
:

F(\widetilde{\gamma},k)=\frac{1}{1+2\widetilde{\gamma}}+O(e^{-2k}),\qquad G(\widetilde{\gamma},k)=O(e^{-k}).

Moreover, for
0<u\leq 1/2
,

\frac{\cosh(k(1-u))}{\sinh k}=e^{-ku}+O(e^{-k}),\qquad\mbox{uniformly in }k\geq 1,

and

\frac{\cosh(ku)}{\sinh k}=O(e^{-k/2}),\qquad\mbox{uniformly in }k\geq 1.

It follows that the contribution to
(
B.26
) from
k\geq 1

equals

b_{\widetilde{\gamma}}(u;0)=\frac{2c_{\rm bd}}{1+2\widetilde{\gamma}}\int_{1}^{\infty}k^{1/2}e^{-ku}\,dk+O(1),

where the error is uniform as
u\downarrow 0
.

The integral over
0<k<1
is uniformly
bounded,
thus,

b_{\widetilde{\gamma}}(u;0)=\frac{2c_{\rm bd}}{1+2\widetilde{\gamma}}\int_{0}^{\infty}k^{1/2}e^{-ku}\,dk+O(1).

By the Gamma integral,

\int_{0}^{\infty}k^{1/2}e^{-ku}\,dk=\Gamma(3/2)u^{-3/2}=\frac{\sqrt{\pi}}{2}u^{-3/2}.

Consequently,

b_{\widetilde{\gamma}}(u;0)=\frac{\sqrt{\pi}c_{\rm bd}}{1+2\widetilde{\gamma}}u^{-3/2}+O(1),\qquad u\downarrow 0.

This proves (
3.18
).∎

Appendix C
Proofs of Theorems
2.3
and
2.4

C.1.
Spectral fractional power of the Neumann Laplacian

Given a function
\varphi\in L^{2}[0,1]
we denote by
\hat{\varphi}_{c}(\ell)
the respective Fourier coefficients.
Suppose that
\alpha\in(0,1]
. We define the operator

\displaystyle|\Delta_{N}|^{\alpha}\varphi(u)=\sum_{n=0}^{+\infty}(n\pi)^{2\alpha}\hat{\varphi}_{c}(n)c_{n}(u),\quad\mbox{with}

(C.1)

\displaystyle{\cal D}(|\Delta_{N}|^{\alpha})=\Big[\varphi\in L^{2}[0,1]:\,\sum_{n=0}^{+\infty}(n\pi)^{4\alpha}\hat{\varphi}_{c}^{2}(n)<+\infty\Big].

C.1.1.
Fractional Sobolev spaces

Suppose that
\alpha>0
. Define
H^{\alpha}[0,1]
as the completion of

C^{\infty}[0,1]
-
C^{\infty}
smooth functions - under that norm

(C.2)

\begin{split}&\|\varphi\|_{\alpha}:=\left(\|\varphi\|^{2}_{L^{2}[0,1]}+\|\varphi\|_{\alpha,0}^{2}\right)^{1/2}\quad\mbox{where}\\
&\|\varphi\|_{L^{2}[0,1]}=\left(\int_{0}^{1}\varphi^{2}(u)\mathrm{d}u\right)^{1/2}=\left(\sum_{\ell=0}^{+\infty}\hat{\varphi}_{c}(\ell)^{2}\right)^{1/2},\\
&\|\varphi\|_{\alpha,0}:=\Big(\sum_{\ell=1}^{+\infty}(\pi\ell)^{2\alpha}\hat{\varphi}_{c}^{2}(\ell)\Big)^{1/2}.\end{split}

Let
P[0,1]
be the space of all finite linear
combinations made of the cosine basis. By
H^{\alpha}_{0}[0,1]
we denote the subspace of
H^{\alpha}[0,1]

being the closure of
C^{\infty}_{c}(0,1)
– the set of
C^{\infty}
smooth functions,
compactly supported in
(0,1)
– under the norm

\|\cdot\|_{\alpha,0}
in (
C.2
). The
spaces
H^{\alpha}[0,1]
and
H^{\alpha}_{0}[0,1]
are Hilbert and we shall denote by

\langle\cdot,\cdot\rangle_{\alpha}
and
\langle\cdot,\cdot\rangle_{\alpha,0}
the respective scalar products. The
scalar product
\langle\cdot,\cdot\rangle_{\alpha,0}
obviously extends to a bounded
bilinear form on
H^{\alpha}[0,1]
.

Lemma C.1
.

Suppose that
\alpha>1/2
. Then,

H^{\alpha}[0,1]\subset C[0,1]
and if
\varphi\in H^{\alpha}[0,1]

(C.3)

\varphi(u)=\sum_{\ell=0}^{+\infty}\hat{\varphi}_{c}(\ell)c_{\ell}(u),\quad u\in[0,1]\quad\mbox{pointwise}.

Proof.

By the Cauchy-Schwarz inequality, we have for
\alpha>1/2

\displaystyle\sum_{\ell=0}^{+\infty}|\hat{\varphi}_{c}(\ell)|\leq\left(\sum_{\ell=0}^{+\infty}(\hat{\varphi}_{c}(\ell))^{2}\ell^{2\alpha}\right)^{1/2}\left(\sum_{\ell=1}^{+\infty}\frac{1}{\ell^{2\alpha}}\right)^{1/2}<+\infty

and the conclusion of Lemma
C.1
follows.
∎

From the lemma we conclude also the following.

Corollary C.2
.

Under the assumption of Lemma
C.1

we have

(C.4)

H^{\alpha}_{0}[0,1]=\Big[\varphi\in H^{\alpha}[0,1]:\,\varphi(0)=\varphi(1)=0\Big].

In addition,
the norms
\|\cdot\|_{\alpha}
and
\|\cdot\|_{\alpha,0}
defined in
(
C.2
) are equivalent on the space
H^{\alpha}_{0}[0,1]
.

Proof.

Denote the space on the right hand side of (
C.4
) as

{\cal H}_{0}^{\alpha}
. From the definition of
H^{\alpha}_{0}[0,1]
it is easy to see that
{\cal H}_{0}^{\alpha}\subset H^{\alpha}_{0}[0,1]
.
We show that
H^{\alpha}_{0}[0,1]\subset{\cal H}_{0}^{\alpha}
.
For
\alpha=1
, suppose that
\varphi\in H^{1}[0,1]
and
\varphi(0)=\varphi(1)=0
.
Then
\varphi^{\prime}\in L^{2}[0,1]
and can be approximated by functions

\chi_{n}\in C_{c}^{\infty}(0,1)
. Consider

\psi_{n}(u):=\int_{0}^{u}\chi_{n}(v)\mathrm{d}v.

We have

\lim_{n\to+\infty}\|\psi_{n}-\varphi\|_{1}=0.

Therefore

\lim_{n\to+\infty}\sup_{u\in[0,1]}|\psi_{n}(u)-\varphi(u)|=0.

Let us fix a function
\chi^{\prime}\in C^{\infty}_{c}(0,1)
such that
\chi(0)=0

and
\chi(1)=1
. Define

\varphi_{n}(u)=\psi_{n}(u)-\chi(u)\psi_{n}(1).

Then,
\varphi_{n}\in C^{\infty}_{c}(0,1)
and

\lim_{n\to+\infty}\|\varphi_{n}-\varphi\|_{1}=0

and the conclusion of the lemma follows for
\alpha=1
.

Suppose now that
\alpha\in(1/2,1)
and

\varphi=\sum_{\ell=0}^{+\infty}\hat{\varphi}_{c}(\ell)c_{\ell}(u)
belongs
to

H^{\alpha}[0,1]
and satisfies
\varphi(0)=\varphi(1)=0.
Then, consider fixed
functions
\chi_{j}\in C^{\infty}[0,1]
,
j=1,2
such that

(C.5)

\begin{split}\chi_{1}(0)=0,\quad\chi_{1}(1)=1\quad\mbox{and}\quad\chi_{2}(0)=1,\quad\chi_{2}(1)=0.\end{split}

Define

r_{n}(u):=\sum_{\ell=n+1}^{+\infty}\hat{\varphi}_{c}(\ell)c_{\ell}(u)

and

\varphi_{n}(u):=\sum_{\ell=0}^{n}\hat{\varphi}_{c}(\ell)c_{\ell}(u)+C_{n}\chi_{1}(u)+C_{n+1}\chi_{2}(u),

where

\begin{split}0=\sum_{\ell=0}^{n}\hat{\varphi}_{c}(\ell)c_{\ell}(0)+C_{n},\qquad 0=\sum_{\ell=0}^{n}\hat{\varphi}_{c}(\ell)c_{\ell}(1)+C_{n+1}.\end{split}

We have

\varphi_{n}(0)=0
,
\varphi_{n}(1)=0

and
\varphi_{n}\in C^{\infty}[0,1]\subset H^{1}_{0}[0,1]
. Therefore

(\varphi_{n})\subset H^{\alpha}_{0}[0,1]
.
Note that also

\begin{split}&|C_{n}|\leq\sqrt{2}\sum_{\ell=n+1}^{+\infty}|\hat{\varphi}_{c}(\ell)|\\
&\leq\sqrt{2}\left(\sum_{\ell=n+1}^{+\infty}\frac{1}{\ell^{3/2}}\right)^{1/2}\left(\sum_{\ell=n+1}^{+\infty}\ell^{3/2}|\hat{\varphi}_{c}(\ell)|^{2}\right)^{1/2}\to 0,\end{split}

as
n\to+\infty
. Analogously,
C_{n+1}\to 0
. We can write then

\displaystyle\|\varphi-\varphi_{n}\|_{\alpha}\leq\|r_{n}\|_{\alpha}+C_{n}\|\chi_{1}\|_{\alpha}+C_{n+1}\|\chi_{2}\|_{\alpha}\to 0,

as
n\to+\infty
, and this ends the proof of (
C.4
).

∎

As an immediate consequence of the above result we can formulate the
following.

Corollary C.3
.

H_{0}^{\alpha}[0,1]
is a closed subspace of
H^{\alpha}[0,1]
of
co-dimension
2
.

We let

(C.6)

\displaystyle{\cal b}^{(v)}(\varphi,\varrho):=\int_{0}^{1}V_{\gamma^{2}\varrho^{4}}(u,v)[\varphi(v)-\varphi(u)]\;\mathrm{d}u,\quad\varphi\in P[0,1].

Define
\Phi_{v}(\varrho;\varphi)
by letting

(C.7)

\displaystyle\Phi_{v}(\varrho;\varphi):=\Phi_{v}^{\rm dir}(\varrho;\varphi)-\Phi_{v}^{\rm cr}(\varrho;\varphi),\quad\mbox{where}

\displaystyle\Phi_{v}^{\rm dir}(\varrho;\varphi):=F(\tilde{\gamma},\gamma\varrho^{2}){\cal b}^{(v)}(\varphi,\varrho),

(C.8)

\displaystyle\Phi_{v}^{\rm cr}(\varrho;\varphi):=G(\tilde{\gamma},\gamma\varrho^{2}){\cal b}^{(1-v)}(\varphi,\varrho),\quad\varphi\in P[0,1].

Note that

(C.9)

\displaystyle\Phi_{v}^{\rm dir}(\varrho;\varphi)=\sum_{\ell=1}^{+\infty}\frac{c_{\ell}(v)\hat{\varphi}_{c}(\ell)(\pi\ell)^{2}}{(\ell\pi)^{2}+\gamma^{2}\varrho^{4}}F(\tilde{\gamma},\gamma\varrho^{2})

\displaystyle\Phi_{v}^{\rm cr}(\varrho;\varphi)=\sum_{\ell=1}^{+\infty}\frac{c_{\ell}(1-v)\hat{\varphi}_{c}(\ell)(\pi\ell)^{2}}{(\ell\pi)^{2}+\gamma^{2}\varrho^{4}}G(\tilde{\gamma},\gamma\varrho^{2}).

The definition of
\Phi_{v}(\varrho;\varphi)
extends to
\varphi\in L^{2}[0,1]
for a.e.
\rho\in[0,+\infty)
. We also have the following.

Lemma C.4
.

There exists
C>0
such that

(C.10)

\int_{0}^{+\infty}\Big(\Phi_{v}(\varrho;\varphi)\Big)^{2}\mathrm{d}\varrho\leq C\|\varphi\|_{3/4,0}^{2},\quad\varphi\in H^{3/4}[0,1],\,v=0,1

and

(C.11)

\int_{0}^{+\infty}\Big({\cal b}^{(v)}(\varphi,\varrho)\Big)^{2}\mathrm{d}\varrho\leq C\|\varphi\|_{3/4,0}^{2},\quad\varphi\in H^{3/4}[0,1],\,v=0,1.

Proof.

We only prove (
C.10
). The proof of
(
C.11
) goes along the same lines.

Suppose that
v=0
, the argument for
v=1
is analogous. We have

\displaystyle\int_{0}^{+\infty}\Big(\Phi_{0}^{\rm dir}(\varrho;\varphi)\Big)^{2}\mathrm{d}\varrho

(C.12)

\displaystyle=\sum_{\ell,\ell^{\prime}=1}^{+\infty}\hat{\varphi}_{c}(\ell)\hat{\varphi}_{c}(\ell^{\prime})\int_{0}^{+\infty}\frac{(\pi\ell)^{2}c_{\ell}(0)F(\tilde{\gamma},\gamma\varrho^{2})}{(\ell\pi)^{2}+\gamma^{2}\varrho^{4}}\cdot\frac{(\pi\ell^{\prime})^{2}c_{\ell^{\prime}}(0)F(\tilde{\gamma},\gamma\varrho^{2})(\tilde{\gamma},\gamma\varrho^{2})}{(\ell^{\prime}\pi)^{2}+\gamma^{2}\varrho^{4}}\mathrm{d}\varrho.

From the definition of
F
, see (
2.17
), one can see that
for any
\tilde{\gamma}>0
there exists
C>0
such that
0\leq F(\tilde{\gamma},k)\leq C
.
Using formula (
C.41
) to integrate over
\varrho
,
the right hand side of (
C.10
)
can be estimated by

(C.13)

\displaystyle C\sum_{\ell,\ell^{\prime}=1}^{+\infty}\frac{(\pi\ell\pi\ell^{\prime})^{1/2}[\pi\ell+\pi\ell^{\prime}+(\pi\ell\pi\ell^{\prime})^{1/2}]\hat{\varphi}_{c}(\ell)\hat{\varphi}_{c}(\ell^{\prime})}{\gamma^{1/2}[(\pi\ell)^{1/2}+(\pi\ell^{\prime})^{1/2}](\pi\ell+\pi\ell^{\prime})}

\displaystyle\leq C^{\prime}\sum_{\ell,\ell^{\prime}=1}^{+\infty}\frac{(\pi\ell\pi\ell^{\prime})^{1/2}|\hat{\varphi}(\ell)||\hat{\varphi}(\ell^{\prime})|}{(\pi\ell)^{1/2}+(\pi\ell^{\prime})^{1/2}}\leq C^{\prime\prime}\|\varphi\|_{3/4,0}^{2},

for some constants
C,C^{\prime},C^{\prime\prime}>0
independent of
\varphi
, by virtue of (
C.46
),
and (
C.10
) follows for
v=0
. Analogously, we show that

(C.14)

\displaystyle\int_{0}^{+\infty}\Big(\Phi_{0}^{\rm cr}(\varrho;\varphi)\Big)^{2}\mathrm{d}\varrho\leq C\|\varphi\|_{3/4,0}^{2}

∎

C.2.
Proof of Theorem
2.3

C.2.1.
Reformulation of equation (
2.18
)

Fix
T_{0},T_{1}>0
as in Definition
2.2
. Define the functionals
{\cal b}^{(v)}_{\rm temp}:[0,+\infty)\times L^{2}[0,1]\to\mathbb{R}
,

v=0,\,1
by

(C.15)

\displaystyle{\cal b}^{(v)}_{\rm temp}(\varrho,\varphi):=T_{v}-{\cal b}^{(v)}(\varrho,\varphi)=T_{v}-\sum_{\ell^{\prime}=0}^{+\infty}\frac{(\ell^{\prime}\pi)^{2}c_{\ell^{\prime}}(v)\hat{\varphi}_{c}(\ell^{\prime})}{(\ell^{\prime}\pi)^{2}+\gamma^{2}\varrho^{4}},\quad\varphi\in L^{2}[0,1],\,\varrho>0.

For
\varphi\in H^{3/4}[0,1]
we can write

(C.16)

\displaystyle{\cal b}^{(v)}_{\rm temp}(\varrho,\varphi)=T_{v}-\varphi(v)+\sum_{\ell^{\prime}=0}^{+\infty}\frac{\gamma^{2}\varrho^{4}c_{\ell^{\prime}}(v)\hat{\varphi}_{c}(\ell^{\prime})}{(\ell^{\prime}\pi)^{2}+\gamma^{2}\varrho^{4}}.

We also let

(C.17)

\Phi_{v,{\rm temp}}(\varrho,\varphi):=F(\tilde{\gamma},\gamma\varrho^{2}){\cal b}^{(v)}_{\rm temp}(\varrho,\varphi)-G(\tilde{\gamma},\gamma\varrho^{2}){\cal b}^{(1-v)}_{\rm temp}(\varrho,\varphi).

Thanks to (
2.19
) we have

(C.18)

\displaystyle\int_{0}^{t}\mathrm{d}s\int_{0}^{+\infty}\big[{\cal b}^{(v)}_{\rm temp}(\varrho,T(s))\big]^{2}\mathrm{d}\varrho<+\infty,\quad t>0\quad\mbox{and}

\displaystyle\int_{0}^{t}\mathrm{d}s\int_{0}^{+\infty}\big[\Phi_{v,{\rm temp}}(\varrho,T(s))\big]^{2}\mathrm{d}\varrho<+\infty,\quad t>0,\,v=0,1.

Equation (
2.18
) can be rewritten as

(C.19)

\begin{split}&\langle\varphi,T(t)-T_{\rm ini}\rangle_{L^{2}[0,1]}=-c_{\rm bulk}\sum_{\ell=1}^{+\infty}(\pi\ell)^{3/2}\int_{0}^{t}\hat{\varphi}_{c}(\ell)\hat{T}_{c}(s,\ell)\mathrm{d}s\\
&+4\gamma^{1/2}c_{\rm bd}\sum_{v=0,1}\int_{0}^{t}\mathrm{d}s\int_{0}^{+\infty}\Phi_{v,{\rm temp}}(\varrho,T(s)){\cal b}^{(v)}(\varrho,\varphi)\mathrm{d}\varrho,\quad\varphi\in H_{0}^{3/4}[0,1].\end{split}

for any
\varphi\in C_{c}^{\infty}(0,1)
. To show Theorem
2.3
it is equivalent with proving
uniqueness of solutions to (
C.19
) in the class of
functions for which
(
C.15
) holds.

Lemma C.5
.

Suppose that
T(\cdot)
, is a solution to (
C.19
) in the
sense of Definition
2.2
. Then,

(C.20)

\int_{0}^{t}T(s)\mathrm{d}s\in H^{3/4}[0,1],\quad\mbox{for any }t>0.

Furthermore, for any
t_{*}>0
we have

(C.21)

\sup_{t\in[0,t_{*}]}\sum_{\ell=1}^{+\infty}(\pi\ell)^{3/2}\big(\int_{0}^{t}\hat{T}_{c}(s,\ell)\mathrm{d}s\big)^{2}<+\infty.

In addition, for
t>0
,
v=0,1

(C.22)

T_{v}t=\int_{0}^{t}T(s,v)\mathrm{d}s.

Proof.

From equation (
C.19
) and Lemmas
C.4
and

C.1
we conclude that for any
t_{*}>0
there exists

C>0
such that

\Big|\left\langle\varphi,\int_{0}^{t}T(s)\mathrm{d}s\right\rangle_{3/4,0}\Big|\leq C\|\varphi\|_{3/4,0},\quad\varphi\in H_{0}^{3/4}[0,1],\,t\in[0,t_{*}].

Define two bounded linear functionals

L_{o}[\varphi]:=\frac{1}{2}\Big(\varphi(0)+\varphi(1)\Big)\quad\mbox{and}\quad L_{e}[\varphi]:=\frac{1}{2^{3/2}}\Big(\varphi(0)-\varphi(1)\Big)

for
\varphi\in H^{3/4}[0,1]
.
Let

\tilde{\varphi}(u)=\varphi(u)-L_{o}[\varphi]c_{0}(u)-L_{e}[\varphi]c_{1}(u).

We have

\displaystyle\tilde{\varphi}(0)=\varphi(0)-L_{o}[\varphi]c_{0}(0)-L_{e}[\varphi]c_{1}(0)=0,

\displaystyle\tilde{\varphi}(1)=\varphi(1)-L_{o}[\varphi]c_{0}(1)-L_{e}[\varphi]c_{1}(1)=0.

According to (
C.4
) we have
\tilde{\varphi}\in H^{3/4}_{0}[0,1]
. As a result
there exist constants
C,C^{\prime}>0
such that

\displaystyle\Big|\left\langle\varphi,\int_{0}^{t}T(s)\mathrm{d}s\right\rangle_{3/4,0}\Big|\leq\Big|\left\langle\tilde{\varphi},\int_{0}^{t}T(s)\mathrm{d}s\right\rangle_{3/4,0}\Big|+\pi^{3/2}\Big|L_{e}[\varphi]\int_{0}^{t}\hat{T}_{c}(s,1)\mathrm{d}s\Big|

\displaystyle\leq C\|\tilde{\varphi}\|_{3/4,0}+\pi^{3/2}|L_{e}[\varphi]|\Big|\int_{0}^{t}\hat{T}_{c}(s,1)\mathrm{d}s\Big|\leq C^{\prime}\|\varphi\|_{3/4}

for all
\varphi\in P[0,1]
.
This proves (
C.21
) (thus also (
C.20
)).

By virtue of (
C.3
) we can write (see (
C.16
))

(C.23)

\displaystyle\int_{0}^{t}{\cal b}^{(0)}_{\rm temp}(\varrho,T(s))\mathrm{d}s=T_{0}t-\int_{0}^{t}{\hat{T}_{c}}(s,0)\mathrm{d}s+\sum_{\ell^{\prime}=1}^{+\infty}\frac{(\ell^{\prime}\pi)^{2}c_{\ell^{\prime}}(0)}{(\ell^{\prime}\pi)^{2}+\gamma^{2}\varrho^{4}}\int_{0}^{t}\hat{T}_{c}(s,\ell^{\prime})\mathrm{d}s.

Thanks to (
C.21
) the last term on the right hand side belongs to

L^{2}[0,+\infty)
(in
\varrho
), and the left hand side does as well, cf
(
C.18
). Therefore,
we conclude the first formula of (
C.22
) for
v=0
. The
proof for
v=1
is analogous. ∎

Define

(C.24)

\begin{split}&{\cal E}_{L}(\varphi,\psi):=c_{\rm bulk}\left\langle\varphi,\psi\right\rangle_{3/2,0}+{\cal E}_{K}(\varphi,\psi)\quad\mbox{and}\\
&{\cal E}_{K}(\varphi,\psi):=4\gamma^{1/2}c_{\rm bd}\sum_{v=0,1}\int_{0}^{+\infty}\Phi_{v}(\varrho;\varphi){\cal b}_{v}(\varrho;\psi)\mathrm{d}\varrho,\quad\varrho,\varphi\in P[0,1].\end{split}

Note that

(C.25)

\begin{split}&\sum_{v=0,1}\Phi_{v}(\varrho;\varphi){\cal b}^{(v)}(\psi,\varrho)=F(\tilde{\gamma},\gamma\varrho^{2}){\cal b}^{(0)}(\varrho,\varphi){\cal b}^{(0)}(\psi,\varrho)-G(\tilde{\gamma},\gamma\varrho^{2}){\cal b}^{(1)}(\varrho,\varphi){\cal b}^{(0)}(\psi,\varrho)\\
&+F(\tilde{\gamma},\gamma\varrho^{2}){\cal b}^{(1)}(\varrho,\varphi){\cal b}^{(1)}(\psi,\varrho)-G(\tilde{\gamma},\gamma\varrho^{2}){\cal b}^{(0)}(\varrho,\varphi){\cal b}^{(1)}(\psi,\varrho).\end{split}

Therefore the form
{\cal E}_{K}(\cdot,\cdot)
is symmetric.

Proposition C.6
.

There exists
C_{K}>0
such that

(C.26)

0\leq{\cal E}_{K}(\varphi)\leq C_{K}\|\varphi\|_{3/4,0}^{2},\quad\varphi\in P[0,1],

where
{\cal E}_{K}(\varphi):={\cal E}_{K}(\varphi,\varphi)
.

Proof.

The expression in (
C.25
) is a bilinear form in
{\cal b}^{(v)}(\varrho,\varphi)
,
{\cal b}^{(v^{\prime})}(\psi,\varrho)
, with

M_{\widetilde{\gamma}}(\rho)
- the matrix of coefficients, given by
(
B.10
), that is positive definite. This implies that
0\leq{\cal E}_{K}(\varphi)
.

Recall that
P[0,1]
is the space of all trigonometric polynomials in
cosines.
We can further write

(C.27)

\begin{split}&{\cal E}_{K}(\varphi,\psi):=\sum_{\ell,\ell^{\prime}=1}^{+\infty}\widehat{\cal K}(\ell,\ell^{\prime})\hat{\varphi}_{c}(\ell)\hat{\psi}_{c}(\ell^{\prime}),\quad\varphi,\psi\in P[0,1],\,\mbox{where}\\
&\widehat{\cal K}(\ell,\ell^{\prime}):=2\gamma^{1/2}c_{\rm bd}\sum_{v=0,1}\int_{0}^{+\infty}\frac{(\pi\ell)^{2}c_{\ell}(v)}{(\ell\pi)^{2}+\gamma^{2}\varrho^{4}}\cdot\frac{(\pi\ell^{\prime})^{2}c_{\ell^{\prime}}(v)}{(\ell^{\prime}\pi)^{2}+\gamma^{2}\varrho^{4}}F(\tilde{\gamma},\gamma\varrho^{2})\mathrm{d}\varrho\\
&-\gamma^{1/2}c_{\rm bd}\int_{0}^{+\infty}\frac{(\pi\ell)^{2}c_{\ell}(0)}{(\ell\pi)^{2}+\gamma^{2}\varrho^{4}}\cdot\frac{(\pi\ell^{\prime})^{2}c_{\ell^{\prime}}(1)}{(\ell^{\prime}\pi)^{2}+\gamma^{2}\varrho^{4}}G(\tilde{\gamma},\gamma\varrho^{2})\mathrm{d}\varrho\\
&-\gamma^{1/2}c_{\rm bd}\int_{0}^{+\infty}\frac{(\pi\ell)^{2}c_{\ell}(1)}{(\ell\pi)^{2}+\gamma^{2}\varrho^{4}}\cdot\frac{(\pi\ell^{\prime})^{2}c_{\ell^{\prime}}(0)}{(\ell^{\prime}\pi)^{2}+\gamma^{2}\varrho^{4}}G(\tilde{\gamma},\gamma\varrho^{2})\mathrm{d}\varrho\quad\ell,\ell^{\prime}=1,2,\ldots.\end{split}

Using formula (
C.41
) to integrate over
\varrho
and estimate
(
C.46
) we conclude that there exist constants
C,C^{\prime}>0
such that

\displaystyle{\cal E}_{K}(\varphi)\leq C\sum_{\ell,\ell^{\prime}=1}^{+\infty}\frac{(\pi\ell)^{1/2}(\pi\ell^{\prime})^{1/2}|\hat{\varphi}_{c}(\ell)||\hat{\varphi}_{c}(\ell^{\prime})|}{(\pi\ell)^{1/2}+(\pi\ell^{\prime})^{1/2}}+C\sum_{\ell,\ell^{\prime}=1}^{+\infty}\frac{(\pi\ell)(\pi\ell^{\prime})|\hat{\varphi}_{c}(\ell)||\hat{\varphi}_{c}(\ell^{\prime})|}{\big((\pi\ell)^{1/2}+(\pi\ell^{\prime})^{1/2}\big)(\pi\ell+\pi\ell^{\prime})}

\displaystyle\leq\frac{3}{2}C\sum_{\ell,\ell^{\prime}=1}^{+\infty}\frac{(\pi\ell)^{1/2}(\pi\ell^{\prime})^{1/2}\hat{|\varphi}_{c}(\ell)||\hat{\varphi}_{c}(\ell^{\prime})|}{(\pi\ell)^{1/2}+(\pi\ell^{\prime})^{1/2}}\leq C^{\prime}\sum_{\ell=1}^{+\infty}(\pi\ell)^{3/2}[\hat{\varphi}_{c}(\ell)]^{2},

for all
\varphi\in P[0,1]
and (
C.26
) follows.
∎

Thanks to
Proposition
C.6
the form
{\cal E}_{K}(\cdot,\cdot)
extends to a closed symmetric positive
definite form on
H^{3/4}[0,1]\times H^{3/4}[0,1]
.

C.3.
The end of the proof of Theorem
2.3

Suppose that
T_{m}(\cdot)
,

m=1,2
are two solutions of (
C.19
) in the class of
functions described in Definition
2.2
. Let
\delta T=T_{2}-T_{1}
. Thanks to Lemma
C.5
we have
I(t):=\int_{0}^{t}\delta T(s)\mathrm{d}s\in H_{0}^{3/4}[0,1]
and it satisfies equation

(C.28)

\begin{split}&\langle\varphi,\delta T(t)\rangle_{L^{2}[0,1]}=-c_{\rm bulk}\left\langle\varphi,I(t)\right\rangle_{3/2,0}-c_{\rm bd}{\cal E}_{K}\Big(\varphi,I(t)\Big),\quad\varphi\in H^{3/4}_{0}[0,1].\end{split}

Integrating both sides of (
C.28
) in
t
we conclude
that
I(t)
satisfies

(C.29)

\begin{split}&\langle\varphi,I(t)\rangle_{L^{2}[0,1]}=-c_{\rm bulk}\left\langle\varphi,\int_{0}^{t}I(s)\mathrm{d}s\right\rangle_{3/2,0}-c_{\rm bd}{\cal E}_{K}\Big(\varphi,\int_{0}^{t}I(s)\mathrm{d}s\Big),\quad\varphi\in H^{3/4}_{0}[0,1].\end{split}

Since the function
[0,+\infty)\ni t\mapsto I(t)\in H^{3/4}_{0}[0,1]
is
locally bounded (in
H^{3/4}_{0}[0,1]
) and
continuous in the weak topology
of
L^{2}[0,1]
,
it is also continuous in the strong topology. Substituting
\int_{0}^{t}I(s)\mathrm{d}s
for a
test function
\varphi
in (
C.29
) we obtain

(C.30)

\begin{split}&\frac{\mathrm{d}}{\mathrm{d}t}\Big\|\int_{0}^{t}I(s)\mathrm{d}s\Big\|_{L^{2}[0,1]}^{2}=-c_{\rm bulk}\left\|\int_{0}^{t}I(s)\mathrm{d}s\right\|^{2}_{3/4,0}-c_{\rm bd}{\cal E}_{K}\Big(\int_{0}^{t}I(s)\mathrm{d}s\Big)\leq 0.\end{split}

This proves that
\int_{0}^{t}I(s)\mathrm{d}s\equiv 0
, for all

t\geq 0
, which in turn implies that
\int_{0}^{t}\delta T(s)\mathrm{d}s\equiv 0
,
t\geq 0
, that ends
the proof of Theorem
2.3
.∎

C.4.
Regularity of solutions. Proof of Theorem

2.4

C.4.1.
The case of a homogeneous boundary condition

Consider first the case when
T_{\rm ini}\in H_{0}^{3/4}[0,1]
. Denote
by
{\cal E}_{L}^{o}(\cdot,\cdot)
the form
{\cal E}_{L}(\cdot,\cdot)
, defined
(
C.24
),
restricted to the space
H_{0}^{3/4}[0,1]\times H_{0}^{3/4}[0,1]
. Thanks
to Proposition (
C.6
)
we conclude that the respective quadratic form is equivalent with

\|\cdot\|_{3/4,0}^{2}
on the space
H^{3/4}_{0}[0,1]
. Its
generator
L:{\cal D}(L)\to L^{2}[0,1]
is a self-adjoint operator
whose kernel
{\rm ker}(L)=\{0\}
.
The inverse
L^{-1}
extends therefore to a compact operator on
L^{2}[0,1]
.
Denote by

\vartheta_{m}
,
m=1,2,\ldots
, the orthonormal base of eigenvectors of
L

together with the respective eigenvalues
0<\lambda_{1}\leq\lambda_{2}\leq\ldots
.
In fact, due to the fact that

{\rm c}^{*}\|\varphi\|_{3/4,0}^{2}\geq{\cal E}_{L}^{o}(\varphi)\geq{\rm c}_{\rm bulk}\|\varphi\|_{3/4,0}^{2},\quad\varphi\in H^{3/4}_{0}[0,1]

for some constant
C_{\rm bulk}
,
by the min-max principle, see
[
8
, Theorem X.4.8, p. 908]
, there
exist
C^{*},C_{*}>0
such that

(C.31)

C_{*}m^{3/2}\leq\lambda_{m}\leq C^{*}m^{3/2},\quad m=1,2,\ldots.

As a result we conclude that the trace of
L^{-1}
is finite.

With the form we can associate, in a standard way, a strongly continuous semigroup

\big(Q_{t}\big)
of
non-negative definite, symmetric contractions on
L^{2}_{\iota}[0,1]
. By
the definition
Q_{t}\big(H^{3/4}_{0}[0,1]\big)\subset H^{3/4}_{0}[0,1]
for all
t\geq 0
.
Define
T(t,u):=Q_{t}T_{\rm ini}(u)
. Since
T_{\rm ini}\in H^{3/4}_{0}[0,1]
we have
T(t)\in H^{3/4}_{0}[0,1]
.
Combining this with the definition of the form
{\cal E}_{L}^{o}(\cdot,\cdot)

we conclude that
T(t,u)
is a solution to (
2.18
) in the
sense of Definition (
2.2
).

C.4.2.
Construction of a stationary solution

Denote the subspaces of
L^{2}[0,1]

\displaystyle L^{2}_{e}[0,1]:=\Big[\varphi(u):=\sum_{\ell=0}^{+\infty}\hat{\varphi}_{c}(2\ell)c_{2\ell}(u)\Big],

\displaystyle L^{2}_{o}[0,1]:=\Big[\varphi(u):=\sum_{\ell=1}^{+\infty}\hat{\varphi}_{c}(2\ell-1)c_{2\ell-1}(u)\Big]

and their respective counterparts
H^{3/4}_{\iota}[0,1]
,

\iota=e,o
- subspaces of
H^{3/4}[0,1]
.
Denote by
P^{(\iota)}
the respective orthogonal projections onto
L^{2}_{\iota}[0,1]
.

Consider the symmetric bilinear forms
{\cal E}^{(\iota)}_{L}(\cdot,\cdot)
obtained by restricting
{\cal E}_{L}(\cdot,\cdot)
to
(\varphi,\psi)\in H_{\iota}^{3/4}[0,1]\times H_{\iota}^{3/4}[0,1]
,
\iota=e,o
. They are given by the respective formulas:

\displaystyle{\cal E}^{(e)}_{L}(\varphi,\psi):=c_{\rm bulk}\sum_{\ell=1}^{+\infty}(2\pi\ell)^{3/2}\hat{\varphi}_{c}(2\ell)\hat{\psi}_{c}(2\ell)+2^{3/2}\pi c_{\rm bd}\sum_{\ell,\ell^{\prime}=1}^{+\infty}\widehat{\cal K}_{e}(\ell,\ell^{\prime})\hat{\varphi}_{c}(2\ell)\hat{\psi}_{c}(2\ell^{\prime}),

(C.32)

\displaystyle{\cal E}^{(o)}_{L}(\varphi,\psi):=c_{\rm bulk}\sum_{\ell=1}^{+\infty}\big(\pi(2\ell-1)\big)^{3/2}\hat{\varphi}_{c}(2\ell-1)\hat{\psi}_{c}(2\ell-1)

\displaystyle+2^{3/2}\pi c_{\rm bd}\sum_{\ell,\ell^{\prime}=1}^{+\infty}\widehat{\cal K}_{o}(\ell,\ell^{\prime})\hat{\varphi}_{c}(2\ell-1)\hat{\psi}_{c}(2\ell^{\prime}-1),

Here

\widehat{\cal K}_{e}(\ell,\ell^{\prime}):=\widehat{\cal K}(2\ell,2\ell^{\prime})
and
\widehat{\cal K}_{o}(\ell,\ell^{\prime}):=\widehat{\cal K}(2\ell-1,2\ell^{\prime}-1).

Note that, see (
C.27
),
{\cal K}(2\ell,2\ell^{\prime}+1)=0
for all

\ell,\ell^{\prime}=0,1,\ldots
. Therefore, for any
\varphi\in H^{3/4}_{\iota}[0,1]
and
\psi\in H^{3/4}[0,1]

{\cal E}_{L}(\varphi,\psi)={\cal E}^{(\iota)}_{L}(\varphi,P^{(\iota)}\psi),\quad\iota\in\{e,o\}.

The quadratic forms
{\cal E}^{(\iota)}_{L}(\cdot)
are equivalent with

\|\cdot\|_{3/4,0}^{2}
on the respective spaces
H_{\iota}^{3/4}[0,1]
,

\iota=e,o
. Their corresponding generators are
self-adjoint operators
L^{(\iota)}:{\cal D}\big(L^{(\iota)}\big)\to L^{2}_{\iota}[0,1]
, that are given by

\displaystyle{\cal D}\big(L^{(\iota)}\big):=\Big[\varphi:\,{\cal E}^{(\iota)}_{L}(\varphi,\cdot)\,\mbox{extends to a bounded lin. funct. on
}L^{2}_{\iota}[0,1]\Big],

\displaystyle{\cal E}^{(\iota)}_{L}(\varphi,\psi)=-\langle L^{(\iota)}\varphi,\psi\rangle_{L^{2}[0,1]},\quad\psi\in H^{3/4}_{\iota}[0,1].

The null space of
L^{(e)}
is
{\rm span}\,(1)
, while
L^{(o)}

is

1-1
. The inverses
\big(L^{(e)}\big)^{-1}
, restricted to
{\rm span}\,(1)^{\perp}
, and
\big(L^{(o)}\big)^{-1}
are well defined
trace class symmetric operators.
Denote by

\vartheta^{(\iota)}_{m}
,
m=1,2,\ldots
, the orthonormal bases of eigenvectors of
L^{(\iota)}

together with the respective eigenvalues
0<\lambda^{(\iota)}_{1}\leq\lambda^{(\iota)}_{2}\leq\ldots
.
We let
\vartheta_{0}^{(e)}(u)\equiv 1
and
\lambda_{0}^{(e)}=0
and by
convention
\vartheta_{0}^{(o)}(u)\equiv 0
and
\lambda_{0}^{(o)}=0
.
In fact,
by the min-max principle, there
exist
C^{*},C_{*}>0
such that

(C.33)

C_{*}m^{3/2}\leq\lambda^{(\iota)}_{m}\leq C^{*}m^{3/2},\quad m=1,2,\ldots.

In addition, since
\vartheta^{(\iota)}_{m}\in{\cal D}\big(L^{(\iota)}\big)
we have
\vartheta^{(\iota)}_{m}\in H^{3/4}[0,1]\subset C[0,1]
. Furthermore,

(C.34)

|{\cal E}^{(\iota)}_{L}(\varphi,\psi)|\leq C\|\varphi\|_{3/2,0}\|\psi\|_{3/2,0},\quad\varphi,\psi\in H^{3/4}_{\iota}[0,1],\,\iota=e,o.

Let

\delta_{e}=\frac{1}{2}(\delta_{0}+\delta_{1})
and
\delta_{o}=\frac{1}{2}(\delta_{0}-\delta_{1}).

The above distributions belong to
H^{-3/4}[0,1]
- the dual to

H^{3/4}[0,1]
, as well as to
H^{-3/4}_{e}[0,1]
and

H^{-3/4}_{o}[0,1]
- the
duals to
H^{3/4}_{e}[0,1]
and
H^{3/4}_{o}[0,1]
, respectively. Here

(C.35)

H^{-3/4}_{\iota}[0,1]=\Big[\varphi=\sum_{m=1}^{+\infty}\tilde{\varphi}_{m}\vartheta_{m}^{(\iota)}:\,\|\varphi\|_{-3/4,\iota}^{2}:=\sum_{m=1}^{+\infty}\frac{\tilde{\varphi}_{m}^{2}}{\lambda_{m}^{(\iota)}}<+\infty\Big],\quad\iota\in\{o,e\}.

We have

\displaystyle\langle\delta_{\iota},\varphi\rangle=\bar{\varphi}^{(\iota)},\quad\mbox{where}\quad\bar{\varphi}^{(e)}=\frac{1}{2}\big(\varphi(0)+\varphi(1)\big),

\displaystyle\bar{\varphi}^{(o)}=\frac{1}{2}\big(\varphi(0)-\varphi(1)\big),\quad\varphi\in H^{3/4}[0,1].

Then,

\delta_{\iota}(u)=\sum_{m=0}^{+\infty}\bar{\vartheta}_{m}^{(\iota)}\vartheta_{m}^{(\iota)}(u)
.

Let

(C.36)

\displaystyle\bar{T}:=\frac{1}{2}(T_{0}+T_{1})\quad\mbox{and}\quad{\scriptstyle\Delta}T:=T_{0}-T_{1}.

Let

(C.37)

\vartheta_{s}(u):=\sum_{m=1}^{+\infty}\frac{{\scriptstyle\Delta}\vartheta_{m}^{(o)}\vartheta_{m}^{(o)}(u)}{2\lambda_{m}^{(o)}}.

Note that at least for some
m_{0}\geq 1
we have

{\scriptstyle\Delta}\vartheta_{m_{0}}^{(o)}\not=0
. Otherwise, we would have

\vartheta_{m}^{(o)}(0)=\vartheta_{m}^{(o)}(1)
and also

\vartheta_{m}^{(o)}(0)=-\vartheta_{m}^{(o)}(1)
, for all
m=1,2,\ldots
. This
would imply that any
\varphi\in H^{3/4}_{o}[0,1]
, thus

H^{3/4}_{o}[0,1]\subset H^{3/4}_{0}[0,1]
, which is obviously false.
We have therefore

(C.38)

\displaystyle 0<{\scriptstyle\Delta}\vartheta_{s}=\sum_{m=1}^{+\infty}\frac{\big({\scriptstyle\Delta}\vartheta_{m}^{(o)}\big)^{2}}{2\lambda_{m}^{(o)}}=\frac{1}{2}\|\delta_{o}\|_{-3/4,o}^{2}<+\infty,

since, see (
C.35
),

\delta_{o}=\sum_{m=1}^{+\infty}({\scriptstyle\Delta}\vartheta_{m}^{(o)})\vartheta_{m}^{(o)}\in H^{-3/4}_{o}[0,1].

In addition,
\vartheta_{s}(0)=-\vartheta_{s}(1)=\frac{1}{2}{\scriptstyle\Delta}\vartheta_{s}
. Note that
\vartheta_{s}\in H^{3/4}[0,1]
as, thanks to (
C.38
),

(C.39)

\|\vartheta_{s}\|^{2}_{3/4,0}=\sum_{m=1}^{+\infty}\frac{({\scriptstyle\Delta}\vartheta_{m}^{(o)})^{2}}{4\lambda_{m}^{(o)}}<+\infty.

Let

(C.40)

\displaystyle T_{s}(u):=\bar{T}+\frac{{\scriptstyle\Delta}T}{{\scriptstyle\Delta}\vartheta_{s}}\vartheta_{s}(u).

Since

{\scriptstyle\Delta}\vartheta_{m}^{(o)}=2\vartheta_{m}^{(o)}(0)=-2\vartheta_{m}^{(o)}(1)
,
we have
T_{s}(v)=T_{v}
,
v=0,1
.
Substitute
T_{s}
to the right hand side of
(
C.19
). Then, for any
\varphi\in H^{3/4}_{0}[0,1]

\displaystyle\langle|\Delta|^{3/4}\varphi,T_{s}\rangle_{L^{2}[0,1]}+{\cal E}_{K}(\varphi,T_{s})=\frac{{\scriptstyle\Delta}T}{{\scriptstyle\Delta}\vartheta_{s}}\sum_{m=1}^{+\infty}\langle\delta_{o},\vartheta_{m}^{(o)}\rangle\langle\vartheta_{m}^{(o)},\varphi\rangle_{L^{2}[0,1]}=\frac{{\scriptstyle\Delta}T{\scriptstyle\Delta}\varphi}{2{\scriptstyle\Delta}\vartheta_{s}}=0,

which shows that
T_{s}(t,u)\equiv T_{s}(u)
given by (
C.40
) is a stationary solution of
(
C.19
). To see that condition (
2.19
) is satisfied we can
use the uniqueness argument made in Section
C.3
.

C.4.3.
The case of an inhomogeneous boundary condition

Finally assume that
T_{\rm ini}\in H^{3/4}[0,1]
. Let
T_{v}:=T_{\rm ini}(v)
,
v=0,1
. Suppose that
T_{s}
is the stationary solution
constructed in Section
C.4.2
. Let
\tilde{T}(t)
be the solution
of the homogeneous problem, corresponding to the initial data
\tilde{T}_{\rm ini}:=T_{\rm ini}-T_{s}\in H^{3/4}_{0}[0,1]
. Thanks to the argument of Section

C.4.1
we have
\tilde{T}(t)\in H^{3/4}_{0}[0,1]
. Then,

T(t)=T_{s}+\tilde{T}(t)\in H^{3/4}[0,1]
is the unique solution of
(
2.18
) in the sense of Definition (
2.2
), thanks to the uniqueness argument made in Section
C.3
.
∎

C.5.
Some technical results

Lemma C.7
.

The following formulas hold: for any
a_{j},b_{j}>0
,
j=1,2
we have

(C.41)

\displaystyle\int_{0}^{+\infty}\frac{\mathrm{d}\lambda}{a_{1}^{2}+b_{1}^{2}\lambda^{4}}

\displaystyle=\frac{\pi}{(2a_{1})^{3/2}b_{1}^{1/2}},\

\displaystyle\int_{0}^{+\infty}\frac{\mathrm{d}\lambda}{(a^{2}_{1}+b^{2}_{1}\lambda^{4})(a^{2}_{2}+b^{2}_{2}\lambda^{4})}

\displaystyle=\frac{\pi\big(a_{1}b_{2}+a_{2}b_{1}+(a_{1}b_{1}a_{2}b_{2})^{1/2}\big)}{2^{3/2}(a_{1}a_{2})^{3/2}[(a_{1}b_{2})^{1/2}+(a_{2}b_{1})^{1/2}](a_{1}b_{2}+a_{2}b_{1})}.

Proof.

See
[
10
, formula (3.112), p. 253]
.
∎

Lemma C.8
.

Suppose that
\alpha,\beta>0
are such that
\alpha+2\beta=1
(then
1/2>\beta>0
). Then, there exists
C>0
such that

(C.42)

0\leq\int_{0}^{+\infty}\int_{0}^{+\infty}\frac{f(x)f(y)\mathrm{d}x\mathrm{d}y}{(x^{\alpha}+y^{\alpha})x^{\beta}y^{\beta}}\leq C\int_{0}^{+\infty}f^{2}(x)\mathrm{d}x

for all
f\in L^{2}(0,+\infty)
.

Proof.

We have

\displaystyle\int_{0}^{+\infty}\int_{0}^{+\infty}\frac{f(x)f(y)\mathrm{d}x\mathrm{d}y}{(x^{\alpha}+y^{\alpha})x^{\beta}y^{\beta}}=\int_{0}^{+\infty}\mathrm{d}\rho\Big(\int_{0}^{+\infty}\frac{e^{-\rho x^{\alpha}}f(x)\mathrm{d}x}{x^{\beta}}\Big)^{2}.

Changing variables
\rho^{\prime}=1/\rho
we can write the right hand side
as

\displaystyle\int_{0}^{+\infty}\frac{\mathrm{d}\rho}{\rho^{2}}\Big(\int_{0}^{+\infty}\frac{e^{-x^{\alpha}/\rho}f(x)\mathrm{d}x}{x^{\beta}}\Big)^{2}\leq\int_{0}^{+\infty}\frac{\mathrm{d}\rho}{\rho^{2}}\Big(\sum_{n=0}^{+\infty}e^{-n^{\alpha}}\int_{n\rho^{1/\alpha}}^{(n+1)\rho^{1/\alpha}}\frac{f(x)\mathrm{d}x}{x^{\beta}}\Big)^{2}.

Since

C_{\alpha}:=\sum_{n=0}^{+\infty}e^{-n^{\alpha}}<+\infty

the utmost right hand side can be estimated by

C_{\alpha}{\rm I}_{n}
, where

\displaystyle{\rm I}_{n}=\int_{0}^{+\infty}\frac{\mathrm{d}\rho}{\rho^{2}}\Big(\int_{n\rho^{1/\alpha}}^{(n+1)\rho^{1/\alpha}}\frac{f(x)\mathrm{d}x}{x^{\beta}}\Big)^{2}=\int_{0}^{+\infty}\frac{\mathrm{d}\rho}{\rho^{2}}\Big(\int_{0}^{\rho^{1/\alpha}}\frac{f(x+n\rho^{1/\alpha})\mathrm{d}x}{(x+n\rho^{1/\alpha})^{\beta}}\Big)^{2}

(C.43)

\displaystyle\leq\int_{0}^{+\infty}\frac{\mathrm{d}\rho}{\rho^{2}}\Big(\int_{0}^{\rho^{1/\alpha}}\frac{f(x+n\rho^{1/\alpha})\mathrm{d}x}{x^{\beta}}\Big)^{2}.

To estimate the utmost right hand side we show that

(C.44)

\begin{split}&{\rm I}[g]\leq C\|g\|_{L^{2}(0,+\infty)}^{2},\quad g\in L^{2}(0,+\infty),\quad\mbox{where}\\
&{\rm I}[g]=\int_{0}^{+\infty}\frac{\mathrm{d}\rho}{\rho^{2}}\Big(\int_{0}^{\rho^{1/\alpha}}\frac{g(x)\mathrm{d}x}{x^{\beta}}\Big)^{2}.\end{split}

This combined with (
C.43
) yields an estimate

(C.45)

\displaystyle\int_{0}^{+\infty}\frac{\mathrm{d}\rho}{\rho^{2}}\Big(\int_{0}^{+\infty}\frac{e^{-x^{\alpha}/\rho}f(x)\mathrm{d}x}{x^{\beta}}\Big)^{2}\leq CC_{\alpha}\|f\|_{L^{2}(0,+\infty)}^{2},

which ends the proof of (
C.42
).
The only remaining part is to show (
C.44
)

Proof of (
C.44
)

We omit the notation for a function writing the functional
{\rm I}
.
Changing variables
\rho:=(\rho^{\prime})^{\alpha}
we obtain

\displaystyle{\rm I}=\alpha\int_{0}^{+\infty}\frac{\mathrm{d}\rho}{\rho^{\alpha+1}}\Big(\int_{0}^{\rho}\frac{g(x)\mathrm{d}x}{x^{\beta}}\Big)^{2}

\displaystyle=\alpha\int_{0}^{+\infty}\mathrm{d}\rho\Big(\frac{1}{\rho^{(\alpha+1)/2}}\int_{1}^{\rho}\frac{g(x)\mathrm{d}x}{x^{\beta}}\Big)^{2}=\alpha\int_{0}^{+\infty}\mathrm{d}\rho\Big(\frac{1}{\rho^{1-\beta}}\int_{1}^{\rho}\frac{g(x)\mathrm{d}x}{x^{\beta}}\Big)^{2}.

Recall the Hardy inequality: if
\beta+\frac{1}{p}<1
and
p>1
,
\beta>0
, then

\displaystyle\int_{0}^{+\infty}\mathrm{d}\rho\Big(\frac{1}{\rho^{1-\beta}}\int_{1}^{\rho}\frac{g(x)\mathrm{d}x}{x^{\beta}}\Big)^{p}\leq\frac{1}{1-\beta-1/p}\int_{0}^{+\infty}|g(x)|^{p}\mathrm{d}x,

see
[
27
, A.4, p. 272]
.
Applying it in our case we get

\displaystyle{\rm I}\leq\frac{\alpha\|g\|_{L^{2}(0,+\infty)}^{2}}{1/2-\beta}

and estimate (
C.44
) follows.
∎

Here is an obvious corollary of the lemma.

Corollary C.9
.

Under the assumptions of Lemma
C.8
there exists a constant
C>0
such that

(C.46)

0\leq\sum_{\ell,\ell^{\prime}=1}^{+\infty}\frac{a_{\ell}a_{\ell^{\prime}}}{(\ell^{\alpha}+(\ell^{\prime})^{\alpha})\ell^{\beta}(\ell^{\prime})^{\beta}}\leq C\sum_{\ell=1}^{+\infty}a^{2}_{\ell}

for all
(a_{\ell})\in\ell^{2}
.

Supplemental Materials

The following material is considered to be a supplement for the
foregoing paper, presenting the calculations (referred to as being
straighforward, but tedious) that have not been performed in the paper.

Appendix
Supplement

1. Proof of Proposition
4.1

Let

\displaystyle{\cal H}_{n}^{(2)}(t)

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{2(n+1)}\sum_{x,x^{\prime}=0}^{n}\bigg\{\left(\mathbb{E}_{n}\left[p_{x}(t)p_{x^{\prime}}(t)\right]\right)^{2}+\left(\mathbb{E}_{n}\left[r_{x}(t)r_{x^{\prime}}(t)\right]\right)^{2}

\displaystyle\quad\qquad\qquad\qquad+2\left(\mathbb{E}_{n}\left[p_{x}(t)r_{x^{\prime}}(t)\right]\right)^{2}\bigg\}.

We have

\displaystyle\frac{\mathrm{d}}{\mathrm{d}t}{\cal H}_{n}^{(2)}(t)=I(t)+{\rm II}(t)+{\rm III}(t),\quad\mbox{with}

\displaystyle I(t):=\frac{1}{n+1}\sum_{x,x^{\prime}=0}^{n}\left(\mathbb{E}_{n}\left[p_{x}(t)p_{x^{\prime}}(t)\right]\right)\frac{\mathrm{d}}{\mathrm{d}t}\left(\mathbb{E}_{n}\left[p_{x}(t)p_{x^{\prime}}(t)\right]\right)

\displaystyle{\rm II}(t):=\frac{1}{n+1}\sum_{x,x^{\prime}=1}^{n}\left(\mathbb{E}_{n}\left[r_{x}(t)r_{x^{\prime}}(t)\right]\right)\frac{\mathrm{d}}{\mathrm{d}t}\left(\mathbb{E}_{n}\left[r_{x}(t)r_{x^{\prime}}(t)\right]\right)

\displaystyle{\rm III}(t):=\frac{2}{n+1}\sum_{x=0}^{n}\sum_{x^{\prime}=1}^{n}\left(\mathbb{E}_{n}\left[p_{x}(t)r_{x^{\prime}}(t)\right]\right)\frac{\mathrm{d}}{\mathrm{d}t}\left(\mathbb{E}_{n}\left[p_{x}(t)r_{x^{\prime}}(t)\right]\right).

Next, for
x,x^{\prime}=1,\ldots,n-1
we have

\displaystyle\frac{\mathrm{d}}{\mathrm{d}t}\mathbb{E}_{n}\left[r_{x}(t)r_{x^{\prime}}^{\prime}(t)\right]=\mathbb{E}_{n}\left[\dot{r}_{x}(t)r_{x^{\prime}}(t)\right]+\mathbb{E}_{n}\left[{r}_{x}(t)\dot{r}_{x^{\prime}}(t)\right]

\displaystyle=n^{3/2}\Big\{\mathbb{E}_{n}\left[\nabla^{\star}p_{x}(t)r_{x^{\prime}}(t)\right]+\mathbb{E}_{n}\left[{r}_{x}(t)\nabla^{\star}p_{x^{\prime}}(t)\right]\Big\},

\displaystyle\frac{\mathrm{d}}{\mathrm{d}t}\left(\mathbb{E}_{n}\left[p_{x}(t)r_{x^{\prime}}(t)\right]\right)=n^{3/2}\Big\{\mathbb{E}_{n}\left[\nabla r_{x}r_{x^{\prime}}(t)\right]+\gamma\mathbb{E}_{n}\left[\Delta_{\rm N}p_{x}(t)r_{x^{\prime}}(t)\right]

\displaystyle+\mathbb{E}_{n}\left[p_{x}(t)\nabla^{\star}p_{x^{\prime}}(t)\right]\Big\}

and

\displaystyle\mathrm{d}\left(\mathbb{E}_{n}\left[p_{x}(t)p_{x^{\prime}}(t)\right]\right)=\left(\mathbb{E}_{n}\left[\mathrm{d}p_{x}(t)p_{x^{\prime}}(t)\right]\right)+\left(\mathbb{E}_{n}\left[p_{x}(t)\mathrm{d}p_{x^{\prime}}(t)\right]\right)

\displaystyle+\left(\mathbb{E}_{n}\left[\mathrm{d}p_{x}(t)\mathrm{d}p_{x^{\prime}}(t)\right]\right)

\displaystyle=n^{3/2}\Big\{\mathbb{E}_{n}\left[\nabla r_{x}(t)p_{x^{\prime}}(t)\right]+\gamma\mathbb{E}_{n}\left[\Delta_{\rm N}p_{x}(t)p_{x^{\prime}}(t)\right]+\mathbb{E}_{n}\left[\nabla r_{x^{\prime}}(t)p_{x}(t)\right]

\displaystyle+\gamma\mathbb{E}_{n}\left[\Delta_{\rm N}p_{x^{\prime}}(t)p_{x}(t)\right]-\delta_{x^{\prime}=x+1}\gamma\mathbb{E}_{n}\left[\nabla^{\star}p_{x+1}(t)\nabla^{\star}p_{x^{\prime}}(t)\right]

\displaystyle-\delta_{x=x^{\prime}+1}\gamma\mathbb{E}_{n}\left[\nabla^{\star}p_{x^{\prime}+1}(t)\nabla^{\star}p_{x}(t)\right]+\delta_{x^{\prime}=x}\gamma\mathbb{E}_{n}\Big[\big(\nabla^{\star}p_{x+1}(t)\big)^{2}+\big(\nabla^{\star}p_{x}(t)\big)^{2}\Big]\Big\}\mathrm{d}t

For
x=0
,
x^{\prime}=1,\ldots,n-1

we have

\displaystyle\frac{\mathrm{d}}{\mathrm{d}t}\mathbb{E}_{n}\left[r_{0}(t)r_{x^{\prime}}(t)\right]=0,

\displaystyle\frac{\mathrm{d}}{\mathrm{d}t}\left(\mathbb{E}_{n}\left[p_{0}(t)r_{x^{\prime}}(t)\right]\right)=n^{3/2}\Big\{\mathbb{E}_{n}\left[\nabla r_{0}r_{x^{\prime}}(t)\right]+\gamma\mathbb{E}_{n}\left[\Delta_{\rm N}p_{0}(t)r_{x^{\prime}}(t)\right]

\displaystyle+\mathbb{E}_{n}\left[p_{0}(t)\nabla^{\star}p_{x^{\prime}}(t)\right]-\tilde{\gamma}\mathbb{E}_{n}\left[p_{0}(t)r_{x^{\prime}}(t)\right]\Big\}

and

\displaystyle\mathrm{d}\left(\mathbb{E}_{n}\left[p_{0}(t)p_{x^{\prime}}(t)\right]\right)=\left(\mathbb{E}_{n}\left[\mathrm{d}p_{0}(t)p_{x^{\prime}}(t)\right]\right)+\left(\mathbb{E}_{n}\left[p_{0}(t)\mathrm{d}p_{x^{\prime}}(t)\right]\right)

\displaystyle+\left(\mathbb{E}_{n}\left[\mathrm{d}p_{0}(t)\mathrm{d}p_{x^{\prime}}(t)\right]\right)

\displaystyle=n^{3/2}\Big\{\mathbb{E}_{n}\left[\nabla r_{0}(t)p_{x^{\prime}}(t)\right]+\gamma\mathbb{E}_{n}\left[\Delta_{\rm N}p_{0}(t)p_{x^{\prime}}(t)\right]-\tilde{\gamma}\mathbb{E}_{n}\left[p_{0}(t)p_{x^{\prime}}(t)\right]

\displaystyle+\mathbb{E}_{n}\left[\nabla r_{x^{\prime}}(t)p_{0}(t)\right]+\gamma\mathbb{E}_{n}\left[\Delta_{\rm N}p_{x^{\prime}}(t)p_{0}(t)\right]-\delta_{x^{\prime}=1}\gamma\mathbb{E}_{n}\left[\Big(\nabla^{\star}p_{1}(t)\Big)^{2}\right]\Big\}\mathrm{d}t

For
x=0
,
x^{\prime}=n

we have

\displaystyle\frac{\mathrm{d}}{\mathrm{d}t}\mathbb{E}_{n}\left[r_{0}(t)r_{n}(t)\right]=0,

\displaystyle\frac{\mathrm{d}}{\mathrm{d}t}\left(\mathbb{E}_{n}\left[p_{0}(t)r_{n}(t)\right]\right)=n^{3/2}\Big\{\mathbb{E}_{n}\left[\nabla r_{0}r_{n}(t)\right]+\gamma\mathbb{E}_{n}\left[\Delta_{\rm N}p_{0}(t)r_{n}(t)\right]

\displaystyle+\mathbb{E}_{n}\left[p_{0}(t)\nabla^{\star}p_{n}(t)\right]-\tilde{\gamma}\mathbb{E}_{n}\left[p_{0}(t)r_{n}(t)\right]\Big\}

and

\displaystyle\mathrm{d}\left(\mathbb{E}_{n}\left[p_{0}(t)p_{n}(t)\right]\right)=\left(\mathbb{E}_{n}\left[\mathrm{d}p_{0}(t)p_{n}(t)\right]\right)+\left(\mathbb{E}_{n}\left[p_{0}(t)\mathrm{d}p_{n}(t)\right]\right)

\displaystyle=n^{3/2}\Big\{\mathbb{E}_{n}\left[\nabla r_{0}(t)p_{n}(t)\right]+\gamma\mathbb{E}_{n}\left[\Delta_{\rm N}p_{0}(t)p_{n}(t)\right]-\tilde{\gamma}\mathbb{E}_{n}\left[p_{0}(t)p_{n}(t)\right]

\displaystyle+\mathbb{E}_{n}\left[\nabla r_{n}(t)p_{0}(t)\right]+\gamma\mathbb{E}_{n}\left[\Delta_{\rm N}p_{n}(t)p_{0}(t)\right]\Big\}\mathrm{d}t

For
x=x^{\prime}=0

we have

\displaystyle\frac{\mathrm{d}}{\mathrm{d}t}\mathbb{E}_{n}\left[\Big(r_{0}(t)\Big)^{2}\right]=0,

\displaystyle\frac{\mathrm{d}}{\mathrm{d}t}\left(\mathbb{E}_{n}\left[p_{0}(t)r_{0}(t)\right]\right)=0

and

\displaystyle\mathrm{d}\left(\mathbb{E}_{n}\left[\Big(p_{0}(t)\Big)^{2}\right]\right)=2\left(\mathbb{E}_{n}\left[\mathrm{d}p_{0}(t)p_{0}(t)\right]\right)+\left(\mathbb{E}_{n}\left[\Big(\mathrm{d}p_{0}(t)\Big)^{2}\right]\right)

\displaystyle=n^{3/2}\Big\{\mathbb{E}_{n}\left[2\nabla r_{0}(t)p_{0}(t)\right]+2\gamma\mathbb{E}_{n}\left[\Delta_{\rm N}p_{0}(t)p_{0}(t)\right]-2\tilde{\gamma}\mathbb{E}_{n}\left[\Big(p_{0}(t)\Big)^{2}\right]

\displaystyle+2\tilde{\gamma}T_{L}+\gamma\mathbb{E}_{n}\left[\Big(\nabla^{\star}p_{1}(t)\Big)^{2}\right]\Big\}\mathrm{d}t

For
x=n
,
x^{\prime}=1,\ldots,n-1

we have

\displaystyle\frac{\mathrm{d}}{\mathrm{d}t}\mathbb{E}_{n}\left[r_{n}(t)r_{x^{\prime}}(t)\right]=\mathbb{E}_{n}\left[\dot{r}_{n}(t)r_{x^{\prime}}(t)\right]+\mathbb{E}_{n}\left[{r}_{n}(t)\dot{r}_{x^{\prime}}(t)\right]

\displaystyle=n^{3/2}\Big\{\mathbb{E}_{n}\left[\nabla^{\star}p_{n}(t)r_{x^{\prime}}(t)\right]+\mathbb{E}_{n}\left[{r}_{n}(t)\nabla^{\star}p_{x^{\prime}}(t)\right]\Big\},

\displaystyle\frac{\mathrm{d}}{\mathrm{d}t}\left(\mathbb{E}_{n}\left[p_{n}(t)r_{x^{\prime}}(t)\right]\right)=n^{3/2}\Big\{\mathbb{E}_{n}\left[\nabla r_{n}r_{x^{\prime}}(t)\right]+\gamma\mathbb{E}_{n}\left[\Delta_{\rm N}p_{n}(t)r_{x^{\prime}}(t)\right]

\displaystyle+\mathbb{E}_{n}\left[p_{n}(t)\nabla^{\star}p_{x^{\prime}}(t)\right]-\tilde{\gamma}\mathbb{E}_{n}\left[p_{n}(t)r_{x^{\prime}}(t)\right]\Big\}

and

\displaystyle\mathrm{d}\left(\mathbb{E}_{n}\left[p_{n}(t)p_{x^{\prime}}(t)\right]\right)=\left(\mathbb{E}_{n}\left[\mathrm{d}p_{n}(t)p_{x^{\prime}}(t)\right]\right)+\left(\mathbb{E}_{n}\left[p_{n}(t)\mathrm{d}p_{x^{\prime}}(t)\right]\right)

\displaystyle+\left(\mathbb{E}_{n}\left[\mathrm{d}p_{n}(t)\mathrm{d}p_{x^{\prime}}(t)\right]\right)

\displaystyle=n^{3/2}\Big\{\mathbb{E}_{n}\left[\nabla r_{n}(t)p_{x^{\prime}}(t)\right]+\gamma\mathbb{E}_{n}\left[\Delta_{\rm N}p_{n}(t)p_{x^{\prime}}(t)\right]-\tilde{\gamma}\mathbb{E}_{n}\left[p_{n}(t)p_{x^{\prime}}(t)\right]

\displaystyle+\mathbb{E}_{n}\left[\nabla r_{x^{\prime}}(t)p_{n}(t)\right]+\gamma\mathbb{E}_{n}\left[\Delta_{\rm N}p_{x^{\prime}}(t)p_{n}(t)\right]-\delta_{x^{\prime}=n-1}\gamma\mathbb{E}_{n}\left[\Big(\nabla^{\star}p_{n}(t)\Big)^{2}\right]\Big\}\mathrm{d}t

For
x=x^{\prime}=n

we have

\displaystyle\frac{\mathrm{d}}{\mathrm{d}t}\mathbb{E}_{n}\left[\Big(r_{n}(t)\Big)^{2}\right]=2\mathbb{E}_{n}\left[\dot{r}_{n}(t)r_{n}(t)\right]=n^{3/2}\mathbb{E}_{n}\left[\nabla^{\star}p_{n}(t)r_{n}(t)\right],

\displaystyle\frac{\mathrm{d}}{\mathrm{d}t}\left(\mathbb{E}_{n}\left[p_{n}(t)r_{n}(t)\right]\right)=n^{3/2}\Big\{\mathbb{E}_{n}\left[\nabla r_{n}r_{n}(t)\right]+\gamma\mathbb{E}_{n}\left[\Delta_{\rm N}p_{n}(t)r_{n}(t)\right]

\displaystyle+\mathbb{E}_{n}\left[p_{n}(t)\nabla^{\star}p_{n}(t)\right]-\tilde{\gamma}\mathbb{E}_{n}\left[p_{n}(t)r_{n}(t)\right]\Big\}

and

\displaystyle\mathrm{d}\left(\mathbb{E}_{n}\left[\Big(p_{n}(t)\Big)^{2}\right]\right)=2\mathbb{E}_{n}\left[\mathrm{d}p_{n}(t)p_{n}(t)\right]+\mathbb{E}_{n}\left[\Big(\mathrm{d}p_{n}(t)\Big)^{2}\right]

\displaystyle=n^{3/2}\Big\{2\mathbb{E}_{n}\left[\nabla r_{n}(t)p_{n}(t)\right]+2\gamma\mathbb{E}_{n}\left[\Delta_{\rm N}p_{n}(t)p_{n}(t)\right]-2\tilde{\gamma}\mathbb{E}_{n}\left[\Big(p_{n}(t)\Big)^{2}\right]

\displaystyle+2\tilde{\gamma}T_{R}+\gamma\mathbb{E}_{n}\left[\Big(\nabla^{\star}p_{n}(t)\Big)^{2}\right]\Big\}\mathrm{d}t

Calculation for the
p-p
covariance

We have

\displaystyle I(t)=\frac{1}{n+1}\sum_{x,x^{\prime}=0}^{n}\left(\mathbb{E}_{n}\left[p_{x}(t)p_{x^{\prime}}(t)\right]\right)\frac{\mathrm{d}}{\mathrm{d}t}\left(\mathbb{E}_{n}\left[p_{x}(t)p_{x^{\prime}}(t)\right]\right)=\frac{1}{n+1}\sum_{j=1}^{6}I_{j}(t),

with

\displaystyle I_{1}(t)=\sum_{x,x^{\prime}=1}^{n-1}\left(\mathbb{E}_{n}\left[p_{x}(t)p_{x^{\prime}}(t)\right]\right)\frac{\mathrm{d}}{\mathrm{d}t}\left(\mathbb{E}_{n}\left[p_{x}(t)p_{x^{\prime}}(t)\right]\right)

\displaystyle=n^{3/2}\sum_{x,x^{\prime}=1}^{n-1}\mathbb{E}_{n}\left[p_{x}(t)p_{x^{\prime}}(t)\right]\mathbb{E}_{n}\left[\nabla r_{x}(t)p_{x^{\prime}}(t)\right]

\displaystyle+n^{3/2}\sum_{x,x^{\prime}=1}^{n-1}\mathbb{E}_{n}\left[p_{x}(t)p_{x^{\prime}}(t)\right]\mathbb{E}_{n}\left[\nabla r_{x^{\prime}}(t)p_{x}(t)\right]

\displaystyle+\gamma n^{3/2}\sum_{x,x^{\prime}=1}^{n-1}\mathbb{E}_{n}\left[p_{x}(t)p_{x^{\prime}}(t)\right]\mathbb{E}_{n}\left[\Delta_{\rm N}p_{x}(t)p_{x^{\prime}}(t)\right]

\displaystyle+\gamma n^{3/2}\sum_{x,x^{\prime}=1}^{n-1}\mathbb{E}_{n}\left[p_{x}(t)p_{x^{\prime}}(t)\right]\mathbb{E}_{n}\left[\Delta_{\rm N}p_{x^{\prime}}(t)p_{x}(t)\right]

\displaystyle+\gamma n^{3/2}\sum_{x=1}^{n-1}\mathbb{E}_{n}\left[\left(p_{x}(t)\right)^{2}\right]\mathbb{E}_{n}\Big[\big(\nabla^{\star}p_{x+1}(t)\big)^{2}+\big(\nabla^{\star}p_{x}(t)\big)^{2}\Big]

\displaystyle-2\gamma n^{3/2}\sum_{x=1}^{n-2}\mathbb{E}_{n}\left[\left(p_{x}(t)\right)\left(p_{x+1}(t)\right)\right]\mathbb{E}_{n}\left[\big(\nabla^{\star}p_{x+1}(t)\big)^{2}\right]

\displaystyle I_{2}(t)=\sum_{x^{\prime}=1}^{n-1}\left(\mathbb{E}_{n}\left[p_{0}(t)p_{x^{\prime}}(t)\right]\right)\frac{\mathrm{d}}{\mathrm{d}t}\left(\mathbb{E}_{n}\left[p_{0}(t)p_{x^{\prime}}(t)\right]\right)

\displaystyle+\sum_{x=1}^{n-1}\left(\mathbb{E}_{n}\left[p_{0}(t)p_{x}(t)\right]\right)\frac{\mathrm{d}}{\mathrm{d}t}\left(\mathbb{E}_{n}\left[p_{0}(t)p_{x}(t)\right]\right)

\displaystyle=n^{3/2}\sum_{x^{\prime}=1}^{n-1}\mathbb{E}_{n}\left[p_{0}(t)p_{x^{\prime}}(t)\right]\mathbb{E}_{n}\left[\nabla r_{0}(t)p_{x^{\prime}}(t)\right]

\displaystyle+n^{3/2}\sum_{x=1}^{n-1}\left(\mathbb{E}_{n}\left[p_{0}(t)p_{x}(t)\right]\right)\mathbb{E}_{n}\left[\nabla r_{0}(t)p_{x}(t)\right]

\displaystyle+n^{3/2}\sum_{x^{\prime}=1}^{n-1}\mathbb{E}_{n}\left[p_{0}(t)p_{x^{\prime}}(t)\right]\mathbb{E}_{n}\left[p_{0}(t)\nabla r_{x^{\prime}}(t)\right]

\displaystyle+n^{3/2}\sum_{x=1}^{n-1}\left(\mathbb{E}_{n}\left[p_{0}(t)p_{x}(t)\right]\right)\mathbb{E}_{n}\left[p_{0}(t)\nabla r_{x}(t)\right]

\displaystyle+\gamma n^{3/2}\sum_{x^{\prime}=1}^{n-1}\mathbb{E}_{n}\left[p_{0}(t)p_{x^{\prime}}(t)\right]\mathbb{E}_{n}\left[\Delta_{\rm N}p_{0}(t)p_{x^{\prime}}(t)\right]

\displaystyle+\gamma n^{3/2}\sum_{x=1}^{n-1}\left(\mathbb{E}_{n}\left[p_{0}(t)p_{x}(t)\right]\right)\mathbb{E}_{n}\left[\Delta_{\rm N}p_{0}(t)p_{x}(t)\right]

\displaystyle-2\gamma n^{3/2}\mathbb{E}_{n}\left[p_{0}(t)p_{1}(t)\right]\mathbb{E}_{n}\left[\Big(\nabla^{\star}p_{1}(t)\Big)^{2}\right]

\displaystyle-2\tilde{\gamma}n^{3/2}\sum_{x^{\prime}=1}^{n-1}\mathbb{E}_{n}\left[p_{0}(t)p_{x^{\prime}}(t)\right]\mathbb{E}_{n}\left[p_{0}(t)p_{x^{\prime}}(t)\right]

\displaystyle I_{3}(t)=\mathbb{E}_{n}\left[\left(p_{0}(t)\right)^{2}\right]\frac{\mathrm{d}}{\mathrm{d}t}\mathbb{E}_{n}\left[\left(p_{0}(t)\right)^{2}\right]=2n^{3/2}\mathbb{E}_{n}\left[\left(p_{0}(t)\right)^{2}\right]\mathbb{E}_{n}\left[\nabla r_{0}(t)p_{0}(t)\right]

\displaystyle+2\gamma n^{3/2}\mathbb{E}_{n}\left[\left(p_{0}(t)\right)^{2}\right]\mathbb{E}_{n}\left[\Delta_{\rm N}p_{0}(t)p_{0}(t)\right]

\displaystyle-2\tilde{\gamma}n^{3/2}\mathbb{E}_{n}\left[\left(p_{0}(t)\right)^{2}\right]\mathbb{E}_{n}\left[\Big(p_{0}(t)\Big)^{2}\right]+2\tilde{\gamma}n^{3/2}T_{L}\mathbb{E}_{n}\left[\left(p_{0}(t)\right)^{2}\right]

\displaystyle+\gamma n^{3/2}\mathbb{E}_{n}\left[\left(p_{0}(t)\right)^{2}\right]\mathbb{E}_{n}\left[\Big(\nabla^{\star}p_{1}(t)\Big)^{2}\right]

\displaystyle I_{4}(t)=2\mathbb{E}_{n}\left[\left(p_{0}(t)\right)\left(p_{n}(t)\right)\right]\frac{\mathrm{d}}{\mathrm{d}t}\mathbb{E}_{n}\left[\left(p_{0}(t)\right)\left(p_{n}(t)\right)\right]

\displaystyle=2n^{3/2}\mathbb{E}_{n}\left[\left(p_{0}(t)\right)\left(p_{n}(t)\right)\right]\mathbb{E}_{n}\left[\nabla r_{0}(t)p_{n}(t)\right]

\displaystyle+2n^{3/2}\mathbb{E}_{n}\left[\left(p_{0}(t)\right)\left(p_{n}(t)\right)\right]\mathbb{E}_{n}\left[\nabla r_{n}(t)p_{0}(t)\right]

\displaystyle+2\gamma n^{3/2}\mathbb{E}_{n}\left[\left(p_{0}(t)\right)\left(p_{n}(t)\right)\right]\mathbb{E}_{n}\left[\Delta_{\rm N}p_{0}(t)p_{n}(t)\right]

\displaystyle+2\gamma n^{3/2}\mathbb{E}_{n}\left[\left(p_{0}(t)\right)\left(p_{n}(t)\right)\right]\mathbb{E}_{n}\left[\Delta_{\rm N}p_{n}(t)p_{0}(t)\right]

\displaystyle-2\tilde{\gamma}n^{3/2}\mathbb{E}_{n}\left[\left(p_{0}(t)\right)\left(p_{n}(t)\right)\right]\mathbb{E}_{n}\left[p_{0}(t)p_{n}(t)\right]

\displaystyle I_{5}(t)=\sum_{x^{\prime}=1}^{n-1}\left(\mathbb{E}_{n}\left[p_{n}(t)p_{x^{\prime}}(t)\right]\right)\frac{\mathrm{d}}{\mathrm{d}t}\left(\mathbb{E}_{n}\left[p_{n}(t)p_{x^{\prime}}(t)\right]\right)

\displaystyle+\sum_{x=1}^{n-1}\left(\mathbb{E}_{n}\left[p_{n}(t)p_{x}(t)\right]\right)\frac{\mathrm{d}}{\mathrm{d}t}\left(\mathbb{E}_{n}\left[p_{n}(t)p_{x}(t)\right]\right)

\displaystyle=n^{3/2}\sum_{x^{\prime}=1}^{n-1}\mathbb{E}_{n}\left[p_{n}(t)p_{x^{\prime}}(t)\right]\mathbb{E}_{n}\left[\nabla r_{n}(t)p_{x^{\prime}}(t)\right]

\displaystyle+n^{3/2}\sum_{x^{\prime}=1}^{n-1}\mathbb{E}_{n}\left[p_{n}(t)p_{x^{\prime}}(t)\right]\mathbb{E}_{n}\left[\nabla r_{x^{\prime}}(t)p_{n}(t)\right]

\displaystyle+n^{3/2}\sum_{x=1}^{n-1}\mathbb{E}_{n}\left[p_{n}(t)p_{x}(t)\right]\mathbb{E}_{n}\left[\nabla r_{n}(t)p_{x}(t)\right]

\displaystyle+n^{3/2}\sum_{x=1}^{n-1}\mathbb{E}_{n}\left[p_{n}(t)p_{x}(t)\right]\mathbb{E}_{n}\left[\nabla r_{x}(t)p_{n}(t)\right]

\displaystyle+\gamma n^{3/2}\sum_{x^{\prime}=1}^{n-1}\mathbb{E}_{n}\left[p_{n}(t)p_{x^{\prime}}(t)\right]\mathbb{E}_{n}\left[\Delta_{\rm N}p_{n}(t)p_{x^{\prime}}(t)\right]

\displaystyle+\gamma n^{3/2}\sum_{x^{\prime}=1}^{n-1}\mathbb{E}_{n}\left[p_{n}(t)p_{x^{\prime}}(t)\right]\mathbb{E}_{n}\left[\Delta_{\rm N}p_{x^{\prime}}(t)p_{n}(t)\right]

\displaystyle+\gamma n^{3/2}\sum_{x=1}^{n-1}\mathbb{E}_{n}\left[p_{n}(t)p_{x}(t)\right]\mathbb{E}_{n}\left[\Delta_{\rm N}p_{n}(t)p_{x}(t)\right]

\displaystyle+\gamma n^{3/2}\sum_{x=1}^{n-1}\mathbb{E}_{n}\left[p_{n}(t)p_{x}(t)\right]\mathbb{E}_{n}\left[\Delta_{\rm N}p_{x}(t)p_{n}(t)\right]

\displaystyle-\tilde{\gamma}n^{3/2}\sum_{x^{\prime}=1}^{n-1}\mathbb{E}_{n}\left[p_{n}(t)p_{x^{\prime}}(t)\right]\mathbb{E}_{n}\left[p_{n}(t)p_{x^{\prime}}(t)\right]

\displaystyle-\tilde{\gamma}n^{3/2}\sum_{x=1}^{n-1}\mathbb{E}_{n}\left[p_{n}(t)p_{x}(t)\right]\mathbb{E}_{n}\left[p_{n}(t)p_{x}(t)\right]

\displaystyle-2\gamma n^{3/2}\mathbb{E}_{n}\left[p_{n}(t)p_{n-1}(t)\right]\mathbb{E}_{n}\left[\Big(\nabla^{\star}p_{n}(t)\Big)^{2}\right]

\displaystyle I_{6}(t)=\mathbb{E}_{n}\left[\left(p_{n}(t)\right)^{2}\right]\frac{\mathrm{d}}{\mathrm{d}t}\mathbb{E}_{n}\left[\left(p_{n}(t)\right)^{2}\right]=2n^{3/2}\mathbb{E}_{n}\left[\left(p_{n}(t)\right)^{2}\right]\mathbb{E}_{n}\left[\nabla r_{n}(t)p_{n}(t)\right]

\displaystyle+2n^{3/2}\gamma\mathbb{E}_{n}\left[\left(p_{n}(t)\right)^{2}\right]\mathbb{E}_{n}\left[\Delta_{\rm N}p_{n}(t)p_{n}(t)\right]

\displaystyle-2n^{3/2}\tilde{\gamma}\mathbb{E}_{n}\left[\left(p_{n}(t)\right)^{2}\right]\mathbb{E}_{n}\left[\Big(p_{n}(t)\Big)^{2}\right]+2n^{3/2}\tilde{\gamma}T_{R}\mathbb{E}_{n}\left[\left(p_{n}(t)\right)^{2}\right]

\displaystyle+n^{3/2}\gamma\mathbb{E}_{n}\left[\left(p_{n}(t)\right)^{2}\right]\mathbb{E}_{n}\left[\Big(\nabla^{\star}p_{n}(t)\Big)^{2}\right]

Recall that
p_{-1}(t)=p_{0}(t)
and
p_{n+1}(t)=p_{n}(t)
.
By summing up we get

\displaystyle(n+1)I(t)=2n^{3/2}\sum_{x,x^{\prime}=0}^{n}\mathbb{E}_{n}\left[p_{x}(t)p_{x^{\prime}}(t)\right]\mathbb{E}_{n}\left[\nabla r_{x}(t)p_{x^{\prime}}(t)\right]

\displaystyle+\gamma n^{3/2}\sum_{x,x^{\prime}=0}^{n}\mathbb{E}_{n}\left[p_{x}(t)p_{x^{\prime}}(t)\right]\mathbb{E}_{n}\left[\Delta_{\rm N}p_{x}(t)p_{x^{\prime}}(t)\right]

\displaystyle+\gamma n^{3/2}\sum_{x,x^{\prime}=0}^{n}\mathbb{E}_{n}\left[p_{x}(t)p_{x^{\prime}}(t)\right]\mathbb{E}_{n}\left[\Delta_{\rm N}p_{x^{\prime}}(t)p_{x}(t)\right]

\displaystyle+\gamma n^{3/2}\sum_{x=0}^{n}\mathbb{E}_{n}\left[\left(p_{x}(t)\right)^{2}\right]\mathbb{E}_{n}\Big[\big(\nabla^{\star}p_{x+1}(t)\big)^{2}+\big(\nabla^{\star}p_{x}(t)\big)^{2}\Big]

\displaystyle-2\gamma n^{3/2}\sum_{x=0}^{n-1}\mathbb{E}_{n}\left[\left(p_{x}(t)\right)\left(p_{x+1}(t)\right)\right]\mathbb{E}_{n}\left[\big(\nabla^{\star}p_{x+1}(t)\big)^{2}\right]

\displaystyle+2\tilde{\gamma}n^{3/2}\Big(T_{L}-\mathbb{E}_{n}\left[\left(p_{0}(t)\right)^{2}\right]\Big)\mathbb{E}_{n}\left[\left(p_{0}(t)\right)^{2}\right]

\displaystyle+2n^{3/2}\tilde{\gamma}\Big(T_{R}-\mathbb{E}_{n}\left[\left(p_{n}(t)\right)^{2}\right]\Big)\mathbb{E}_{n}\left[\left(p_{n}(t)\right)^{2}\right]

\displaystyle-2n^{3/2}\tilde{\gamma}\sum_{x=1}^{n}\left(\mathbb{E}_{n}\left[p_{0}(t)p_{x}(t)\right]\right)^{2}-2\tilde{\gamma}n^{3/2}\sum_{x=0}^{n-1}\left(\mathbb{E}_{n}\left[p_{n}(t)p_{x}(t)\right]\right)^{2}

\displaystyle=2n^{3/2}\sum_{x,x^{\prime}=0}^{n}\mathbb{E}_{n}\left[p_{x}(t)p_{x^{\prime}}(t)\right]\mathbb{E}_{n}\left[\nabla r_{x}(t)p_{x^{\prime}}(t)\right]

\displaystyle-2\gamma n^{3/2}\sum_{x=1}^{n}\sum_{x^{\prime}=0}^{n}\left(\mathbb{E}_{n}\left[\nabla^{\star}p_{x}(t)p_{x^{\prime}}(t)\right]\right)^{2}

\displaystyle+\gamma n^{3/2}\sum_{x=0}^{n-1}\mathbb{E}_{n}\left[\left(p_{x}(t)\right)^{2}\right]\mathbb{E}_{n}\Big[\big(\nabla^{\star}p_{x+1}(t)\big)^{2}\Big]

\displaystyle+\gamma n^{3/2}\sum_{x=0}^{n-1}\mathbb{E}_{n}\left[\left(p_{x+1}(t)\right)^{2}\right]\mathbb{E}_{n}\Big[\big(\nabla^{\star}p_{x+1}(t)\big)^{2}\Big]

\displaystyle-2\gamma n^{3/2}\sum_{x=0}^{n-1}\mathbb{E}_{n}\left[\left(p_{x}(t)\right)\left(p_{x+1}(t)\right)\right]\mathbb{E}_{n}\left[\big(\nabla^{\star}p_{x+1}(t)\big)^{2}\right]

\displaystyle+2\tilde{\gamma}n^{3/2}\Big(T_{L}-\mathbb{E}_{n}\left[\left(p_{0}(t)\right)^{2}\right]\Big)\mathbb{E}_{n}\left[\left(p_{0}(t)\right)^{2}\right]

\displaystyle+2n^{3/2}\tilde{\gamma}\Big(T_{R}-\mathbb{E}_{n}\left[\left(p_{n}(t)\right)^{2}\right]\Big)\mathbb{E}_{n}\left[\left(p_{n}(t)\right)^{2}\right]

\displaystyle-2n^{3/2}\tilde{\gamma}\sum_{x=1}^{n}\left(\mathbb{E}_{n}\left[p_{0}(t)p_{x}(t)\right]\right)^{2}-2\tilde{\gamma}n^{3/2}\sum_{x=0}^{n-1}\left(\mathbb{E}_{n}\left[p_{n}(t)p_{x}(t)\right]\right)^{2}

\displaystyle=2n^{3/2}\sum_{x,x^{\prime}=0}^{n}\mathbb{E}_{n}\left[p_{x}(t)p_{x^{\prime}}(t)\right]\mathbb{E}_{n}\left[\nabla r_{x}(t)p_{x^{\prime}}(t)\right]

\displaystyle-2\gamma n^{3/2}\sum_{x=1}^{n}\sum_{x^{\prime}=0}^{n}\left(\mathbb{E}_{n}\left[\nabla^{\star}p_{x}(t)p_{x^{\prime}}(t)\right]\right)^{2}+\gamma n^{3/2}\sum_{x=0}^{n-1}\left(\mathbb{E}_{n}\Big[\big(\nabla^{\star}p_{x+1}(t)\big)^{2}\Big]\right)^{2}

\displaystyle+2\tilde{\gamma}n^{3/2}\Big(T_{L}-\mathbb{E}_{n}\left[\left(p_{0}(t)\right)^{2}\right]\Big)\mathbb{E}_{n}\left[\left(p_{0}(t)\right)^{2}\right]

\displaystyle+2n^{3/2}\tilde{\gamma}\Big(T_{R}-\mathbb{E}_{n}\left[\left(p_{n}(t)\right)^{2}\right]\Big)\mathbb{E}_{n}\left[\left(p_{n}(t)\right)^{2}\right]

\displaystyle-2n^{3/2}\tilde{\gamma}\sum_{x=1}^{n}\left(\mathbb{E}_{n}\left[p_{0}(t)p_{x}(t)\right]\right)^{2}-2\tilde{\gamma}n^{3/2}\sum_{x=0}^{n-1}\left(\mathbb{E}_{n}\left[p_{n}(t)p_{x}(t)\right]\right)^{2}

\displaystyle=n^{3/2}\sum_{x,x^{\prime}=0}^{n}\mathbb{E}_{n}\left[p_{x}(t)p_{x^{\prime}}(t)\right]\mathbb{E}_{n}\left[\nabla r_{x}(t)p_{x^{\prime}}(t)\right]

\displaystyle+n^{3/2}\sum_{x,x^{\prime}=0}^{n-1}\mathbb{E}_{n}\left[p_{x}(t)p_{x^{\prime}}(t)\right]\mathbb{E}_{n}\left[\nabla r_{x^{\prime}}(t)p_{x}(t)\right]

\displaystyle-2\gamma n^{3/2}\sum_{x=1}^{n}\mathop{\sum_{x^{\prime}=0}^{n}}_{x^{\prime}\not\in\{x-1,x\}}\left(\mathbb{E}_{n}\left[\nabla^{\star}p_{x}(t)p_{x^{\prime}}(t)\right]\right)^{2}+P_{n}(t)

\displaystyle+2\tilde{\gamma}n^{3/2}\Big(T_{L}-\mathbb{E}_{n}\left[\left(p_{0}(t)\right)^{2}\right]\Big)\mathbb{E}_{n}\left[\left(p_{0}(t)\right)^{2}\right]

\displaystyle+2n^{3/2}\tilde{\gamma}\Big(T_{R}-\mathbb{E}_{n}\left[\left(p_{n}(t)\right)^{2}\right]\Big)\mathbb{E}_{n}\left[\left(p_{n}(t)\right)^{2}\right]

\displaystyle-2n^{3/2}\tilde{\gamma}\sum_{x=1}^{n}\left(\mathbb{E}_{n}\left[p_{0}(t)p_{x}(t)\right]\right)^{2}-2\tilde{\gamma}n^{3/2}\sum_{x=0}^{n-1}\left(\mathbb{E}_{n}\left[p_{n}(t)p_{x}(t)\right]\right)^{2},

with

\displaystyle P_{n}(t):=-2\gamma n^{3/2}\sum_{x=1}^{n}\left(\mathbb{E}_{n}\left[\nabla^{\star}p_{x}(t)p_{x}(t)\right]\right)^{2}-2\gamma n^{3/2}\sum_{x=1}^{n}\left(\mathbb{E}_{n}\left[\nabla^{\star}p_{x}(t)p_{x-1}(t)\right]\right)^{2}

\displaystyle+\gamma n^{3/2}\sum_{x=0}^{n-1}\left(\mathbb{E}_{n}\Big[\big(\nabla^{\star}p_{x+1}(t)\big)^{2}\Big]\right)^{2}

\displaystyle=-2\gamma n^{3/2}\sum_{x=1}^{n}\left(\mathbb{E}_{n}\left[(p_{x})^{2}(t)\right]-\mathbb{E}_{n}\left[p_{x-1}(t)p_{x}(t)\right]\right)^{2}

\displaystyle-2\gamma n^{3/2}\sum_{x=1}^{n}\left(\mathbb{E}_{n}\left[(p_{x-1})^{2}(t)\right]-\mathbb{E}_{n}\left[p_{x-1}(t)p_{x}(t)\right]\right)^{2}

\displaystyle+\gamma n^{3/2}\sum_{x=0}^{n-1}\left(\mathbb{E}_{n}\Big[\big(p_{x+1}(t)-p_{x}(t)\big)^{2}\Big]\right)^{2}

\displaystyle=-2\gamma n^{3/2}\sum_{x=1}^{n}\left(\mathbb{E}_{n}\left[(p_{x})^{2}(t)\right]-\mathbb{E}_{n}\left[p_{x-1}(t)p_{x}(t)\right]\right)^{2}

\displaystyle-2\gamma n^{3/2}\sum_{x=1}^{n}\left(\mathbb{E}_{n}\left[(p_{x-1})^{2}(t)\right]-\mathbb{E}_{n}\left[p_{x-1}(t)p_{x}(t)\right]\right)^{2}

\displaystyle+\gamma n^{3/2}\sum_{x=0}^{n-1}\left(\mathbb{E}_{n}\Big[(p_{x+1})^{2}(t)+(p_{x})^{2}(t)-2p_{x+1}^{\prime}(t)p_{x}(t)\Big]\right)^{2}

\displaystyle=-2\gamma n^{3/2}\sum_{x=1}^{n}\left\{\left(\mathbb{E}_{n}\left[(p_{x})^{2}(t)\right]\right)^{2}+\left(\mathbb{E}_{n}\left[p_{x-1}(t)p_{x}(t)\right]\right)^{2}-2\mathbb{E}_{n}\left[(p_{x})^{2}(t)\right]\mathbb{E}_{n}\left[p_{x-1}(t)p_{x}(t)\right]\right\}

\displaystyle-2\gamma n^{3/2}\sum_{x=1}^{n}\left\{\left(\mathbb{E}_{n}\left[(p_{x-1})^{2}(t)\right]\right)^{2}+\left(\mathbb{E}_{n}\left[p_{x-1}(t)p_{x}(t)\right]\right)^{2}-2\mathbb{E}_{n}\left[(p_{x-1})^{2}(t)\right]\mathbb{E}_{n}\left[p_{x-1}(t)p_{x}(t)\right]\right\}

\displaystyle+\gamma n^{3/2}\sum_{x=0}^{n-1}\Big\{\left(\mathbb{E}_{n}\Big[(p_{x+1})^{2}(t)\Big]\right)^{2}+\left(\mathbb{E}_{n}\Big[(p_{x})^{2}(t)\Big]\right)^{2}+4\left(\mathbb{E}_{n}\Big[p_{x+1}(t)p_{x}(t)\Big]\right)^{2}

\displaystyle+2\mathbb{E}_{n}\Big[\tilde{p}^{2}_{x+1}(t)\Big]\mathbb{E}_{n}\Big[\tilde{p}^{2}_{x}(t)\Big]-4\mathbb{E}_{n}\Big[\tilde{p}^{2}_{x+1}(t)\Big]\mathbb{E}_{n}\Big[\tilde{p}_{x+1}(t)\tilde{p}_{x}(t)\Big]-4\mathbb{E}_{n}\Big[\tilde{p}^{2}_{x}(t)\Big]\mathbb{E}_{n}\Big[\tilde{p}_{x+1}(t)\tilde{p}_{x}(t)\Big]\Big\}

\displaystyle=-\gamma n^{3/2}\sum_{x=0}^{n-1}\left\{\cancel{2}\left(\mathbb{E}_{n}\left[(p_{x+1})^{2}(t)\right]\right)^{2}+\cancel{2\left(\mathbb{E}_{n}\left[p_{x}(t)p_{x+1}(t)\right]\right)^{2}}-\cancel{4\mathbb{E}_{n}\left[(p_{x+1})^{2}(t)\right]\mathbb{E}_{n}\left[p_{x}(t)p_{x+1}(t)\right]}\right\}

\displaystyle-\gamma n^{3/2}\sum_{x=0}^{n-1}\left\{\cancel{2}\left(\mathbb{E}_{n}\left[(p_{x})^{2}(t)\right]\right)^{2}+\cancel{2\left(\mathbb{E}_{n}\left[p_{x}(t)p_{x+1}(t)\right]\right)^{2}}-\cancel{4\mathbb{E}_{n}\left[(p_{x})^{2}(t)\right]\mathbb{E}_{n}\left[p_{x}(t)p_{x+1}(t)\right]}\right\}

\displaystyle+\gamma n^{3/2}\sum_{x=0}^{n-1}\Big\{\cancel{\left(\mathbb{E}_{n}\Big[\tilde{p}^{2}_{x+1}(t)\Big]\right)^{2}}+\cancel{\left(\mathbb{E}_{n}\Big[\tilde{p}^{2}_{x}(t)\Big]\right)^{2}}+\cancel{4\left(\mathbb{E}_{n}\Big[\tilde{p}_{x+1}(t)\tilde{p}_{x}(t)\Big]\right)^{2}}

\displaystyle+2\mathbb{E}_{n}\Big[\tilde{p}^{2}_{x+1}(t)\Big]\mathbb{E}_{n}\Big[\tilde{p}^{2}_{x}(t)\Big]-\cancel{4\mathbb{E}_{n}\Big[\tilde{p}^{2}_{x+1}(t)\Big]\mathbb{E}_{n}\Big[\tilde{p}_{x+1}(t)\tilde{p}_{x}(t)\Big]}-\cancel{4\mathbb{E}_{n}\Big[\tilde{p}^{2}_{x}(t)\Big]\mathbb{E}_{n}\Big[\tilde{p}_{x+1}(t)\tilde{p}_{x}(t)\Big]}\Big\}

\displaystyle=-\gamma n^{3/2}\sum_{x=0}^{n-1}\Big[\nabla\left(\mathbb{E}_{n}\left[(p_{x})^{2}(t)\right]\right)\Big]^{2}

Summarizing, we have shown that

\displaystyle I(t)=\frac{2n^{3/2}}{n+1}\sum_{x,x^{\prime}=0}^{n}\mathbb{E}_{n}\left[p_{x}(t)p_{x^{\prime}}(t)\right]\mathbb{E}_{n}\left[\nabla r_{x}(t)p_{x^{\prime}}(t)\right]

\displaystyle-2\gamma\frac{n^{3/2}}{n+1}\sum_{x=1}^{n}\mathop{\sum_{x^{\prime}=0}^{n}}_{x^{\prime}\not\in\{x-1,x\}}\left(\mathbb{E}_{n}\left[\nabla^{\star}p_{x}(t)p_{x^{\prime}}(t)\right]\right)^{2}-\gamma\frac{n^{3/2}}{n+1}\sum_{x=0}^{n-1}\Big[\nabla\left(\mathbb{E}_{n}\left[(p_{x})^{2}(t)\right]\right)^{2}\Big]^{2}

\displaystyle+2\tilde{\gamma}\frac{n^{3/2}}{n+1}\Big(T_{L}-\mathbb{E}_{n}\left[\left(p_{0}(t)\right)^{2}\right]\Big)\mathbb{E}_{n}\left[\left(p_{0}(t)\right)^{2}\right]

\displaystyle+2\frac{n^{3/2}}{n+1}\tilde{\gamma}\Big(T_{R}-\mathbb{E}_{n}\left[\left(p_{n}(t)\right)^{2}\right]\Big)\mathbb{E}_{n}\left[\left(p_{n}(t)\right)^{2}\right]

\displaystyle-2\frac{n^{3/2}}{n+1}\tilde{\gamma}\sum_{x=1}^{n}\left(\mathbb{E}_{n}\left[p_{0}(t)p_{x}(t)\right]\right)^{2}-2\tilde{\gamma}\frac{n^{3/2}}{n+1}\sum_{x=0}^{n-1}\left(\mathbb{E}_{n}\left[p_{n}(t)p_{x}(t)\right]\right)^{2},

Calculation for the
r-r
covariance

We have

\displaystyle{\rm II}(t)=\frac{1}{n+1}\sum_{x,x^{\prime}=1}^{n}\left(\mathbb{E}_{n}\left[r_{x}(t)r_{x^{\prime}}(t)\right]\right)\frac{\mathrm{d}}{\mathrm{d}t}\left(\mathbb{E}_{n}\left[r_{x}(t)r_{x^{\prime}}(t)\right]\right)

\displaystyle=\frac{n^{3/2}}{n+1}\sum_{x,x^{\prime}=1}^{n}\mathbb{E}_{n}\left[r_{x}(t)r_{x^{\prime}}(t)\right]\mathbb{E}_{n}\left[\nabla^{\star}p_{x}(t)r_{x^{\prime}}(t)\right]

\displaystyle=\frac{2n^{3/2}}{n+1}\sum_{x,x^{\prime}=1}^{n}\mathbb{E}_{n}\left[r_{x}(t)r_{x^{\prime}}(t)\right]\mathbb{E}_{n}\left[\nabla^{\star}p_{x}(t)r_{x^{\prime}}(t)\right]

Calculation for the
r-p
covariance

We have

\displaystyle{\rm III}(t)=\frac{2}{n+1}\sum_{x=0}^{n}\sum_{x^{\prime}=1}^{n}\left(\mathbb{E}_{n}\left[p_{x}(t)r_{x^{\prime}}(t)\right]\right)\frac{\mathrm{d}}{\mathrm{d}t}\left(\mathbb{E}_{n}\left[p_{x}(t)r_{x^{\prime}}(t)\right]\right)=\frac{2}{n+1}\sum_{j=1}^{3}{\rm III}_{j}(t),

with

\displaystyle{\rm III}_{1}(t)=n^{3/2}\sum_{x=1}^{n-1}\sum_{x^{\prime}=1}^{n}\mathbb{E}_{n}\left[p_{x}(t)r_{x^{\prime}}(t)\right]\mathbb{E}_{n}\left[\nabla r_{x}r_{x^{\prime}}(t)\right]

\displaystyle+\gamma n^{3/2}\sum_{x=1}^{n-1}\sum_{x^{\prime}=1}^{n}\mathbb{E}_{n}\left[p_{x}(t)r_{x^{\prime}}(t)\right]\mathbb{E}_{n}\left[\Delta_{\rm N}p_{x}(t)r_{x^{\prime}}(t)\right]

\displaystyle+n^{3/2}\sum_{x=1}^{n-1}\sum_{x^{\prime}=1}^{n}\mathbb{E}_{n}\left[p_{x}(t)r_{x^{\prime}}(t)\right]\mathbb{E}_{n}\left[p_{x}(t)\nabla^{\star}p_{x^{\prime}}(t)\right],

\displaystyle{\rm III}_{2}(t)=n^{3/2}\sum_{x^{\prime}=1}^{n}\mathbb{E}_{n}\left[p_{0}(t)r_{x^{\prime}}(t)\right]\mathbb{E}_{n}\left[\nabla r_{0}r_{x^{\prime}}(t)\right]

\displaystyle+\gamma n^{3/2}\sum_{x^{\prime}=1}^{n}\mathbb{E}_{n}\left[p_{0}(t)r_{x^{\prime}}(t)\right]\mathbb{E}_{n}\left[\Delta_{\rm N}p_{0}(t)r_{x^{\prime}}(t)\right]

\displaystyle+n^{3/2}\sum_{x^{\prime}=1}^{n}\mathbb{E}_{n}\left[p_{0}(t)r_{x^{\prime}}(t)\right]\mathbb{E}_{n}\left[p_{0}(t)\nabla^{\star}p_{x^{\prime}}(t)\right]

\displaystyle-\tilde{\gamma}n^{3/2}\sum_{x^{\prime}=1}^{n}\mathbb{E}_{n}\left[p_{0}(t)r_{x^{\prime}}(t)\right]\mathbb{E}_{n}\left[p_{0}(t)r_{x^{\prime}}(t)\right]

\displaystyle{\rm III}_{3}(t)=n^{3/2}\sum_{x^{\prime}=1}^{n}\mathbb{E}_{n}\left[p_{n}(t)r_{x^{\prime}}(t)\right]\mathbb{E}_{n}\left[\nabla r_{n}r_{x^{\prime}}(t)\right]

\displaystyle+\gamma n^{3/2}\sum_{x^{\prime}=1}^{n}\mathbb{E}_{n}\left[p_{n}(t)r_{x^{\prime}}(t)\right]\mathbb{E}_{n}\left[\Delta_{\rm N}p_{n}(t)r_{x^{\prime}}(t)\right]

\displaystyle+n^{3/2}\sum_{x^{\prime}=1}^{n}\mathbb{E}_{n}\left[p_{n}(t)r_{x^{\prime}}(t)\right]\mathbb{E}_{n}\left[p_{n}(t)\nabla^{\star}p_{x^{\prime}}(t)\right]

\displaystyle-\tilde{\gamma}n^{3/2}\sum_{x^{\prime}=1}^{n}\mathbb{E}_{n}\left[p_{n}(t)r_{x^{\prime}}(t)\right]\mathbb{E}_{n}\left[p_{n}(t)r_{x^{\prime}}(t)\right]

Hence

\displaystyle{\rm III}(t)=\frac{2n^{3/2}}{n+1}\sum_{x=0}^{n}\sum_{x^{\prime}=1}^{n}\mathbb{E}_{n}\left[p_{x}(t)r_{x^{\prime}}(t)\right]\mathbb{E}_{n}\left[\nabla r_{x}(t)r_{x^{\prime}}(t)\right]

\displaystyle+\gamma\frac{2n^{3/2}}{n+1}\sum_{x=0}^{n}\sum_{x^{\prime}=1}^{n}\mathbb{E}_{n}\left[p_{x}(t)r_{x^{\prime}}(t)\right]\mathbb{E}_{n}\left[\Delta_{\rm N}p_{x}(t)r_{x^{\prime}}(t)\right]

\displaystyle+\frac{2n^{3/2}}{n+1}\sum_{x=0}^{n}\sum_{x^{\prime}=1}^{n}\mathbb{E}_{n}\left[p_{x}(t)r_{x^{\prime}}(t)\right]\mathbb{E}_{n}\left[p_{x}(t)\nabla^{\star}p_{x^{\prime}}(t)\right]

\displaystyle-\tilde{\gamma}\frac{2n^{3/2}}{n+1}\sum_{x^{\prime}=1}^{n}\mathbb{E}_{n}\left[p_{0}(t)r_{x^{\prime}}(t)\right]\mathbb{E}_{n}\left[p_{0}(t)r_{x^{\prime}}(t)\right]

\displaystyle-\tilde{\gamma}\frac{2n^{3/2}}{n+1}\sum_{x^{\prime}=1}^{n}\mathbb{E}_{n}\left[p_{n}(t)r_{x^{\prime}}(t)\right]\mathbb{E}_{n}\left[p_{n}(t)r_{x^{\prime}}(t)\right]

\displaystyle=-\frac{2n^{3/2}}{n+1}\sum_{x=1}^{n}\sum_{x^{\prime}=1}^{n}\mathbb{E}_{n}\left[\nabla^{\star}p_{x}(t)r_{x^{\prime}}(t)\right]\mathbb{E}_{n}\left[r_{x}(t)r_{x^{\prime}}(t)\right]

\displaystyle+\gamma\frac{2n^{3/2}}{n+1}\sum_{x=0}^{n}\sum_{x^{\prime}=1}^{n}\mathbb{E}_{n}\left[p_{x}(t)r_{x^{\prime}}(t)\right]\mathbb{E}_{n}\left[\Delta_{\rm N}p_{x}(t)r_{x^{\prime}}(t)\right]

\displaystyle+\frac{2n^{3/2}}{n+1}\sum_{x=0}^{n}\sum_{x^{\prime}=1}^{n}\mathbb{E}_{n}\left[p_{x}(t)r_{x^{\prime}}(t)\right]\mathbb{E}_{n}\left[p_{x}(t)\nabla^{\star}p_{x^{\prime}}(t)\right]

\displaystyle-\tilde{\gamma}\frac{2n^{3/2}}{n+1}\sum_{x^{\prime}=1}^{n}\mathbb{E}_{n}\left[p_{0}(t)r_{x^{\prime}}(t)\right]\mathbb{E}_{n}\left[p_{0}(t)r_{x^{\prime}}(t)\right]

\displaystyle-\tilde{\gamma}\frac{2n^{3/2}}{n+1}\sum_{x^{\prime}=1}^{n}\mathbb{E}_{n}\left[p_{n}(t)r_{x^{\prime}}(t)\right]\mathbb{E}_{n}\left[p_{n}(t)r_{x^{\prime}}(t)\right]

The equation for

\displaystyle\frac{\mathrm{d}}{\mathrm{d}t}{\cal H}_{n}^{(2)}(t)=\cancel{\frac{2n^{3/2}}{n+1}\sum_{x,x^{\prime}=0}^{n}\mathbb{E}_{n}\left[p_{x}(t)p_{x^{\prime}}(t)\right]\mathbb{E}_{n}\left[\nabla r_{x}(t)p_{x^{\prime}}(t)\right]}
